### F-019 [Medium / CONFIRMED] Whole-project pyright regressions never fail CI

- **Area / Dimension.** CI-OPS
- **Affected files.** `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `pyright-safety.json`
- **Line ranges.** .github/workflows/ci.yml:53-68, 82-89; pyright-safety.json:2-26
- **Observed behavior.**
  The `type-check` job runs `pyright` on the whole project with `continue-on-error: true` (ci.yml:60-61, and again for paper_runtime at ci.yml:67-68). The narrower `type-check-safety` job (ci.yml:88-89) blocks on only the 25 files listed in `pyright-safety.json:2-26`. Files critical to safety but not in that include list (e.g., `src/trading_research/paper_books/external_broker.py`, the $50/day-notional external-paper coordinator; `src/trading_research/execution/live_gateway.py`; `src/trading_research/services/submit_credentialed_paper_order.py`; every risk gate in `paper_books/risk.py`, `daily_risk.py`, `safety_pause.py`) are only exercised by the non-blocking whole-project pyright, whose failures never fail CI.
- **Failure scenario.**
  A refactor introduces a mistyped-None deref or wrong return type in `paper_books/external_broker.py::_reserve_daily_notional` (which the design digest identifies as safety-critical for the $150/UTC-day cap). Pyright flags it in the non-blocking whole-project job; because `continue-on-error: true` sets the outcome to success regardless of exit code, the merge proceeds. Only at runtime does the type error surface.
- **Impact.** The pyright signal that the workflow comment (ci.yml:53-58) claims will 'surface the count so regressions are visible' is not actionable — no branch protection can key off a `continue-on-error` job's success, and Milestone 12.1 Item 10's blocking gate only covers a hand-picked subset.
- **Why existing tests do not prevent it.** Pytest cannot catch missing-None or wrong-shape defects that only manifest on rare code paths (e.g., ambiguous-outcome branch); these are precisely what a blocking pyright would catch.
- **Recommended correction.** Either widen `pyright-safety.json` include list to cover all authoritative safety boundaries (paper_books/external_broker.py, paper_books/risk.py, execution/*, services/submit_credentialed_paper_order.py), or add a separate blocking pyright job that fails on new errors above a checked-in baseline count.
- **Recommended regression tests.** Add a CI step that greps pyright output for `error:` count and compares to a baseline file (fail-if-greater).
- **Capability blocked.** Static safety-boundary regression detection
- **Finder confidence.** high
- **Verification.** 3 votes (3 confirmed, 0 plausible, 0 refuted).

The blocking `type-check-safety` job (lines 88-89) has no `continue-on
  - _severity_: verdict=CONFIRMED, refuted=False - Verified against the actual files. `.github/workflows/ci.yml:59-61` runs whole-project `pyright` with `continue-on-error: true`, and `:65-68` does the same for paper_runtime pyright — so both jobs report success regardless of exit code and cannot be enforced via branch protection. The blocking job at `.github/workflows/ci.yml:70-89` runs `pyright --project pyright-safety.json` without `continue-on

### F-020 [Medium / CONFIRMED] No lint, dependency-audit, or security scanning in CI

- **Area / Dimension.** CI-OPS
- **Affected files.** `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `pyproject.toml`
- **Line ranges.** .github/workflows/ci.yml:1-165; pyproject.toml:10-37
- **Observed behavior.**
  The workflow contains only pytest + pyright + a tiny schema smoke. There is no ruff/flake8/black, no bandit, no pip-audit, no `safety`, no `pip check`, no lockfile enforcement. The project pulls `anthropic>=0.116.0`, `lumibot==4.5.74`, `httpx>=0.28.0`, `pandas>=2.2.0`, `streamlit>=1.36.0`, etc. (pyproject.toml:10-20) — none of these are scanned for known CVEs on any cadence in-repo.
- **Failure scenario.**
  A CVE in `anthropic`, `httpx`, `pandas`, or `streamlit` (the dashboard opens on 127.0.0.1 via scripts/run_dashboard.sh, and Tailscale-serve exposes it via scripts/tailscale_serve_dashboard.sh) surfaces after release; nothing in CI notices. Same for a supply-chain compromise of an unpinned transitive.
- **Impact.** Given the codebase handles broker paper credentials via `paper_runtime` (alpaca-py) and Anthropic API keys, silent CVE exposure is realistic. Also, style/lint drift accumulates and hides genuine bugs.
- **Why existing tests do not prevent it.** Unit tests do not exercise dependency vulnerabilities or lint conformance.
- **Recommended correction.** Add a lightweight lint job (ruff), a `pip-audit` (or `safety check`) job, and consider a `bandit -r src/` job. Optionally freeze a `constraints.txt` for reproducibility.
- **Recommended regression tests.** N/A — these are new CI checks, not test regressions.
- **Capability blocked.** Supply-chain and static-analysis assurance
- **Finder confidence.** high
- **Verification.** 3 votes (3 confirmed, 0 plausible, 0 refuted).

No constraints.txt, requirements*.txt, or .pre-commit-config.yaml exists. Howeve

### F-021 [Medium / CONFIRMED] Third-party GitHub Actions pinned by mutable major tag, not SHA

- **Area / Dimension.** CI-OPS
- **Affected files.** `.github/workflows/ci.yml`
- **Line ranges.** .github/workflows/ci.yml:17-18, 30-31, 45-46, 74-75, 95-96
- **Observed behavior.**
  Every action reference is `actions/checkout@v4` and `actions/setup-python@v5`. Neither is pinned to an immutable commit SHA. Anyone with push access to the action's `v4`/`v5` tag namespace (or a repo takeover) can silently swap what runs in this workflow.
- **Failure scenario.**
  A malicious force-push (or a compromised owner) rewrites the `v4` tag on `actions/checkout` to a build that exfiltrates the workspace. Because this workflow runs on `pull_request` events (ci.yml:10) — including PRs from forks — the compromised action executes on every PR checkout with the workspace mounted. Any secrets a future PR wires into GH environment variables would be leaked.
- **Impact.** Supply-chain risk elevated by the fact that Milestone 4/5/11 real-broker/Claude smoke tests are gated on env vars (RUN_EXTERNAL_PAPER_BROKER_TESTS, RUN_CLAUDE_RESEARCH_TESTS, etc.) — the moment someone adds those secrets to CI, an untrusted actions@v5 could exfiltrate them.
- **Why existing tests do not prevent it.** Not a code test surface; workflow supply chain is invisible to pytest.
- **Recommended correction.** Pin to full-length commit SHAs, e.g. `actions/checkout@<40-char sha> # v4.1.x`. Add a Dependabot config for `github-actions` ecosystem so SHAs are updated deliberately.
- **Recommended regression tests.** N/A.
- **Capability blocked.** Reproducible / tamper-resistant CI
- **Finder confidence.** high
- **Verification.** 3 votes (3 confirmed, 0 plausible, 0 refuted).
  - _severity_: verdict=CONFIRMED, refuted=False - Confirmed: every actions/checkout and actions/setup-python reference in .github/workflows/ci.yml (lines 17-18, 30-31, 45-46, 74-75, 95-96) is pinned to a mutable major tag (v4/v5) rather than a full commit SHA, and no .github/dependabot.yml exists to update pins deliberately. Workflow runs on pull_request (line 10) without fork restrictions, so a compromised tag would execute on every PR.\n\nHowev
  - _code-refute_: verdict=CONFIRMED, refuted=False - Re-reading .github/workflows/ci.yml confirms all cited lines verbatim: - ci.yml:17 `- uses: actions/checkout@v4` - ci.yml:18 `- uses: actions/setup-python@v5` - ci.yml:30-31 same pair - ci.yml:45-46 same pair - ci.yml:74-75 same pair - ci.yml:95-96 same pair  Every third-party action reference in the workflow uses a mutable major-version tag (`@v4`, `@v5`) rather than a full 40-character commit SH
  - _reachability_: verdict=CONFIRMED, refuted=False - Verified against .github/workflows/ci.yml (the only workflow file in the repo). All five cited references are literally present and pinned to mutable major tags: - ci.yml:17 `uses: actions/checkout@v4` - ci.yml:18 `uses: actions/setup-python@v5` - ci.yml:30-31 same pair - ci.yml:45-46 same pair - ci.yml:74-75 same pair - ci.yml:95-96 same pair  Reachability analysis:  1. ### F-022 [Medium / CONFIRMED] sync_paper_orders `_sync_one` writes save_event/mark_event_ledger_applied/update_submission_status in three separate autocommit transactions with no bracketing

- **Area / Dimension.** CONCURRENCY-TX
- **Affected workflow.** Milestone 3/4 credentialed paper-broker poll / fill ingestion
- **Affected files.** `src/trading_research/services/sync_paper_orders.py`, `src/trading_research/execution/ledger_events.py`, `src/trading_research/storage/execution_repositories.py`
- **Line ranges.** src/trading_research/services/sync_paper_orders.py:66-104; src/trading_research/execution/ledger_events.py:42-69; src/trading_research/storage/execution_repositories.py:130-156, 334-361
- **Observed behavior.**
  `sync_paper_orders._sync_one` fetches broker order (line 68), calls `apply_all_new_events(conn, ledger, (event,), now=now)` which enters `apply_paper_execution_event` (ledger_events.py:26-69). Inside that: `exec_repo.save_event` INSERTs and commits (execution_repositories.py:148); `ledger.apply_external_fill` mutates ledger tables in its own writes; `mark_event_ledger_applied` UPDATEs and commits (execution_repositories.py:152-156). Only after those three separate autocommits does `_sync_one` call `update_submission_status` (line 96-99), which is yet another autocommit. Neither `_sync_one` nor `apply_paper_execution_event` opens a `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`. Additionally `save_event` (execution_repositories.py:130-149) is a plain INSERT preceded by an `event_exists` check — not `INSERT OR IGNORE` — so two concurrent CLI invocations of `sync-paper-orders` that both see the same broker order state can both compute the identical deterministic `event_id = f"{intent_id}-sync-{event_type}-{cumulative_filled}"` (sync_paper_orders.py:118), both see `event_exists=False`, and one will raise `sqlite3.IntegrityError` on the PRIMARY KEY (paper_execution_events.event_id).
- **Failure scenario.**
  Operator schedules the `sync-paper-orders` CLI twice (or a cron overlap runs it twice). Both fetch the same non-terminal submission and see `filled_quantity=10, status=PARTIALLY_FILLED`. Both compute `event_id = 'intent42-sync-PARTIALLY_FILLED-10'`, both find `event_exists=False`, both call `save_event`. Second insert raises sqlite3.IntegrityError and aborts that sync — but the first has already committed the event and the ledger fill, and the second's `update_submission_status` never runs. The submission row status may lag the ledger by one poll cycle; worse, the crashing invocation returns an unhandled traceback rather than a bounded `SyncOutcome`.
- **Impact.** A concurrent second poller crashes with an unhandled sqlite3.IntegrityError; even a single-poller crash between `save_event`+ledger apply and `update_submission_status` leaves the ledger correctly applied but the poll marker not advanced (self-healing next poll, but no atomicity guarantee).
- **Recommended correction.** Bracket the whole `_sync_one` write path (save_event → apply_external_fill → mark_event_ledger_applied → update_submission_status) inside a single `transaction(conn)` with `commit=False` variants, or convert `save_event` to `INSERT OR IGNORE` and guard against the race by returning False when the row already exists. Add a lease around `sync_paper_orders` similar to the paper_books external order-scope lease.
- **Finder confidence.** high
- **Verification.** 3 votes (2 confirmed, 1 plausible, 0 refuted).
  - _reachability_: verdict=PLAUSIBLE, refuted=False - The mechanics of the finding are correct as claimed against the code:  1. sync_paper_orders._sync_one (services/sync_paper_orders.py:66-104) opens no transaction — it calls save_event (execution_repositories.py:148 commit), then ledger.apply_external_fill, then mark_event_ledger_applied (line 156 commit), then update_submission_status (line 351 commit). With conn.isolation_level=None (database.py:
  - _code-refute_: verdict=CONFIRMED, refuted=False - Re-read all cited files and independently verified every load-bearing claim.   - _severity_: verdict=CONFIRMED, refuted=False - Verified against source. ### F-023 [Medium / CONFIRMED] SQLiteResearchRepository.save_attempt uses plain INSERT (no OR IGNORE); a resume of a still-RUNNING research run whose deterministic attempt_id collides raises sqlite3.IntegrityError inside analyze_with_research_committee

- **Area / Dimension.** CONCURRENCY-TX
- **Affected workflow.** Fresh research invocation flow / resume
- **Affected files.** `src/trading_research/storage/research_repositories.py`, `src/trading_research/research/orchestration.py`
- **Line ranges.** src/trading_research/storage/research_repositories.py:185-219; src/trading_research/research/orchestration.py:245-252, 807-811
- **Observed behavior.**
  `save_run_started` uses `INSERT OR IGNORE` (research_repositories.py:178-183). `save_attempt_failure` uses `INSERT OR IGNORE` (line 226). `save_role_report` uses `INSERT OR IGNORE` (line 250). `save_decision` uses `INSERT OR IGNORE` (line 262). But `save_attempt` (line 187-219) is a plain `INSERT`. `_attempt_id` (orchestration.py:245-252) is deterministic across replays (research_run_id-role-attempt_number, optionally suffixed by a hash of scheduler_run_id). Because the reuse gate at orchestration.py:740-762 short-circuits only on COMPLETED / ANALYSIS_INCOMPLETE / ANALYST_REPORTS_COMPLETE_NO_MANAGER — not RUNNING or FAILED — a caller that re-enters `analyze_with_research_committee` for a research_run_id whose research_committee_runs row is stuck at status='RUNNING' (e.g. after a crash between save_run_started and any terminal mark_run_finished) will re-run every role from attempt_number=0, and if a prior attempt row was already persisted, the plain INSERT will raise IntegrityError.
- **Failure scenario.**
  A shadow scheduler run crashes after `save_attempt` committed for role='analyst_growth', attempt_number=0 but before `mark_run_finished`. The research_committee_runs row is stuck at RUNNING. A subsequent invocation with the same snapshot/provider/roles/config yields the same research_run_id (compute_research_run_id, orchestration.py:214-236). The reuse gate at :740-762 sees status='RUNNING' and does NOT short-circuit. `_run_role_with_retries` runs analyst_growth again, computes the same attempt_id, `save_attempt` raises `sqlite3.IntegrityError: UNIQUE constraint failed: research_attempts.attempt_id`. The whole orchestration aborts and the run remains permanently stuck at RUNNING.
- **Impact.** Once a research run gets stranded at RUNNING (any crash mid-orchestration), all subsequent replays with the same identity crash before completing; no janitor code path resets RUNNING→FAILED and no code writes RUN_STATUS_FAILED (declared but never used at orchestration.py:60).
- **Recommended correction.** Change save_attempt to `INSERT OR IGNORE` (matching the rest of the repository), and/or transition orphaned RUNNING rows to FAILED on entry to `analyze_with_research_committee` (i.e., make RUNNING+missing terminal a first-class case in the reuse gate).

### F-024 [Medium / CONFIRMED] Research reuse gate: read-then-write between get_run_status and save_run_started is not serialized; concurrent workers with the same deterministic research_run_id race to run the same orchestration and double-consume budget

- **Area / Dimension.** CONCURRENCY-TX
- **Affected workflow.** Fresh research invocation / concurrent scheduler and manual run
- **Affected files.** `src/trading_research/research/orchestration.py`, `src/trading_research/storage/research_repositories.py`
- **Line ranges.** src/trading_research/research/orchestration.py:735-773; src/trading_research/storage/research_repositories.py:152-183
- **Observed behavior.**
  `analyze_with_research_committee` reads `research_repository.get_run_status(research_run_id)` at orchestration.py:741 (plain SELECT, no lock) and — if None — falls through to call `save_run_started` at :769-773 which uses INSERT OR IGNORE and commits in autocommit (research_repositories.py:177-183). Two workers computing the identical deterministic research_run_id can both observe status=None and both proceed past the reuse gate. The header row is deduped by INSERT OR IGNORE, but the actual provider calls, attempt persistence, and token-budget reservations are not: each worker calls `_run_role_with_retries` independently, and per-attempt idempotency keys only key by (run_id, symbol, provider, model, attempt_identity, utc_date) (`reserve_research_tokens`, strategies/research_budget.py:158-166) — the attempt_identity is (role, attempt_number), so each worker independently increments through its own attempt sequence and both charge the token budget.
- **Failure scenario.**
  Two scheduled runners fire nearly simultaneously (schedule catch-up + a manual `run-research` on the same snapshot). Both compute the same research_run_id, both see status=None, both save_run_started (only one INSERTs; the other's INSERT OR IGNORE is a no-op), both invoke provider.generate_structured for role='analyst_value', both reserve tokens with distinct idempotency keys (attempt_identity 'analyst_value:0' vs 'analyst_value:0' — actually the same, so the second's reserve_research_tokens will return the existing reservation). But two save_attempts run on the same deterministic attempt_id (finding CONCURRENCY-TX-03) → one raises IntegrityError. Provider call was already made twice, however, so real API tokens were consumed twice on the caller's actual Claude subscription/Codex/etc.
- **Impact.** Duplicate provider calls (and real token consumption) on any concurrent invocation collision; the double-charge is invisible to the persistent token budget because both workers reuse the same reservation identity.
- **Recommended correction.** Wrap the reuse gate in a caller-owned BEGIN IMMEDIATE + insert-with-conflict-detect (i.e., a real reservation lease keyed by research_run_id), or short-circuit early on a still-active RUNNING row instead of merely None.

### F-025 [Medium / CONFIRMED] Cross-day carry-forward of an unstarted RESERVED attempt reservation returns yesterday's row and skips today's daily-notional cap check

- **Area / Dimension.** CONCURRENCY-TX
- **Affected workflow.** External paper submission (cross-day)
- **Affected files.** `src/trading_research/paper_books/external_broker.py`, `src/trading_research/storage/paper_books_repositories.py`
- **Line ranges.** src/trading_research/paper_books/external_broker.py:274-293, 328-335; src/trading_research/storage/paper_books_repositories.py:1792-1832
- **Observed behavior.**
  `_reserve_daily_notional` (external_broker.py:274-293) calls `load_active_attempt_reservation` and, if it finds an active row whose `reservation_date != today` and `state == RESERVED`, validates payload and returns the yesterday row unchanged. It never runs the today-cap sum (external_broker.py:328-335) and never updates `reservation_date`. `sum_attempt_reservations_by_date(today)` (paper_books_repositories.py:1792-1832) filters strictly by `reservation_date = ?` and therefore does not count the carried-forward yesterday row against today's cap.
- **Failure scenario.**
  On UTC-day 1, `_reserve_daily_notional` writes attempt-0 RESERVED with reservation_date='2026-07-19' for a $100 order (assume account cap $150). Runtime submission raises before SUBMISSION_REQUESTED commits, leaving RESERVED. The event chain rolls back so the intent is not obviously in flight. On UTC-day 2 the operator re-invokes submit; `_reserve_daily_notional` returns the yesterday row without cap check. Meanwhile a second, distinct order is submitted for $100 today; `sum_attempt_reservations_by_date('2026-07-20')` sees only the second order's row and permits it. Effective total reserved for day 2 = $200 vs cap $150.
- **Impact.** Daily-notional cap can be silently exceeded across UTC-day boundaries whenever an unstarted RESERVED attempt lingers from a prior day.
- **Recommended correction.** When an active reservation is found with a different reservation_date, either (a) reject with ATTEMPT_RESERVATION_CONFLICT (require an explicit cross-day retry with supersede evidence), or (b) supersede the stale RESERVED row atomically and reserve a fresh row against today. Also consider counting active carry-forward RESERVED rows against every day they remain active until superseded.
- **Finder confidence.** medium
- **Verification.** 3 votes (3 confirmed, 0 plausible, 0 refuted).
  - _severity_: verdict=CONFIRMED, refuted=False - Verified against code. In `_reserve_daily_notional` (external_broker.py:265), the reservation row is committed in its own transaction independent of the caller's fenced_write. `_submit_once` (external_broker.py:1210-1226) then writes SUBMISSION_REQUESTED inside `lease.fenced_write()`; if that fenced_write rolls back (lease loss at verify_or_raise line 856, insufficient shares/cash, or any other fa
  - _code-refute_: verdict=CONFIRMED, refuted=False - Verified against the cited files.  1) `_reserve_daily_notional` (external_broker.py:261-293) does exactly what the finding claims. When `load_active_attempt_reservation` returns a row (which matches by client_order_id/attempt/fingerprint/book_id without any date predicate — see repositories.py:1759-1775), the code only rejects it when BOTH the reservation_date differs AND the state is not RESERVED
  - _reachability_: verdict=CONFIRMED, refuted=False - Verified the cited lines and traced the reachability paths.  At external_broker.py:281-284, `_validate_attempt_reservation_payload` is called WITHOUT the `reservation_date=` kwarg, so cross-date payload does not fail validation. The guard at 285-292 raises ATTEMPT_RESERVATION_CONFLICT only when `active["state"] != RESERVED`; a yesterday RESERVED row falls through the guard and is returned at 293. 

### F-026 [Medium / CONFIRMED] `shadow-no-op-{intended_schedule_id}` synthetic summary id collides with the real scheduler_run_id summary; INSERT OR REPLACE silently overwrites lightweight-no-op history

- **Area / Dimension.** CONCURRENCY-TX
- **Affected workflow.** Scheduled shadow cycle no-op summary persistence
- **Affected files.** `src/trading_research/shadow/scheduler.py`, `src/trading_research/storage/shadow_alerts_repositories.py`

- **Observed behavior.**
  `_save_no_op_summary` (scheduler.py:238) synthesizes `summary_id = scheduler_run_id or f"shadow-no-op-{intended_schedule_id or uuid.uuid4().hex}"`. Every subsequent lightweight no-op (DISABLED / NOT_DUE / PAUSED / LEASE_HELD / ALREADY_COMPLETED) for the same intended slot lands on the identical synthetic `summary_id`. `save_run_summary` (shadow_alerts_repositories.py:130-149) uses `INSERT OR REPLACE INTO shadow_run_summaries`, so a repeated no-op for the same intended slot silently overwrites the prior no-op summary row. There is no ordering discipline (INSERT OR REPLACE keeps only the last), so history of e.g. DISABLED→NOT_DUE→PAUSED transitions is not preserved.
- **Failure scenario.**
  The scheduler is invoked hourly by cron for an already-COMPLETED intended slot: each invocation writes a fresh `shadow-no-op-{intended_schedule_id}` row via INSERT OR REPLACE, overwriting whatever verdict the last no-op recorded. Operators auditing why the scheduler ran at 14:00 will see only the most-recent no-op's `health_reasons_json`; the prior 13:00 verdict is destroyed.
- **Impact.** Loss of audit history for repeated no-op invocations against the same intended slot; possible masking of a transient KILLED/PAUSED state that was later cleared.
- **Recommended correction.** Use an append-only per-invocation id (e.g. include a wall-clock timestamp or uuid4 in the synthetic key), or add an ordered `no_op_seq` column and switch from INSERT OR REPLACE to insert-if-new-else-append.
- **Finder confidence.** high
- **Verification.** 3 votes (3 confirmed, 0 plausible, 0 refuted).

The CLI wiring for run-due-shado
  - _code-refute_: verdict=CONFIRMED, refuted=False - Verified all cited lines in the actual code: scheduler.py:238 constructs `summary_id = scheduler_run_id or f"shadow-no-op-{intended_schedule_id or uuid.uuid4().hex}"`; shadow_alerts_repositories.py:130 uses `INSERT OR REPLACE INTO shadow_run_summaries`; shadow_alerts_schema.py:44 declares `scheduler_run_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY`. For every no-op call site where the intended_schedule_id is resolved (NOT
  - _severity_: verdict=CONFIRMED, refuted=False - Mechanic confirmed. In scheduler.py:238 `_save_no_op_summary` derives `summary_id = scheduler_run_id or f"shadow-no-op-{intended_schedule_id or uuid.uuid4().hex}"`. ### F-027 [Medium / CONFIRMED] Local paper BUY intent persisted with no cash reservation when market input is unavailable — a subsequent lifecycle re-submit refuses on the mismatched-reservation guard, permanently stranding the intent

- **Area / Dimension.** CONCURRENCY-TX
- **Affected workflow.** Local paper intent creation / lifecycle re-submit
- **Affected files.** `src/trading_research/paper_books/scheduled_integration.py`, `src/trading_research/paper_books/execution.py`, `src/trading_research/paper_books/lifecycle.py`
- **Line ranges.** src/trading_research/paper_books/scheduled_integration.py:385-391; src/trading_research/paper_books/execution.py:117-168; src/trading_research/paper_books/lifecycle.py:186-232
- **Observed behavior.**
  In `_process_arm`, when `_build_market_simulation_input` returns None the arm writes the intent via `order_intent.persist_order_intent(conn, intent)` alone (scheduled_integration.py:385-391). No `cash_ledger.reserve_for_order` is called; the intent row is inserted at status PENDING_SUBMISSION and no BUY reservation exists. On a subsequent lifecycle pass (`_process_pending_orders` at lifecycle.py:186-232), `submit_and_simulate` takes the `existing is not None` branch (execution.py:117-168). Because `existing['side']==BUY` and status==PENDING_SUBMISSION, the guard at execution.py:158-168 compares `cash_ledger.remaining_buy_reservation(conn, book_id, intent_id)` (which returns 0 for an intent with no reservation) to `intent.notional_usd` (positive), fails the equality check, and raises `FillSimulationError('existing reservation does not match the frozen intent notional -- refusing to proceed rather than fabricate a release or adjustment')`. Because the fail-closed check refuses `submit_and_simulate` unconditionally, the intent is now stranded — it never receives a fill even when the market input becomes available in a later cycle.
- **Failure scenario.**
  Cycle 1 for symbol AAPL: `_build_market_simulation_input` returns None (e.g. evidence snapshot has no bid/ask). `persist_order_intent` writes the intent PENDING_SUBMISSION with $500 notional and no reservation. Cycle 2 next market day: `_process_pending_orders` finds AAPL pending, sees market input available, calls `submit_and_simulate`; the existing-branch guard sees remaining_buy_reservation=0 ≠ $500 and raises FillSimulationError. The intent never fills. It never expires either until `age_market_days >= expire_after_market_days` — until then every lifecycle pass raises.
- **Impact.** Silent hang of any BUY intent that ever went through the market-simulation-unavailable branch; operator has no clean remediation path until the expiry window elapses. Every lifecycle pass raises an unhandled `FillSimulationError` which may abort the whole run depending on caller.
- **Recommended correction.** Either write the BUY reservation together with the persist_order_intent in a single atomic transaction on the market-simulation-unavailable branch, or teach `submit_and_simulate`'s existing-intent branch to lazily create the missing reservation when no external evidence exists (mirroring the lazy claim path immediately above).
- **Finder confidence.** medium
- **Verification.** 3 votes (2 confirmed, 0 plausible, 1 refuted).

  - _code-refute_: verdict=CONFIRMED, refuted=False - Verified against source code. The finding faithfully reproduces from the cited files.  1) scheduled_integration.py:385-391: On the market-input-unavailable branch, the code calls only `order_intent.persist_order_intent(conn, intent)` and does NOT invoke `cash_ledger.reserve_for_order`. `persist_order_intent` (order_intent.py:61-64) just calls `repo.save_order_intent`, so the intent row is written 
  - _severity_: verdict=CONFIRMED, refuted=False - Verified against source. In scheduled_integration.py:385-391 (`_process_arm`), when `_build_market_simulation_input` returns None the intent is persisted via `order_intent.persist_order_intent` (order_intent.py:61-64 -> `repo.save_order_intent` only) with no cash reservation. On a subsequent lifecycle pass, `_process_pending_orders` (lifecycle.py:174-254) rebuilds the intent, obtains a fresh marke

### F-029 [Medium / CONFIRMED] research.enabled and scheduled_research.enabled flags have no runtime effect

- **Area / Dimension.** CONFIG-ACTIVATION
- **Affected files.** `src/trading_research/research/configuration.py`, `src/trading_research/research/scheduled_research_config.py`, `src/trading_research/cli.py`, `src/trading_research/research/orchestration.py`
- **Line ranges.** research/configuration.py:75,297; research/scheduled_research_config.py:43,120; cli.py:775-846, 1383-1448; research/orchestration.py (no matches for 'enabled')
- **Observed behavior.**
  `ResearchConfiguration.enabled` and `ScheduledResearchYamlConfiguration.enabled` are both required, strict-typed boolean fields (research/configuration.py:75,297; research/scheduled_research_config.py:43,120). Grepping `research_config.enabled|research_configuration.enabled|sr_config.enabled|configuration.enabled` across `src/` returns no runtime consumers — no code path in `run_research_cli` (cli.py:775-846), `run_research_cycle_cli` (cli.py:1383-1448), `run_due_shadow_cycle_cli` (cli.py:1641-1882), orchestration.analyze_with_research_committee, or scheduled_cycle.run_scheduled_research_cycle checks the flag. Only `require_ready()` (research/configuration.py:103-112) is called, and it only validates provider/model — never `enabled`.
- **Failure scenario.**
  Operator loads `config/production/research.yaml` (which sets `research.enabled: true`, `provider: claude_code`) for a preflight test, then reverts to the shipped `config/research.yaml` (`research.enabled: false`) intending to disable research until further review. They subsequently run `python -m trading_research.cli run-research --provider claude_code --snapshot-id ...`. Because no code consults `research_config.enabled`, the disabled setting has no effect: provider preflight and inference proceed. The same applies for `scheduled_research.enabled` in `run-research-cycle` — although that CLI hard-codes a DeterministicResearchProvider, it still executes a full cycle (evidence load, DB writes) when `scheduled_research.enabled=false`.
- **Impact.** Documentation (research.yaml:12-13, scheduled_research.yaml:9-10) frames these as top-level capability gates that must be flipped to true before any real Claude call. An operator relying on the shipped `enabled: false` to prevent invocations gets no protection; the flag is decorative. This also creates a monotonicity risk: once a config is temporarily set true it must be affirmatively un-set, and the operator has no runtime guarantee the flag matters.
- **Recommended correction.** Add explicit `if not research_config.enabled` and `if not sr_config.enabled` guards at CLI entry points (or in `require_ready()`) returning a fail-closed error status. Alternatively, remove the field if it is intentionally documentation-only, matching the pattern used for `execution.yaml`'s `trading_mode` (which does have a runtime effect).

  - _code-refute_: verdict=CONFIRMED, refuted=False - Direct re-reading of the cited files reproduces the claim. ### F-030 [Medium / CONFIRMED] Research runs orphaned at RUN_STATUS_RUNNING after crash; RUN_STATUS_FAILED constant is never written and there is no janitor

- **Area / Dimension.** CRASH-RECOVERY
- **Affected workflow.** Fresh research invocation flows
- **Affected files.** `src/trading_research/research/orchestration.py`, `src/trading_research/storage/research_repositories.py`
- **Line ranges.** orchestration.py:57-67, 740-773; research_repositories.py:173-183, 272-277
- **Observed behavior.**
  `RUN_STATUS_FAILED = 'FAILED'` is declared at orchestration.py:60 but Grep across `src/trading_research` returns only its definition — nothing ever writes it. `save_run_started` uses `INSERT OR IGNORE` and commits (research_repositories.py:177-183). The reuse gate at orchestration.py:742-762 acts only on COMPLETED / ANALYSIS_INCOMPLETE / ANALYST_REPORTS_COMPLETE_NO_MANAGER — RUN_STATUS_RUNNING and RUN_STATUS_FAILED are NOT short-circuited. `mark_run_finished` (research_repositories.py:272-277) is a plain UPDATE with no WHERE-status predicate.
- **Failure scenario.**
  A research run for a given deterministic `research_run_id` writes a `research_committee_runs` row with status='RUNNING' at :770-773, then the process is killed (SIGTERM, host reboot, provider CLI SIGKILL) before any terminal `mark_run_finished` fires. The row is now permanently RUNNING. On the next invocation the reuse gate sees existing_status='RUNNING' (not in the three handled states) and falls through to full re-orchestration, re-entering `_run_role_with_retries`. Because `save_attempt` (research_repositories.py:185-219) uses a plain `INSERT` (not `INSERT OR IGNORE` like the other saves), if any prior attempt row was persisted for the same `attempt_id`, this raises sqlite3.IntegrityError and aborts the retry.
- **Impact.** After any hard crash during research execution the affected run is unrecoverable: reuse gate lets it re-enter, but the plain-INSERT `save_attempt` collision aborts. Operators cannot re-run without manual DB intervention. Persistent counters in `shadow_budget_reservations` may also have RESERVED rows that were AMBIGUOUS at crash but never got settled.
- **Why existing tests do not prevent it.** Tests always start from clean DBs and complete every run.
- **Recommended correction.** Transition RUNNING → FAILED on next invocation if the row is older than some grace threshold, use `INSERT OR IGNORE` in save_attempt keyed by attempt_id, and gate the reuse check on RUNNING/FAILED explicitly.
- **Recommended regression tests.** Missing: no test opens a DB with a pre-seeded RUNNING research_committee_runs row and re-invokes `analyze_with_research_committee`.
- **Capability blocked.** Research resume after crash
- **Finder confidence.** high
- **Verification.** 3 votes (2 confirmed, 1 plausible, 0 refuted).
  - _code-refute_: verdict=CONFIRMED, refuted=False - Independently reproduced each factual claim from the cited files: - orchestration.py:60 defines RUN_STATUS_FAILED = "FAILED"; grep across src/trading_research shows only this one occurrence — the constant is dead. - research_repositories.py:177-183: save_run_started uses INSERT OR IGNORE, writes status='RUNNING'. - orchestration.py:742-762: reuse gate has three branches for COMPLETED / ANALYSIS_IN
  - _severity_: verdict=CONFIRMED, refuted=False - Verified against source: - orchestration.py:60 declares RUN_STATUS_FAILED; grep of entire repo finds no other reference — never assigned/written. - Reuse gate at orchestration.py:742-762 short-circuits only COMPLETED, ANALYSIS_INCOMPLETE, ANALYST_REPORTS_COMPLETE_NO_MANAGER. RUNNING and FAILED fall through to save_run_started (INSERT OR IGNORE, no state transition) and re-orchestration. - save_run
  - _reachability_: verdict=PLAUSIBLE, refuted=False - Code-level facts are confirmed by reading the cited files: (1) RUN_STATUS_FAILED is defined at orchestration.py:60 but Grep across the repo returns only its definition — no write path exists; (2) save_run_started at research_repositories.py:177-183 writes 'RUNNING' and commits before role work begins; (3) the reuse gate at orchestration.py:742-762 handles only COMPLETED, ANALYSIS_INCOMPLETE, ANALY

### F-031 [Medium / CONFIRMED] `save_attempt` uses plain INSERT, so a crash between attempt save and role-report save produces IntegrityError on resume

- **Area / Dimension.** CRASH-RECOVERY
- **Affected workflow.** Fresh research invocation flows
- **Affected files.** `src/trading_research/storage/research_repositories.py`, `src/trading_research/research/orchestration.py`
- **Line ranges.** research_repositories.py:185-219; orchestration.py:807-811
- **Observed behavior.**
  `save_attempt` (research_repositories.py:187-197) is a plain `INSERT ... VALUES (...)` followed by `self._conn.commit()` — unlike `save_run_started` (:178 INSERT OR IGNORE), `save_attempt_failure` (:226 INSERT OR IGNORE), `save_role_report` (:250 INSERT OR IGNORE) and `save_decision` (:262 INSERT OR IGNORE). The orchestrator persists attempts one-at-a-time in the loop at orchestration.py:807-811 after they succeed, and the role report is only persisted at :839. `attempt_id` is deterministic (`compute_research_run_id`+role+attempt_number), so a resumed run reconstructs the same id.
- **Failure scenario.**
  Attempt 1 of role 'analyst_a' produces a valid report. `save_attempt` commits (:809). Process crashes before `save_role_report` at :839. On resume the reuse gate at orchestration.py:791-797 finds no persisted role report for 'analyst_a' and re-enters `_run_role_with_retries`. `_run_role_with_retries` computes `_attempt_id(research_run_id,'analyst_a',1)` — identical to the prior attempt — and eventually calls `save_attempt` again. Plain INSERT collides on the PRIMARY KEY of `research_attempts`, raising sqlite3.IntegrityError, which is not caught inside orchestration.py and propagates out of `analyze_with_research_committee`.
- **Impact.** Unrecoverable role-level resume: the operator cannot re-run without manually deleting the previously-persisted attempt row, and immutability elsewhere in the schema makes that unsafe.
- **Why existing tests do not prevent it.** No test seeds a research_attempts row and re-invokes the orchestrator for the same run_id/role.

Additionally `record_failure` is only called for RuntimeUnavailable/Timeout transport errors on :70-73 — an UNKNOWN_BROKER_STATUS raise leaves NO row in `paper_execution_failures`.
- **Failure scenario.**

Every poll of the affected submission aborts the poller with an uncaught RuntimeOperationError; the CLI `sync-paper-orders` returns non-zero without persisting any evidence of which intent failed, and (because the raise is inside the loop over `list_unresolved_submissions`) every subsequent submission in the same sweep is skipped.

### F-034 [Medium / CONFIRMED] External-paper submit's daily-notional reservation and SUBMISSION_REQUESTED checkpoint are not co-atomic, allowing an orphan RESERVED row on crash

- **Area / Dimension.** CRASH-RECOVERY
- **Affected workflow.** External paper submission
- **Affected files.** `src/trading_research/paper_books/external_broker.py`
- **Line ranges.** 261-344, 1329-1359, 1188-1240
- **Observed behavior.**
  `submit_external_paper_order` calls `_reserve_daily_notional` at :1349 which commits its own transaction (:265). Only afterward is `_submit_once` called at :1354, which opens a separate `lease.fenced_write()` transaction at :1210 that commits the cash reservation and STATE_SUBMISSION_REQUESTED event. Between the two commits nothing prevents a hard crash.
- **Failure scenario.**
  Attempt-0 for a new client_order_id: `_reserve_daily_notional` commits RESERVED for today. Host is killed before `_submit_once`'s fenced_write commits. On restart, the event chain is still at STATE_PREVIEWED but a RESERVED row exists for attempt-0 today. A fresh `submit_external_paper_order` re-enters and reuses the existing reservation (line 267-273) — self-heals same-day. However, if the restart occurs on the next UTC day, `_reserve_daily_notional` at :280-293 special-cases the cross-date active RESERVED and returns it unchanged (`active["reservation_date"] != reservation_date` combined with `active["state"] == _RESERVATION_STATE_RESERVED` → returns active at :293), so today's cap is never charged for the actual submit that runs today.
- **Impact.** Same-day recovery works, but a crash on day boundary allows a submission to bypass today's daily-notional cap check by carrying forward yesterday's reservation. Depending on cadence this could allow $50 over the $150/day account cap.
- **Why existing tests do not prevent it.** Existing tests use a single frozen clock; no cross-day crash test exists.
- **Recommended correction.** Either co-commit the daily reservation and SUBMISSION_REQUESTED event in one transaction, or when carrying forward a stale-date RESERVED row across UTC boundaries force a re-charge against today's `sum_attempt_reservations_by_date`.
- **Recommended regression tests.** Missing.
- **Capability blocked.** Daily-notional cap under cross-day crash
- **Finder confidence.** medium
- **Verification.** 3 votes (2 confirmed, 1 plausible, 0 refuted).

On day D+1, a resubmit with a

### F-035 [Medium / CONFIRMED] Migration 2 performs O(N * 4) row-scoped SELECTs inside a single BEGIN IMMEDIATE and can hold the write lock past the 5s busy_timeout

- **Area / Dimension.** DB-MIGRATION
- **Affected files.** `src/trading_research/storage/schema_version.py`, `src/trading_research/storage/database.py`
- **Line ranges.** src/trading_research/storage/schema_version.py:61-93, 325-341; src/trading_research/storage/database.py:47
- **Observed behavior.**
  `_migration_2_backfill_execution_namespace_claims` (schema_version.py:71-93) issues one full `SELECT book_id, paper_order_intent_id FROM paper_book_orders` then, per row, up to four `SELECT 1 FROM <table> WHERE book_id=? AND paper_order_intent_id=? LIMIT 1` probes plus one `INSERT ... WHERE NOT EXISTS` into `paper_order_execution_claims`. The entire loop executes inside the `with transaction(conn):` BEGIN IMMEDIATE that wraps every migration (schema_version.py:333-338). No batching, no yielding of the write lock.
- **Failure scenario.**
  A production upgrade against a large legacy database (thousands of rows in `paper_book_orders`) runs the migration on first `connect()`. During the migration transaction, any concurrent `connect()` from a shadow scheduler, dashboard, or CLI must acquire the SQLite write lock (BEGIN IMMEDIATE holds it exclusively) and will fail with `sqlite3.OperationalError: database is locked` once its 5000ms `busy_timeout` (database.py:47) expires. The dashboard, CLI, and scheduler processes then error out during startup for the duration of the migration.
- **Impact.** Upgrade cost is proportional to legacy volume with no upper bound; concurrent connections during upgrade can spuriously fail even though the migration itself is progressing. This becomes progressively worse over the deployment lifetime.
- **Recommended correction.** Batch the backfill (e.g., commit every 500-1000 rows via nested `transaction(conn)` blocks) or drive it from a single SQL statement (`INSERT INTO paper_order_execution_claims ... SELECT ... FROM paper_book_orders LEFT JOIN ...`).

### F-036 [Medium / CONFIRMED] Legacy external-daily-reservation backfill is bounded at LIMIT 100000 with no advancement pointer; a single bad row wedges every connect()

- **Area / Dimension.** DB-MIGRATION
- **Affected files.** `src/trading_research/storage/paper_books_schema.py`
- **Line ranges.** src/trading_research/storage/paper_books_schema.py:1588-1633, 1676-1692
- **Observed behavior.**
  `_backfill_active_legacy_external_reservations` selects the first 100000 active rows ordered by `client_order_id` (paper_books_schema.py:1590-1594) inside a `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` (1680). It raises `sqlite3.IntegrityError` (1606, 1632) on any mismatch, and the outer `try/except: conn.rollback(); raise` unwinds the whole batch. `_external_reservation_migration_needed` (1652-1673) re-triggers the same backfill on every future `connect()` as long as any qualifying legacy row lacks an attempt-0 twin.
- **Failure scenario.**
  A legacy database has 5 legacy rows whose current lineage differs from the deterministic `derive_external_attempt_reservation_id(client_order_id, 0, fingerprint, date)` — e.g., a manual repair inserted an attempt-0 row with a different `reserved_notional_usd`. On next `connect()`, `_backfill_active_legacy_external_reservations` iterates rows in `client_order_id` order and raises on the first offending one; the transaction rolls back, discarding any successful inserts earlier in the batch. Every subsequent `connect()` re-enters the migration, hits the same offending row, and raises again — the database can no longer be opened until the offending row is manually reconciled.
- **Impact.** A single conflicting legacy row indefinitely bricks `connect()` for the whole application; there is no forward-progress guarantee and no partial-completion checkpoint.
- **Recommended correction.** Skip-and-log failing rows into a persistent quarantine table (or advance a monotonic `last_migrated_client_order_id` cursor) so a bad row can be observed by operators without blocking further upgrades, and commit progress in bounded batches instead of one 100k-row transaction.

  - _code-refute_: verdict=CONFIRMED, refuted=False - Verified against src/trading_research/storage/paper_books_schema.py:1588-1692. ### F-037 [Medium / CONFIRMED] Cross-day carry-forward of an unstarted RESERVED attempt-0 reservation bypasses today's $150 UTC-day cap

- **Severity note.** Original finder severity: High; adjusted after verification to Medium.
- **Area / Dimension.** FINANCIAL-SAFETY
- **Affected workflow.** External-paper submission — daily notional budget
- **Affected files.** `src/trading_research/paper_books/external_broker.py`, `src/trading_research/storage/paper_books_repositories.py`
- **Line ranges.** src/trading_research/paper_books/external_broker.py:261-293; src/trading_research/storage/paper_books_repositories.py:1759-1775,1792-1832
- **Observed behavior.**
  `_reserve_daily_notional` opens its own BEGIN IMMEDIATE (external_broker.py:265), computes `reservation_date = today` (:261), and first looks up any active attempt reservation via `load_active_attempt_reservation` (:275). If one exists on a *different* `reservation_date` but its state is still `RESERVED`, the code path at :285-293 explicitly returns that prior-day row unchanged (`only an unstarted reserved attempt may carry forward across dates`) and never inserts a new row against today's date. `sum_attempt_reservations_by_date` (repositories:1792-1832) filters strictly by `reservation_date = ?`, so that carried-forward reservation counts toward day-1's cap and today's cap is completely unaware of it. Because `_reserve_daily_notional` runs in a transaction distinct from `_submit_once`'s SUBMISSION_REQUESTED fenced_write (external_broker.py:1210-1226), a crash between those two commits is exactly the state that produces an orphan attempt-0 RESERVED row for later carry-forward.
- **Failure scenario.**
  Day 1: operator submits an external paper order for $50. `_reserve_daily_notional` commits a RESERVED row for attempt=0/date=Day1/$50. The process crashes before `_submit_once` commits SUBMISSION_REQUESTED. Day 2: operator (or an automated retry) re-invokes `submit_external_paper_order` for the same intent. The current event is still PREVIEWED (the fenced_write never committed), so the code passes the current-event guard at 1341-1343 and re-enters `_reserve_daily_notional` with today=Day2. `load_active_attempt_reservation` returns the Day-1 RESERVED row; state==RESERVED so :285-293 returns it. Day-2's `sum_attempt_reservations_by_date` is 0 -- the operator is now free to submit up to $150 more today under fresh reservations, for an effective account-wide total of $200 in a rolling 48h window despite the invariant.
- **Impact.** Violates the authoritative-boundary invariant `maximum_daily_notional_usd = $150` (config/paper_books.yaml:77) whenever a crash occurs between the reservation commit and the SUBMISSION_REQUESTED commit. The excess submission is real broker exposure, not internal simulation.
- **Why existing tests do not prevent it.** Existing tests assert same-day idempotent replay of the reservation row; the cross-date carry-forward branch is an intentional-looking special case (guard message reads `only an unstarted reserved attempt may carry forward across dates`) that appears to have no negative test for the cap-underflow scenario it enables.
- **Recommended correction.** Refuse to carry forward any reservation whose `reservation_date` differs from today's UTC date. Either (a) require the caller to explicitly settle/supersede the stale RESERVED row before a fresh-date reserve, or (b) always insert a new row against today's date and settle the old one to SUPERSEDED_BY_RETRY inside the same transaction (mirroring the cross-day retry supersede path already implemented at :294-327).
- **Recommended regression tests.** Freeze clock to Day 1 UTC, run `_reserve_daily_notional` for $50 at attempt=0, then advance clock to Day 2 UTC and call `_reserve_daily_notional` again for the same client_order_id/attempt=0. Assert that either the second call charges Day-2's cap OR the first row is settled to a terminal-for-budget state. Then run a fresh $150 reserve on Day 2 and assert it is rejected.
- **Finder confidence.** high
- **Verification.** 3 votes (2 confirmed, 1 plausible, 0 refuted).
  - _code-refute_: verdict=CONFIRMED, refuted=False - The finding is corroborated by direct reading of the code. `_reserve_daily_notional` (external_broker.py:230-344) commits the RESERVED row in its own `with transaction(conn):` block at :265, while `_submit_once` (:1188-1226) later commits the reservation-plus-SUBMISSION_REQUESTED write in a distinct `lease.fenced_write()` transaction. A crash between the two commits leaves a durable RESERVED attem
  - _severity_: verdict=CONFIRMED, refuted=False - Traced the exact code path: `_reserve_daily_notional` (external_broker.py:265) opens its own transaction and commits the RESERVED row before `_submit_once` begins its fenced_write (external_broker.py:1210-1226). Verified the carry-forward branch at :285-293 returns the prior-date RESERVED row without inserting a new date row. Confirmed `sum_attempt_reservations_by_date` (repositories:1792-1832) fi
  - _reachability_: verdict=PLAUSIBLE, refuted=False - The described mechanism (Day-1 RESERVED row returned by :285-293 without new-date accounting) is real in code, and the crash window between the reservation transaction (:265) and the SUBMISSION_REQUESTED commit (:1210-1226) is genuinely reachable. However, the finding's literal scenario ("Day 1 crash, Day 2 retry") is REFUTED for typical multi-hour cross-day gaps: `_intent` enforces `reject_stale_

### F-038 [Medium / CONFIRMED] Required paper-approval phrase is documented but never checked at the submission entry point

- **Severity note.** Original finder severity: High; adjusted after verification to Medium.
- **Area / Dimension.** FINANCIAL-SAFETY
- **Affected workflow.** External-paper submission — operator approval
- **Affected files.** `src/trading_research/paper_books/external_broker.py`, `src/trading_research/cli.py`
- **Line ranges.** src/trading_research/paper_books/external_broker.py:1319-1367, 115-119; src/trading_research/cli.py:361-372
- **Observed behavior.**
  The invariant audit lists `Required approval phrase (Paper): 'Approved, submit this exact paper order.'` as `documented-not-enforced`/`MISSING`. `submit_external_paper_order` accepts arbitrary `operator` and `reason` strings and only checks them through `_bounded(...)` (external_broker.py:1324, helper at :115-119), which merely enforces 1..N characters. The CLI shim `external_paper_submit_cli` (cli.py:361-372) forwards those strings without additional phrase checks. No file in `paper_books/` or `cli.py` contains a comparison against the required approval string.
- **Failure scenario.**
  Operator (or a scripted caller) invokes `submit_external_paper_order(..., operator='ops', reason='ok')`. The submission proceeds through every gate and (assuming preview/reservation/lease succeed) reaches the runtime — with no evidence that the approval phrase was ever spoken. The system-wide invariant guarding real-money-adjacent broker submission is a documentation artifact only.
- **Impact.** Removes a documented human-in-the-loop safeguard against accidental or automated submission on the isolated Alpaca-paper account. Because the same code path is the only production route into the external broker, an approval-phrase policy that is not code-enforced is trivially bypassed.
- **Why existing tests do not prevent it.** No test asserts the phrase is required; existing tests supply arbitrary reason strings and expect success, which the code allows.
- **Recommended correction.** Enforce the exact approval phrase in `submit_external_paper_order` (and in every mutating external entry point: retry, cancel) as a fail-closed check performed before any lease acquisition, and record the raw check-passed evidence in the persisted event row. Alternatively, delete the invariant from the audit and document the trust model in code.
- **Recommended regression tests.** Assert `submit_external_paper_order(..., reason='no')` fails closed with a distinct error code. Assert the phrase check runs before the runtime is contacted (no `runtime.*` calls made).
- **Finder confidence.** high
- **Verification.** 3 votes (3 confirmed, 0 plausible, 0 refuted).

### F-039 [Medium / CONFIRMED] Retry crash between supersede-and-new-reserve transaction and SUBMISSION_REQUESTED commit strands a RESERVED row that no code path can release

- **Area / Dimension.** FINANCIAL-SAFETY
- **Affected workflow.** External-paper cross-day retry — reservation lifecycle
- **Affected files.** `src/trading_research/paper_books/external_broker.py`
- **Line ranges.** external_broker.py:230-344, 377-420, 1546-1632, 1635-1696, 1959-2199
- **Observed behavior.**
  `retry_external_paper_order` runs two separate commits: first `_reserve_daily_notional` commits the atomic prior→SUPERSEDED_BY_RETRY + new attempt=(N+1) RESERVED transition (:1610-1615, transaction opens at :265 and commits at :344), then `_submit_once` opens a *separate* fenced_write and commits SUBMISSION_REQUESTED for attempt N+1 (:1617-1622, first fenced write at :1210-1226). A hard crash between the two commits leaves: (a) the ambiguous chain still at STATE_UNKNOWN with attempt_number=N, (b) attempt N's reservation transitioned to SUPERSEDED_BY_RETRY, (c) attempt (N+1)'s reservation at RESERVED, and (d) no event chain entry for attempt N+1. Recovery paths: `recover_stranded_submission` refuses because the chain is not at SUBMISSION_REQUESTED (:1679-1684); a fresh `retry_external_paper_order` fails at `load_active_attempt_reservation(client_order_id, attempt_number=N, ...)` which returns None (prior is SUPERSEDED_BY_RETRY) → raises ATTEMPT_RESERVATION_MISSING (:1600-1609). Immutability triggers (paper_books_schema.py:1434-1443) forbid manual mutation.
- **Failure scenario.**
  Operator retries an ambiguous order (attempt N=0 UNKNOWN). `_reserve_daily_notional` commits the supersede+new-reserve transaction; the process is killed before `_submit_once` can commit SUBMISSION_REQUESTED. On restart, `retry_external_paper_order` refuses (ATTEMPT_RESERVATION_MISSING); `recover_stranded_submission` refuses (RECOVERY_NOT_APPLICABLE); `reconcile_external_paper_order` runs `_reconcile_attempt_reservation` against event N whose active reservation is None and latest is SUPERSEDED_BY_RETRY — the target-state predicate in :400-404 raises RESERVATION_STATE_CONFLICT. Attempt (N+1)'s RESERVED row consumes budget under `sum_attempt_reservations_by_date` (excludes SUPERSEDED_BY_RETRY, includes RESERVED) forever without a recoverable code path.
- **Impact.** A single retry crash permanently reduces today's $150 UTC-day cap by the order's notional and blocks any further retry/reconciliation of the same intent without hand-editing the database (which the immutability triggers actively refuse).
- **Why existing tests do not prevent it.** No test injects a crash between `_reserve_daily_notional`'s commit and `_submit_once`'s SUBMISSION_REQUESTED commit; unit tests exercise the happy path and the ambiguous-response path but not this specific window.
- **Recommended correction.** Perform the supersede + new-reserve + SUBMISSION_REQUESTED writes inside a single fenced transaction (as `_submit_once` already does for the initial submission's reservation + event pair), or add an explicit orphan-reservation recovery entry point that walks stranded RESERVED rows whose attempt_number has no event.
- **Recommended regression tests.** Simulate crash after supersede+new-reserve commit but before SUBMISSION_REQUESTED commit (e.g. inject a `raise` immediately after `_reserve_daily_notional` returns). Assert some code path can advance the (N+1) reservation off RESERVED.
- **Finder confidence.** high

### F-040 [Medium / CONFIRMED] Safety pause / CRITICAL alert / critical reconciliation checks run before the order-scope lease and are never re-verified inside the write

- **Area / Dimension.** FINANCIAL-SAFETY
- **Affected workflow.** External-paper submission — safety guardrail
- **Affected files.** `src/trading_research/paper_books/external_broker.py`
- **Line ranges.** external_broker.py:585-607, 1319-1366
- **Observed behavior.**
  `submit_external_paper_order` calls `_safety_checks(conn, book_id)` at :1327, then acquires the order-scope lease at :1329, then runs runtime calls (account_check, fingerprint history, baseline, validated preview) and only later commits the reservation and SUBMISSION_REQUESTED. `_safety_checks` is not re-invoked inside `_submit_once`'s fenced_write (external_broker.py:1188-1286). `retry_external_paper_order` is similar (:1576-1578 runs `_safety_checks` before the reservation transaction, no re-check thereafter).
- **Failure scenario.**
  Operator triggers `submit_external_paper_order`. Between `_safety_checks` returning at :1327 and the SUBMISSION_REQUESTED commit inside `_submit_once` (potentially seconds later, since intervening runtime calls for account_check/preview validation take real wall-clock time), another actor writes a CRITICAL shadow alert (`list_alerts(..., severity='CRITICAL', unresolved_only=True)`) or a shadow safety-pause becomes blocking. The submission proceeds to broker despite the freshly raised alert; the durable event chain protects against replay but not against a real-time safety block.
- **Impact.** The documented safety gate is a point-in-time check rather than a fencing check. Broker exposure can be created after the system is supposed to be paused.
- **Why existing tests do not prevent it.** Existing tests set the pause/alert state either before invocation (expect refusal) or not at all; no test flips the state mid-invocation between `_safety_checks` and the reservation commit.
- **Recommended correction.** Re-run `_safety_checks` (with the confirmed-NOT_FOUND allowance still applied for retry) inside `_submit_once`'s fenced_write, right before the SUBMISSION_REQUESTED event is appended and before `runtime.submit_limit_order` is called. Mirror the pattern the code already uses for `lease.verify_or_raise()`.
- **Recommended regression tests.** Simulate a CRITICAL alert insertion after `_safety_checks` returns but before `_reserve_daily_notional`. Assert the submission is refused.
- **Finder confidence.** medium
- **Verification.** 3 votes (3 confirmed, 0 plausible, 0 refuted).

  - _severity_: verdict=CONFIRMED, refuted=False - Confirmed by direct inspection. `_safety_checks` (external_broker.py:585-607) is a point-in-time DB read set. ### F-041 [Medium / CONFIRMED] AMBIGUOUS reservations do not block retries across UTC dates

- **Area / Dimension.** MODEL-TOKEN-COST
- **Affected workflow.** Research reuse / budget accounting
- **Affected files.** `src/trading_research/strategies/research_budget.py`
- **Line ranges.** 292-309
- **Observed behavior.**
  `reserve_research_tokens` scans for a prior AMBIGUOUS reservation with `row.get('budget_date') == budget_date.isoformat()` (research_budget.py:299) where `budget_date = now.astimezone(timezone.utc).date()` (research_budget.py:267). Any AMBIGUOUS row whose `budget_date` is not today is silently ignored. There is no separate cross-date gate: neither `_date_has_budget_breach` (215-221) nor `_live_tokens_reserved_for_date` (203-212) blocks activity based on unresolved-but-old AMBIGUOUS state.
- **Failure scenario.**
  At 2026-07-19 23:55 UTC, an attempt for `(research_run_id='run-abc', symbol='XYZ', provider='anthropic', model='claude-x', role='analyst', attempt=1)` invokes the controller. The provider call raises after transmission (timeout, EOF). `mark_research_tokens_ambiguous` records the row with `budget_date=2026-07-19`, `status=AMBIGUOUS` (research_budget.py:424-431). At 2026-07-20 00:05 UTC the orchestrator retries with `attempt=2` for the same run. `reserve_research_tokens` derives `budget_date=2026-07-20`, the AMBIGUOUS-prefix scan at 296-302 finds no row for today, and a fresh reservation is issued — a new provider call proceeds even though the prior attempt's usage was never reconciled and may or may not have actually consumed tokens at the provider.
- **Impact.** Violates the stated invariant that 'ambiguous attempts block retries across dates'. The daily cap is not double-charged (the prior AMBIGUOUS row is on yesterday's budget_date), but the operator-reconciliation gate exists precisely so a new call cannot proceed while the prior outcome is unknown. A user re-running around midnight UTC can silently amplify a provider incident into a second live call.
- **Recommended correction.** Drop the `budget_date == today` filter from the AMBIGUOUS-prefix scan at research_budget.py:299 (or scan the full history for AMBIGUOUS rows matching the run/symbol/provider/model prefix regardless of date). Add a unit test that a same-attempt-identity AMBIGUOUS row from a prior UTC day rejects a new-day reservation with `TOKEN_RESERVATION_RECONCILIATION_REQUIRED`.
- **Recommended regression tests.** tests/unit/test_research_budget.py appears to only exercise the same-day AMBIGUOUS path (Grep on tests would confirm).
- **Finder confidence.** high
- **Verification.** 3 votes (3 confirmed, 0 plausible, 0 refuted).
  - _reachability_: verdict=CONFIRMED, refuted=False - Verified against source. research_budget.py:267 derives budget_date from now; research_budget.py:296-302 restricts the AMBIGUOUS-prefix scan to rows whose budget_date matches today. mark_research_tokens_ambiguous (research_budget.py:424-431) does not shift the row's budget_date. The idempotency key includes utc_date (research_budget.py:158-166), so the same attempt identity on the next UTC day pro
  - _severity_: verdict=CONFIRMED, refuted=False - Confirmed by reading src/trading_research/strategies/research_budget.py directly.   - _code-refute_: verdict=CONFIRMED, refuted=False - Re-read research_budget.py directly. The AMBIGUOUS-prefix scan at lines 296-302 filters by row.get('budget_date') == budget_date.isoformat() (line 299), and budget_date is derived from the current clock via now.astimezone(timezone.utc).date() at line 267. mark_research_tokens_ambiguous (lines 424-431) does not rewrite budget_date, so an AMBIGUOUS row created on day-1 keeps budget_date=day-1. - **Area / Dimension.** MODEL-TOKEN-COST
- **Affected workflow.** Pre-flight shortlist gate

  `decide_research_action` (research_budget.py:62-90) is documented as the pre-flight gate that enforces `maximum_symbols_to_research_per_day`, `maximum_fresh_research_cycles_per_day`, and `daily_token_budget`, driven by a caller-supplied `DailyResearchBudgetState`.   Configure `strategy_candidate_selection.maximum_symbols_to_research_per_day=5` in config/strategies.yaml. There is no companion persistent counter — `DailyResearchBudgetState` is an in-memory dataclass, not a persistent aggregation over `shadow_budget_reservations` or `research_cycle_symbol_results`.

- **Recommended correction.** Either delete `decide_research_action`/`record_research_outcome`/`DailyResearchBudgetState` and their config knobs, or wire them into `run_scheduled_research_cycle` with a persistent counter derived from `research_cycles`/`shadow_budget_reservations` so two overlapping schedulers cannot each independently observe headroom.
- **Recommended regression tests.** tests/unit/test_research_budget.py is the sole caller; no integration test asserts that exceeding maximum_symbols_to_research_per_day skips symbols in the scheduled cycle.

### F-043 [Medium / CONFIRMED] Strategy shortlist + candidate-selection subsystem (research_budget.decide_research_action, selector.select_shortlist, momentum/mean-reversion/event-catalyst scanners) has no production caller

- **Area / Dimension.** OPERATIONAL-WIRING
- **Affected workflow.** scheduled research CLI -> shortlist gate (never happens)

  Grep for `select_shortlist(` under `src/` returns only the definition; the constructors `MomentumBreakoutStrategy(`, `MeanReversionStrategy(`, `EventDrivenCatalystStrategy(` are called by no non-test module under `src/`. Cost/latency guardrails that the design intended before every fresh research cycle do not run. Combined with OPERATIONAL-WIRING-01 the *only* daily-scoped cap standing between an operator and unlimited provider spend is the per-cycle ShadowResearchAttemptController budgets.

### F-044 [Medium / CONFIRMED] Approval-phrase invariant is documented but no CLI or service enforces it; `--reason` accepts any 1..512-char string

- **Area / Dimension.** OPERATIONAL-WIRING
- **Affected workflow.** external-paper-submit CLI -> submit_external_paper_order
- **Affected files.** `src/trading_research/cli.py`, `src/trading_research/paper_books/external_broker.py`

  `external_paper_submit_cli` (cli.py:361-372) and `submit_external_paper_order` (external_broker.py:1319-1367) accept `reason: str` and pass it through `_bounded(reason, "reason", 512)` (external_broker.py:115-119, called at 1324/1551/1717/1776) which only rejects empty/oversized values. Grep for `Approved,`, `approval_phrase`, `_APPROVAL_PHRASE`, `_validate_approval` across `src/` returns no matches. The invariant audit already labeled the paper approval phrase as `documented-not-enforced` (see INVARIANT AUDIT DIGEST).
- **Failure scenario.**
  Operator runs `external-paper-submit --book-id b --intent-id i --preview-id p --operator alice --reason "lgtm"`. The submission proceeds; no policy check compares the reason against the documented required phrase `"Approved, submit this exact paper order."`. An accidental automated caller passing a boilerplate reason is indistinguishable from a deliberate human approval.
- **Impact.** The single ceremonial gate that documentation claims separates a script from an intentional human submission is not a real gate; a compromised script or automation harness that reaches the CLI can submit external-paper orders with any string. Because everything else in the external-paper path is `fail-closed`, this is the one place that fails open on operator identity semantics.
- **Recommended correction.** Add a strict phrase match in `submit_external_paper_order` (and any live-execution stub) that rejects submissions unless `reason` equals the exact repository-defined phrase for the operation; keep the free-text field separate. Cover with unit tests.

### F-045 [Medium / CONFIRMED] RUN_STATUS_FAILED constant is declared but never written; a crash between save_run_started and any terminal mark_run_finished leaves a research_committee_run stuck at status='RUNNING' forever

- **Area / Dimension.** OPERATIONAL-WIRING
- **Affected workflow.** analyze_with_research_committee crash-restart
- **Affected files.** `src/trading_research/research/orchestration.py`, `src/trading_research/storage/research_repositories.py`
- **Line ranges.** orchestration.py:60, 691-924 (mark_run_finished calls at 777, 865, 881, 905, 917); research_repositories.py:152-277
- **Observed behavior.**
  `RUN_STATUS_FAILED = "FAILED"` is defined at orchestration.py:60 but every terminal branch in `analyze_with_research_committee` writes either RUN_STATUS_ANALYSIS_INCOMPLETE (:777,:865,:905), RUN_STATUS_ANALYST_REPORTS_COMPLETE_NO_MANAGER (:881), or the value in `final_status` derived from the decision (:917) -- never FAILED. The reuse gate at :740-762 short-circuits only on {COMPLETED, ANALYSIS_INCOMPLETE, ANALYST_REPORTS_COMPLETE_NO_MANAGER}; RUNNING and FAILED are not handled. There is no janitor that transitions RUNNING -> FAILED, and no `try/except` in `analyze_with_research_committee` writes FAILED on unexpected exception.
- **Failure scenario.**
  Provider process crashes (or the OS kills the CLI) after `save_run_started` (orchestration.py:769-773) has committed but before `mark_run_finished` runs. On restart the same deterministic `research_run_id` re-enters the orchestrator; `get_run_status` returns `RUNNING`, which the reuse gate ignores. The orchestrator falls through to `save_run_started` again (INSERT OR IGNORE -- no-op) and then starts running attempts. The result is a permanently `RUNNING` row that operators cannot distinguish from a genuine in-flight run.
- **Impact.** Operator dashboards that filter on `research_committee_runs.status IN ('COMPLETED','ANALYSIS_INCOMPLETE',...)` silently drop crashed runs; the reuse gate never bridges them; recomputed `research_run_id`s can duplicate attempts (save_attempt uses plain INSERT, research_repositories.py:187) and raise IntegrityError. The `FAILED` constant is a design placeholder, not a working state.
- **Recommended correction.** Wrap `analyze_with_research_committee` after `save_run_started` in a try/except that on exit-without-mark_run_finished calls `mark_run_finished(RUN_STATUS_FAILED, ...)`. Extend the reuse gate to treat RUNNING as a soft-recoverable state (or explicit resume path) and FAILED as terminal-restartable per policy.
- **Capability blocked.** crashed-run bookkeeping and idempotent resume
- **Finder confidence.** high
- **Verification.** 3 votes (3 confirmed, 0 plausible, 0 refuted).

  - _code-refute_: verdict=CONFIRMED, refuted=False - Verified against source. orchestration.py:60 defines RUN_STATUS_FAILED = "FAILED". Every mark_run_finished call site in analyze_with_research_committee (lines 777, 865, 881, 905, 917) writes ANALYSIS_INCOMPLETE, ANALYST_REPORTS_COMPLETE_NO_MANAGER, or a value derived from decision.rating (COMPLETED or ANALYSIS_INCOMPLETE) — never FAILED. Grep across the repo confirms RUN_STATUS_FAILED is only refe
  - _reachability_: verdict=CONFIRMED, refuted=False - Verified against code: RUN_STATUS_FAILED is defined at orchestration.py:60 but grep confirms it is never written by any code path — every mark_run_finished call at lines 777, 865, 881, 905, 917 uses ANALYSIS_INCOMPLETE, ANALYST_REPORTS_COMPLETE_NO_MANAGER, COMPLETED, or a decision-derived final_status. The reuse gate at 740-762 only handles those three terminal statuses, so RUNNING falls through. 

### F-046 [Medium / CONFIRMED] PaperExecutionEligibilityPolicy is always constructed with the default `_null_portfolio_guardrail` in every production call site; the injection point exists only for tests

Not a live-trading risk today (live_gateway is disabled) but a real gap the moment paper execution is expected to respect account-scoped limits.
- **Recommended correction.** Either delete the unused parameter to remove the false-safety signal, or wire a real portfolio guardrail into `cli.py:720` (and the currently-orphaned `submit_credentialed_paper_order` if it is revived).
