### F-047 [Medium / CONFIRMED] Backtest input identity omits economic_events; changing the events feed but keeping config+signals silently returns stale persisted results

- **Severity note.** Original finder severity: High; adjusted after verification to Medium.
- **Area / Dimension.** POINT-IN-TIME
- **Affected workflow.** Historical Backtesting persistence & replay identity
- **Affected files.** `src/trading_research/backtesting/engine.py`
- **Line ranges.** 123-138, 402-441
- **Observed behavior.**
  `run_backtest` composes `input_hash = hash_config({configuration_hash, signal_set_hash, code_version})` (engine.py:135-138) but does NOT include the caller-supplied `economic_events` tuple (engine.py:125). The blackout gate at engine.py:291-298 consumes those events to reject entries. `_persist_result` (:416-429) detects `existing is not None` and treats it as an idempotent replay of the identical run, returning without writing new daily_states/fills/metrics.
- **Failure scenario.**
  1) Call `run_backtest(configuration=cfg, data_provider=dp, signals=S, economic_events=())` — no blackout, entries fill, row persisted with `input_hash=H`. 2) Call again with the same `cfg`/`S` but `economic_events=(fomc_event_on_entry_day,)` — the engine now rejects those entries via `ECONOMIC_EVENT_BLACKOUT` and the in-memory `BacktestResult` has different fills. But `input_hash` recomputes to the same `H`; `_persist_result` at :428-429 sees the existing row and returns without persisting. Downstream analyses that read `backtest_daily_states`/`backtest_fills` see the first (stale) result, not the just-computed rejected-entry result.
- **Impact.** Two economically distinct backtests collapse onto one persisted row. Callers who trust the DB to reflect the latest `run_backtest` invocation get silently stale data. This is exactly the invariant `_signal_set_hash` (:46-64) was introduced to protect against for signals, but was not extended to the events input.
- **Why existing tests do not prevent it.** Unit tests exercise `_persist_result` with a fixed events tuple; there is no test that re-runs the same config+signals with different events and asserts DB inequality.
- **Recommended correction.** Include a deterministic content hash of `economic_events` (sorted by event_id, sub-fields hashed via `hash_config`) in `input_hash`, mirroring the pattern of `_signal_set_hash`.
- **Recommended regression tests.** Add a test that runs the same (cfg, signals) with two different `economic_events` tuples against a shared conn and asserts that the second call raises the identity-collision `BacktestError` or produces a distinct `backtest_run_id`.
- **Finder confidence.** high
- **Verification.** 3 votes (2 confirmed, 1 plausible, 0 refuted).
  - _severity_: verdict=CONFIRMED, refuted=False - Confirmed the mechanism. engine.py:135-138 builds `input_hash` from only `{configuration_hash, signal_set_hash, code_version}`, and `_configuration_hash` at :67-92 only captures the blackout policy shape, not the contents of the caller-supplied `economic_events` tuple (parameter at :125). Those events are consumed at :291-298 where `evaluate_economic_event_blackout` can reject BUY entries with `EC
  - _reachability_: verdict=PLAUSIBLE, refuted=False - The described defect is real at the code level: engine.py:125 accepts `economic_events`, engine.py:291-298 consumes it to reject entries, but the `input_hash` at engine.py:135-138 excludes it, and `_persist_result` at engine.py:428-429 short-circuits on the existing row — so two logically distinct runs that differ only in `economic_events` would indeed collapse onto one persisted `backtest_runs` r
  - _code-refute_: verdict=CONFIRMED, refuted=False - Re-reading engine.py confirms every element of the finding. Line 125: economic_events is a run_backtest parameter. Lines 135-138: input_hash includes only configuration_hash + signal_set_hash + code_version — economic_events is absent. Lines 67-92: _configuration_hash hashes only the blackout *config* (enabled/window/importance/markets/categories), not the event list. Lines 46-64: _signal_set_hash

### F-048 [Medium / CONFIRMED] Backtest entry-blackout uses a hardcoded 14:30 UTC entry-as-of, which is one hour AFTER market open during EDT — DST-agnostic

- **Area / Dimension.** POINT-IN-TIME
- **Affected workflow.** Historical Backtesting economic-event blackout
- **Affected files.** `src/trading_research/backtesting/engine.py`
- **Line ranges.** 291-298
- **Observed behavior.**
  `entry_as_of = datetime.combine(current_date, time(14, 30), tzinfo=timezone.utc)` (engine.py:291). 14:30 UTC equals 09:30 America/New_York during EST but 10:30 America/New_York during EDT (roughly mid-March through early November). `MARKET_OPEN_TIME = time(9,30)` in local ET (market_calendar.py:21). The blackout window is `event.scheduled_at - before_minutes ... event.scheduled_at + after_minutes` in absolute UTC (economic_calendar.py:173-176), so a fixed 14:30 UTC anchor mis-aligns with the true market-open moment by one hour for ~8 months a year.
- **Failure scenario.**
  An economic event with `scheduled_at = 2024-06-12T14:00Z` (10:00 ET during EDT, a common CPI release timestamp), `before_minutes=30`, `after_minutes=30` produces the blackout window [13:30Z, 14:30Z]. The engine computes `entry_as_of = 2024-06-12T14:30Z` and evaluates `13:30 <= 14:30 <= 14:30 -> True`, blocking entry. The real market-open moment on that date is 2024-06-12T13:30Z (09:30 EDT), which is at the START of the blackout window; conversely, an event at 13:15Z with the same window (12:45Z-13:45Z) would test 14:30Z (out of window) and allow entry — but real market open at 13:30Z is inside the window and the entry should have been blocked. Both directions of error exist depending on event time.
- **Impact.** Blackout decisions for backtests spanning EDT months are systematically anchored one hour late, producing false-positive AND false-negative blocks around any event within [market_open, market_open+1h] or [market_open-1h, market_open]. Undermines the deterministic guarantee stated in the module docstring that the engine models market-open entry.
- **Why existing tests do not prevent it.** Existing blackout tests likely fix a UTC clock, and none of them straddle the ~13:30Z vs 14:30Z boundary; no test asserts equality between the backtest engine's entry-anchor and `regular_session_close` conventions for both EDT and EST dates.
- **Recommended correction.** Replace with `regular_session_close(current_date)`-style logic anchored at true market open in America/New_York: e.g., `datetime.combine(current_date, MARKET_OPEN_TIME, ZoneInfo(MARKET_TIMEZONE_NAME)).astimezone(timezone.utc)`. Add unit tests around DST transitions.
- **Recommended regression tests.** Test a backtest with `current_date=2024-06-12` (EDT) and an event at 13:45Z with a 30-minute window; assert the entry is blocked.
- **Finder confidence.** high
- **Verification.** 3 votes (3 confirmed, 0 plausible, 0 refuted).
  - _reachability_: verdict=CONFIRMED, refuted=False - The 14:30 UTC hardcode at engine.py:291 is executed unconditionally on every eligible-signal iteration inside run_backtest. Its effect on the blackout decision is gated only by BacktestConfiguration.economic_event_blackout.enabled — a public, user-settable dataclass field with no additional flag or environment guard. The blackout comparison itself (economic_calendar.py:173-175) is done in absolute
  - _severity_: verdict=CONFIRMED, refuted=False - Verified the cited code:  - src/trading_research/backtesting/engine.py:291 hardcodes `entry_as_of = datetime.combine(current_date, time(14, 30), tzinfo=timezone.utc)`. This is passed directly as `as_of` to `evaluate_economic_event_blackout` (engine.py:292-295).  - src/trading_research/evidence_providers/economic_calendar.py:173-176 builds the blackout window as `[event.scheduled_at - before_minute
  - _code-refute_: verdict=CONFIRMED, refuted=False - Confirmed against source. engine.py:291 hardcodes `time(14, 30)` in UTC as the entry-as-of anchor for the blackout check. The canonical market open is defined in `evaluation/market_calendar.py:20-21` as `MARKET_OPEN_TIME = time(9, 30)` in `America/New_York`, which equals 13:30 UTC during EDT and 14:30 UTC during EST. The blackout evaluator (`economic_calendar.py:173-176`) builds the window `[sched

### F-049 [Medium / CONFIRMED] Concentration/allocation caps sized against a stale-marked portfolio

- **Area / Dimension.** PORTFOLIO-CONCENTRATION
- **Affected files.** `src/trading_research/paper_books/risk.py`, `src/trading_research/paper_books/valuation.py`
- **Line ranges.** risk.py:139-144,156-160; valuation.py:206-241
- **Observed behavior.**
  evaluate_paper_risk (risk.py:139-144) accepts context.valuation_status in (VALUATION_COMPLETE, VALUATION_PARTIAL_STALE_PRICE) and then computes max_position_notional = context.net_liquidation_value_usd * risk_config.max_position_weight (risk.py:156) and max_symbol_notional = ... * risk_config.max_symbol_concentration_weight (risk.py:159). valuation.build_portfolio_snapshot (valuation.py:206-241) still adds a stale-priced position's market_value to gross_market_value (line 217) — the only positions excluded are VALUATION_POINT_IN_TIME_UNSAFE and VALUATION_SOURCE_UNAVAILABLE (lines 208-213); a stale price merely increments stale_count and sets the overall status to VALUATION_PARTIAL_STALE_PRICE (line 238-239). Because net_liq = cash_available + cash_reserved + gross_mv (line 245) uses those stale marks, both the 10% max_position_weight and the 10% max_symbol_concentration_weight caps are computed off potentially-stale equity.
- **Failure scenario.**
  A book holds a $150,000 legacy position in AAPL whose last available bar is 30 days old (valuation.select_valuation_price at valuation.py:117-119 marks it VALUATION_PARTIAL_STALE_PRICE when staleness > maximum_price_age_seconds=900). The stale close is $180, but the true market has moved to $110 — the true position market value is ~$91,700, roughly 30% of true equity. The snapshot still reports gross_mv using $180 and net_liq around $250,000. evaluate_paper_risk then computes max_position_notional = $25,000 (10% of stale net_liq) and applies that as the allowable capacity for a new BUY on the same or a different name, when the true 10% cap would be much lower. remaining_position_capacity is likewise computed off the stale current_position_market_value_usd (from build_portfolio_context risk.py:52-58 which just reads snap_positions.market_value_usd), so a stale down-mark can also under-report existing concentration and let a new BUY through the max_symbol_concentration cap.
- **Impact.** Portfolio-concentration limits (max_position_weight, max_symbol_concentration_weight, minimum_cash_buffer_weight, and the max_position_weight ceiling threaded into compute_atr_position_plan at scheduled_integration.py:305) are not sized off a fresh mark. In a fast market the caps can be materially wrong; the ATR sizing gate in scheduled_integration.py demands a same-session bar (scheduled_integration.py:125) but the risk gate silently tolerates a stale mark for every OTHER position in the book. The two staleness policies are asymmetric.
- **Why existing tests do not prevent it.** Existing tests build PaperPortfolioContext directly with valuation_status=VALUATION_COMPLETE (tests/unit/test_paper_books_risk_and_intent.py:55) and never exercise the accepted stale-portfolio path.
- **Recommended correction.** Either (a) reject risk sizing when valuation_status == VALUATION_PARTIAL_STALE_PRICE (require VALUATION_COMPLETE), matching the ATR path's insistence on a same-session bar, or (b) parametrize the acceptable staleness explicitly via risk_config (independent of valuation_status). If (a), thread the choice through cli_support.paper_book_run_cycle_cli's shim so fixture-mode does not regress.
- **Recommended regression tests.** Add tests/unit/test_paper_books_risk_and_intent.py case: PaperPortfolioContext built from a snapshot with valuation_status=VALUATION_PARTIAL_STALE_PRICE and a stale mark inflating net_liq to twice its fresh value must reject with a stale-portfolio reason code, not merely price-side stale.
- **Finder confidence.** medium
- **Verification.** 3 votes (2 confirmed, 1 plausible, 0 refuted).
  - _code-refute_: verdict=CONFIRMED, refuted=False - The finding's factual claims are faithful to the current code.  1) risk.py:139-144 — evaluate_paper_risk explicitly accepts context.valuation_status in {VALUATION_COMPLETE, VALUATION_PARTIAL_STALE_PRICE}: `if context.valuation_status not in (VALUATION_COMPLETE, VALUATION_PARTIAL_STALE_PRICE) or context.net_liquidation_value_usd is None: return rejected(...)`. Confirmed verbatim.  2) risk.py:156-16
  - _reachability_: verdict=PLAUSIBLE, refuted=False - The described code paths exist as claimed: risk.py:139-144 admits VALUATION_PARTIAL_STALE_PRICE, valuation.py:215-217 folds stale-priced positions into gross_market_value, and the concentration caps at risk.py:156,159 are computed off potentially-stale net_liq. However, the primary production scheduled-integration entry point (scheduled_integration._process_arm) turns on the authoritative daily-ri
  - _severity_: verdict=CONFIRMED, refuted=False - Verified end-to-end against source. risk.py:139-144 accepts context.valuation_status in (VALUATION_COMPLETE, VALUATION_PARTIAL_STALE_PRICE) before computing max_position_notional (line 156), max_symbol_notional (line 159), and min_cash_buffer (line 164) off context.net_liquidation_value_usd. valuation.py:208-220 confirms that VALUATION_PARTIAL_STALE_PRICE positions still contribute quantity*select

### F-050 [Medium / CONFIRMED] GitHub Actions workflow has no `permissions:` block; every job runs with the repo's default GITHUB_TOKEN scope

- **Area / Dimension.** SECURITY-PRIVACY
- **Affected files.** `.github/workflows/ci.yml`
- **Line ranges.** .github/workflows/ci.yml:1-165
- **Observed behavior.**
  .github/workflows/ci.yml declares four jobs (main-tests, paper-runtime-tests, type-check, type-check-safety, migration-smoke) with no workflow-level or job-level `permissions:` key. The comment at lines 3-5 asserts "no credentials are configured anywhere in this workflow," but GITHUB_TOKEN is always injected and its default scope depends on the repository/org 'Workflow permissions' setting.
- **Failure scenario.**
  If a supply-chain compromise in `actions/checkout@v4`, `actions/setup-python@v5`, or any transitive pip-installed package (main-tests runs `pip install -e ".[dev]"` at line 22) executes arbitrary code during a PR/push, GITHUB_TOKEN is available to that step. When the repo's default workflow permission is "Read and write" (the pre-2023 default that is still common on repos migrated in), the attacker could push commits, publish releases, mint deployments, or comment on issues. An explicit top-level `permissions: contents: read` would limit blast radius to reads.
- **Impact.** Blast radius of any CI supply-chain compromise is unnecessarily large. Cheap, well-known mitigation is absent even though `docs/milestones/review.md` treats CI-integrity as a first-class concern.
- **Recommended correction.** Add `permissions: contents: read` at the workflow top level (and elevate per-job for the rare job that needs write access, of which there is none in ci.yml today).
- **Finder confidence.** high
- **Verification.** 3 votes (2 confirmed, 1 plausible, 0 refuted).
  - _severity_: verdict=CONFIRMED, refuted=False - Verified against .github/workflows/ci.yml:1-165 (the only workflow in the repo per Glob of .github/workflows/*.yml). No workflow-level or job-level `permissions:` key exists on any of the five jobs (main-tests, paper-runtime-tests, type-check, type-check-safety, migration-smoke). The finding's factual claims are accurate: GITHUB_TOKEN is implicitly injected and its scope is governed by the repo/or
  - _code-refute_: verdict=CONFIRMED, refuted=False - Re-reading C:\workspace\other\ai_model\.github\workflows\ci.yml (only workflow file in the repo per Glob of .github/**/*.yml) confirms the observation is faithful to the code:  - The file has no top-level `permissions:` key (lines 1-11 contain only `name`, a comment, and `on:` triggers). - None of the five jobs (main-tests L13-24, paper-runtime-tests L26-39, type-check L41-68, type-check-safety L7
  - _reachability_: verdict=PLAUSIBLE, refuted=False - Confirmed by reading .github/workflows/ci.yml (only workflow in .github/workflows/): no workflow-level or job-level `permissions:` key anywhere in the 165-line file across all five jobs. Triggers on push to main and every pull_request (lines 7-10), so GITHUB_TOKEN with the repo's default scope is injected on every run — not gated by any flag or branch. ### F-052 [Medium / CONFIRMED] Momentum/mean-reversion do not fail closed on non-positive or inverted ATR-derived stops

- **Area / Dimension.** STRATEGY-CORRECTNESS
- **Affected files.** `src/trading_research/strategies/momentum_breakout.py`, `src/trading_research/strategies/mean_reversion.py`, `src/trading_research/strategies/event_catalyst.py`, `src/trading_research/strategies/contracts.py`
- **Line ranges.** momentum_breakout.py:129-136; mean_reversion.py:132-151; event_catalyst.py:233-238; contracts.py:88-100
- **Observed behavior.**
  event_catalyst.py:233-238 correctly guards the ATR-derived stop: `if stop <= 0 or stop >= entry: return INCOMPLETE(...)`. momentum_breakout.py:129-136 and mean_reversion.py:132-151 do neither — they build the ELIGIBLE StrategySignal directly. The StrategySignal `__post_init__` (contracts.py:88-100) then raises `StrategyContractError('...must have a positive initial_stop_reference')` or `'...initial_stop_reference must be below entry_reference'` if `stop <= 0` or `stop >= entry`. That exception propagates unhandled out of `evaluate()`.
- **Failure scenario.**
  A low-priced high-volatility ticker with `latest_close=1.00`, ATR(14)=0.60, and `atr_stop_multiple=2.0` (a typical value): `stop = round(1.00 - 2.0*0.60, 4) = -0.20`. `MomentumBreakoutStrategy.evaluate(...)` raises `StrategyContractError` from the frozen dataclass — the caller (e.g. `selector.select_shortlist`) never sees a signal for this symbol and the entire batch-per-symbol contract of 'always return a StrategySignal' is violated. Same for mean_reversion whenever ATR exceeds `latest_close / atr_stop_multiple`.
- **Impact.** Fails hard rather than failing closed. ### F-053 [Medium / CONFIRMED] FakeResearchRepository masks real SQLite integrity constraints exercised by the production repository

- **Severity note.** Original finder severity: High; adjusted after verification to Medium.
- **Area / Dimension.** TESTING-QUALITY
- **Affected files.** `tests/support/research_fixtures.py`, `tests/unit/test_research_orchestration.py`, `tests/unit/test_research_replay.py`, `src/trading_research/storage/research_repositories.py`
- **Line ranges.** tests/support/research_fixtures.py:17-60; src/trading_research/storage/research_repositories.py:185-219
- **Observed behavior.**
  Every orchestration test in tests/unit/test_research_orchestration.py (including test_resumption_after_interruption_skips_already_persisted_roles at :160-190) and every replay test in tests/unit/test_research_replay.py runs against FakeResearchRepository (research_fixtures.py:17-60). The fake's save_attempt is a plain list.append (research_fixtures.py:40-41) that never rejects duplicate attempt_ids, and save_run_started uses dict.setdefault (:34-38) so a re-registration with different (snapshot_id, provider, model_name) is silently ignored. The production SQLiteResearchRepository.save_attempt uses a plain 'INSERT INTO research_attempts' (research_repositories.py:185-219) with no OR IGNORE, and the research_attempts table has an append-only trigger (research_schema.py) so the same attempt_id inserted twice raises sqlite3.IntegrityError. No unit test in tests/unit/ ever exercises SQLiteResearchRepository against a live schema for the resumption / duplicate-attempt path.
- **Failure scenario.**
  A crashed committee run leaves status RUNNING with attempt 'run-XYZ-fundamental-1' persisted. Operator re-invokes analyze_with_research_committee for the same research_run_id. The reuse gate at orchestration.py:740-762 does not short-circuit RUNNING, so orchestration.py:790-839 re-enters _run_role_with_retries; a resumed provider call reaches SQLiteResearchRepository.save_attempt (:807-811) with the deterministic attempt_id already present -> raises sqlite3.IntegrityError inside analyze_with_research_committee. Tests using FakeResearchRepository never see this because save_attempt is list.append; they silently pass while production would crash.
- **Impact.** The primary orchestrator test surface does not detect a real, reachable IntegrityError in the resume path (workflow trace 1 finding_lead 1), and the test suite gives the misleading impression that resumption is idempotent.
- **Why existing tests do not prevent it.** The fake repository is engineered around dict/list containers, so any state that would violate a SQL UNIQUE constraint or an append-only trigger is silently absorbed rather than propagated.
- **Recommended correction.** Add unit tests that run analyze_with_research_committee twice against SQLiteResearchRepository (with a temp sqlite DB) for the same research_run_id whose status is still RUNNING (a role's report absent, an attempt row present); assert no IntegrityError leaks. Alternatively make FakeResearchRepository enforce (attempt_id) uniqueness and (research_run_id, snapshot_id, provider, model_name) identity matching so the fake fails closed the way SQLite does.
- **Recommended regression tests.** tests/unit/test_research_orchestration.py::test_sqlite_repository_resume_of_running_run_does_not_raise_on_duplicate_attempt; parametrized copy of the existing resume test against SQLiteResearchRepository.
- **Capability blocked.** Resume-in-flight orchestration correctness cannot be validated at unit level.
- **Finder confidence.** high
- **Verification.** 3 votes (3 confirmed, 0 plausible, 0 refuted).
  - _code-refute_: verdict=CONFIRMED, refuted=False - The finding accurately describes the code. Verified: (1) FakeResearchRepository.save_attempt at tests/support/research_fixtures.py:40-41 is a plain list.append with no attempt_id uniqueness; save_run_started at :34-38 uses dict.setdefault. (2) SQLiteResearchRepository.save_attempt at src/trading_research/storage/research_repositories.py:187-219 uses "INSERT INTO research_attempts" (not INSERT OR I
  - _severity_: verdict=CONFIRMED, refuted=False - Verified against cited files. The
  - _reachability_: verdict=CONFIRMED, refuted=False - Verified against source: FakeResearchRepository.save_attempt is a plain list.append (research_fixtures.py:40-41) with no uniqueness check, while SQLiteResearchRepository.save_attempt uses plain INSERT into a table whose attempt_id is PRIMARY KEY (research_schema.py:51-52) — so duplicates raise sqlite3.IntegrityError. _attempt_id (orchestration.py:245-252) is deterministic for the non-scheduled pat

### F-055 [Medium / CONFIRMED] sync_paper_orders tests use a single connection and never cover crash-between-commits

- **Area / Dimension.** TESTING-QUALITY
- **Affected files.** `tests/unit/test_sync_paper_orders.py`, `src/trading_research/services/sync_paper_orders.py`, `src/trading_research/execution/ledger_events.py`
- **Line ranges.** tests/unit/test_sync_paper_orders.py:29-49 and 76-177; src/trading_research/services/sync_paper_orders.py; src/trading_research/execution/ledger_events.py:26-69
- **Observed behavior.**
  The whole file test_sync_paper_orders.py shares a single sqlite3.Connection through the 'conn' fixture (:29-33) and drives the full flow — submit + poll + apply — through that one connection. save_event, mark_event_ledger_applied and update_submission_status each commit independently (workflow trace 5 finding_lead 1). Every test in this file passes even though there is no bracketing transaction across those three writes. No test opens a second connection, no test simulates a crash after save_event/apply but before update_submission_status.
- **Failure scenario.**
  sync_paper_orders receives a FILLED status, save_event/apply_paper_execution_event commits the fill to the ledger, then the process dies before update_submission_status commits (sync_paper_orders.py commits each step separately). The paper_broker_submissions row still shows the prior non-terminal status, and no test proves that a subsequent poll self-heals cleanly.
- **Impact.** Even if the code is self-healing in practice, the test suite gives no guarantee, and a future change could introduce a persistent divergence between the ledger and the submission-status row without failing a test.
- **Why existing tests do not prevent it.** Single-connection tests inherit implicit ordering; they cannot observe a partial-commit sequence that a real crash-and-restart would expose.
- **Recommended correction.** Add a test that opens two real connections and monkeypatches update_submission_status to raise after the ledger write commits; on the next sync_paper_orders pass (fresh connection) assert the submission row eventually converges to the terminal status and the ledger position is not double-applied.
- **Recommended regression tests.** tests/unit/test_sync_paper_orders.py::test_crash_between_ledger_write_and_status_update_is_self_healing
- **Capability blocked.** Cross-transaction crash safety of the credentialed-paper polling path.
- **Finder confidence.** medium
- **Verification.** 3 votes (2 confirmed, 1 plausible, 0 refuted).

  - _code-refute_: verdict=CONFIRMED, refuted=False - Every factual claim in the finding is directly verifiable in the source. - **Observed behavior.**
  test_no_bid_ask_falls_back_to_deterministic_simulated_spread and test_unavailable_price_leaves_intent_pending_never_fabricates_fill (test_paper_books_scheduled_integration.py:506-543) verify that a BUY intent lands as OUTCOME_INTENT_CREATED_PENDING_FILL with no reservation, but no test then re-enters submit_and_simulate (execution.py:99-232) for that already-persisted PENDING_SUBMISSION intent. The workflow trace 4 finding_lead 1 flags that the 'existing is not None' branch (execution.py:117-168) will call remaining_buy_reservation(...) != intent.notional_usd — 0 vs positive — raising FillSimulationError 'existing reservation does not match the frozen intent notional'. That branch is un-tested end-to-end.

A later lifecycle iteration re-enters submit_and_simulate for the same intent with a market input available; execution.py:158-168 evaluates remaining_buy_reservation('BASELINE', 'p-1') == 0 while intent.notional_usd == 80 and raises FillSimulationError. ### F-057 [Medium / CONFIRMED] Shadow scheduler tests never assert paper_submissions/alert_count telemetry or per-attempt token accounting

### F-059 [Low / PLAUSIBLE] Three overlapping reservation subsystems with independent state machines and no shared abstraction

- **Severity note.** Original finder severity: Medium; adjusted after verification to Low.
- **Area / Dimension.** ARCHITECTURE
- **Affected workflow.** Cross-cutting (reservations)
- **Affected files.** `src/trading_research/paper_books/cash_ledger.py`, `src/trading_research/paper_books/external_broker.py`, `src/trading_research/strategies/research_budget.py`, `src/trading_research/storage/paper_books_schema.py`, `src/trading_research/storage/shadow_operations_schema.py`
- **Line ranges.** paper_books/external_broker.py:230-420 (attempt reservations); cash_ledger.reserve_for_order/release/settle usage at external_broker.py:1211-1220; research_budget.reserve_research_tokens/settle/release/mark_ambiguous at research_budget.py:235-431; storage/paper_books_schema.py:937-948 (paper_external_attempt_reservations)
- **Observed behavior.**
  Three distinct reservation systems live side by side: (a) `paper_external_attempt_reservations` (RESERVED -> SUBMITTED/RELEASED/RECONCILIATION_REQUIRED/SUPERSEDED_BY_RETRY) governing UTC-day notional caps; (b) `paper_book_cash_ledger` reservations governing book-scoped cash (reserve_for_order/release_reservation/settle_buy); (c) `shadow_budget_reservations` rows with `reservation_kind='RESEARCH_TOKENS'` (RESERVED -> SETTLED/RELEASED/AMBIGUOUS). Each has its own transitions, tables, idempotency-key convention, and helper module, with no shared `Reservation` abstraction. External_broker imports from cash_ledger AND owns its own attempt-reservation state machine directly; research_budget owns a third one.
- **Failure scenario.**
  A change to reservation semantics (e.g., adding a new SUSPENDED terminal state) has to be threaded through three code paths, three schemas, and three test suites. Absence of a shared abstraction makes it easy to leave one system out of sync — as illustrated by ARCHITECTURE-01 where the research-token reservation is not wired to production at all.
- **Impact.** Maintenance burden and drift risk. Reasoning about 'is this order's budget still held?' requires reading three modules. Ownership of state transitions is fragmented.

### F-060 [Low / PLAUSIBLE] Same-book concurrency race in the fresh-run reuse gate (`get_run_status` + `save_run_started`)

- **Area / Dimension.** ARCHITECTURE
- **Affected workflow.** Fresh research invocation
- **Affected files.** `src/trading_research/research/orchestration.py`, `src/trading_research/storage/research_repositories.py`
- **Line ranges.** research/orchestration.py:740-773; storage/research_repositories.py:152-183
- **Observed behavior.**
  `analyze_with_research_committee` computes a deterministic `research_run_id`, calls `research_repository.get_run_status(research_run_id)` (a plain SELECT via research_repositories.py:152-156), and only if it returns None does it call `save_run_started` (INSERT OR IGNORE with an immediate `self._conn.commit()`, :177-183). There is no BEGIN IMMEDIATE bracketing the read-then-insert. Two workers computing the same deterministic run_id can both observe status None and both proceed to full orchestration; INSERT OR IGNORE deduplicates the header row only, not the provider calls or per-attempt writes. There is no lease on `research_committee_runs`.
- **Failure scenario.**
  Two schedulers (e.g., a launchd overlap or an operator manually running `run-research` while `run-due-shadow-cycle` fires) target the same snapshot and produce the same `research_run_id`. Both call the provider; the second worker's per-attempt `save_attempt` uses plain INSERT (:187) so it may collide on attempt-id or double-count against a per-attempt token reservation.
- **Impact.** Duplicate paid provider calls and duplicate token-budget consumption on the same logical run. Ownership of 'exactly one worker per run_id' is unclear at the domain boundary.
- **Why existing tests do not prevent it.** Existing tests are single-threaded.
- **Recommended correction.** Wrap the get_run_status + save_run_started pair in BEGIN IMMEDIATE, or add a lease (mirroring `shadow_run_leases`) keyed on `research_run_id`. Also consider changing `save_attempt` to INSERT OR IGNORE so replay is idempotent.
- **Recommended regression tests.** Concurrency test: two threads simultaneously call `analyze_with_research_committee` with the same inputs; assert only one provider call is made.
- **Capability blocked.** Single-writer-per-run guarantee
- **Finder confidence.** low
- **Verification.** 3 votes (1 confirmed, 2 plausible, 0 refuted).

  - _severity_: verdict=PLAUSIBLE, refuted=False - Verified against source.   - _reachability_: verdict=PLAUSIBLE, refuted=False - Verified against code:  1. `research/orchestration.py:740-773` performs `get_run_status` (SELECT at `research_repositories.py:152-156`) then, only if None, calls `save_run_started` (INSERT OR IGNORE + commit at `research_repositories.py:177-183`) — no `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` bracket, no lease on `research_committee_runs`. ### F-062 [Low / CONFIRMED] Blackout evaluation timestamp fixed at 14:30 UTC ignores US DST

- **Area / Dimension.** BACKTEST-CORRECTNESS
- **Affected workflow.** Economic event blackout evaluation
- **Affected files.** `src/trading_research/backtesting/engine.py`

  `entry_as_of = datetime.combine(current_date, time(14, 30), tzinfo=timezone.utc)` hard-codes the entry moment. 14:30 UTC equals 09:30 America/New_York only during EST; during EDT (mid-March through early November) US market open is 13:30 UTC.
- **Failure scenario.**
  Economic event scheduled at 09:30 ET on 2026-06-15 (EDT) has UTC timestamp 13:30. A blackout window of 30 minutes covers 13:00-14:00 UTC. The engine evaluates blackout at 14:30 UTC (30 minutes after the window closed) and lets an entry through that would have been blocked at real market open. Symmetric error for events shortly after open causes false blocks in winter.
- **Impact.** Economic-event blackout inclusion/exclusion decisions off by up to an hour depending on the season; count of `economic_event_blocks` in metrics is distorted.
- **Recommended correction.** Compute `entry_as_of` from an America/New_York-aware 09:30 conversion (e.g. via `zoneinfo.ZoneInfo('America/New_York')`) or make it configurable per-run.
- **Recommended regression tests.** Feed a summer (EDT) session with a 13:30 UTC blackout event; assert entry_as_of falls inside the blackout window.
- **Finder confidence.** medium
- **Verification.** 3 votes (2 confirmed, 1 plausible, 0 refuted).

However, reachability in current production usage is highly restricted:  1. The engine's blackout evaluation (engine.
  - _code-refute_: verdict=CONFIRMED, refuted=False - Confirmed in code. engine.py:291 hard-codes `entry_as_of = datetime.combine(current_date, time(14, 30), tzinfo=timezone.utc)` with no DST-aware conversion (no ZoneInfo/America/New_York usage anywhere in the file). This value is passed as `as_of` to `evaluate_economic_event_blackout`, which compares it against `event.scheduled_at ± (before_minutes, after_minutes)` in real UTC time (economic_calenda
  - _severity_: verdict=CONFIRMED, refuted=False - Confirmed by reading engine.py:291 and economic_calendar.py:112-188. - **Severity note.** Original finder severity: High; adjusted after verification to Low.

- **Observed behavior.**
  The `main-tests` job installs only `.[dev]` (ci.yml:22) and never installs the `paper` optional extra (`lumibot==4.5.74`, pyproject.toml:35-37). Both LumiBot adapter unit tests unconditionally short-circuit via `pytest.importorskip('lumibot', reason='lumibot is an optional "paper" extra ...')` (test_lumibot_adapter.py:26 and test_lumibot_event_mapper.py). No CI job installs the extra, so these tests are permanently skipped in every push/PR. The paper_runtime job installs its own lumibot pin but exercises only paper_runtime/tests (ci.yml:34-39), not the trading_research LumiBot adapter under test.
- **Failure scenario.**
  A refactor in src/trading_research/runtime/lumibot/adapter.py's `_translate_intent` or event mapping (e.g., inverting BUY/SELL enum mapping, dropping LIMIT price, mismapping a broker status) merges cleanly because both `main-tests` and `type-check` pass green while the lumibot-guarded tests silently skip. The next operator using `pyproject.toml` extra `paper` for a manual paper-broker smoke would issue a wrong-side or wrong-price order.
- **Impact.** The only in-tree tests exercising the actual `lumibot.entities.order.Order` translation and status/event mapping never run automatically. Regressions in the paper-broker adapter would ship without any CI signal, contradicting the module's safety-critical role (paper_runtime/src/trading_paper_runtime/lumibot_gateway.py is the sole file constructing `lumibot.brokers.Alpaca`).
- **Why existing tests do not prevent it.** Both tests use `pytest.importorskip` at module top; the default CI env has no lumibot -> collection converts them to unconditional skips. There is no CI job that installs the `paper` extra of the main package.
- **Recommended correction.** Add a dedicated CI job (or matrix leg) that installs `.[dev,paper]` and runs `pytest tests/unit/test_lumibot_adapter.py tests/unit/test_lumibot_event_mapper.py` (or a `-m lumibot_adapter` selection) so the boundary translation is exercised on every PR. Fail loudly if `importorskip` skips there.
- **Recommended regression tests.** Add a CI assertion job that imports `lumibot` and asserts these two unit tests were collected AND ran (e.g., pytest --collect-only followed by a run producing >0 non-skipped test items).

### F-064 [Low / PLAUSIBLE] Migration smoke only exercises fresh + single reopen; legacy backfill paths are unverified

- **Severity note.** Original finder severity: Medium; adjusted after verification to Low.
- **Area / Dimension.** CI-OPS
- **Affected files.** `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `src/trading_research/storage/paper_books_schema.py`, `src/trading_research/storage/schema_version.py`
- **Line ranges.** .github/workflows/ci.yml:91-164; src/trading_research/storage/paper_books_schema.py:1459-1465, 1558-1691; src/trading_research/storage/schema_version.py:71-93
- **Observed behavior.**
  The `migration-smoke` job (ci.yml:101-164) only: (1) opens a fresh empty DB and checks a few tables/columns exist; (2) reopens that just-created DB once and asserts one added column. It never seeds a legacy-shaped DB (e.g., a paper_books DB without the `input_hash`, `scope_sequence`, `token_accounting_policy` columns; a `paper_external_attempt_reservations` table populated only via the pre-Milestone-24 shape) and reopens it. The legacy backfills - `_backfill_active_legacy_external_reservations` with its `LIMIT 100000` cap (workflow-digest finding), `_upgrade_triggers` versioned drop/recreate (paper_books_schema.py:1558-1691), and the Migration 2 backfill loop (schema_version.py:71-93) - are never exercised end-to-end in CI.
- **Failure scenario.**
  A future migration adds a NOT NULL column with no server-side default, or the trigger drop/recreate order silently changes semantics. A fresh-DB smoke passes; a real operator upgrading a database with existing rows encounters an IntegrityError or a trigger version mismatch. There is no CI job that would have caught it.
- **Impact.** Schema migrations are the CLAUDE.md-mandated 'idempotent additive DDL' path; a partial or incorrect legacy-DB upgrade can silently corrupt reservation state.
- **Why existing tests do not prevent it.** No fixture DB exists in-repo; the current smoke only builds a fresh DB via the very code it is trying to test.
- **Recommended correction.** Bundle a small legacy-shaped SQLite fixture (or programmatically build one at an earlier `CURRENT_SCHEMA_VERSION` snapshot) and add a CI step that copies it into place, opens it, and asserts every declared column/trigger is present after upgrade. Ideally, seed rows into `paper_external_attempt_reservations` and assert the backfill completed.
- **Recommended regression tests.** New pytest under tests/integration exercising the full upgrade path from a fixture DB.
- **Capability blocked.** Migration safety on operator upgrades

- **Verification.** 3 votes (1 confirmed, 1 plausible, 1 refuted).

### F-065 [Low / CONFIRMED] No permissions, timeout-minutes, or concurrency controls on the workflow

  The workflow declares no top-level or job-level `permissions:` (default GITHUB_TOKEN permissions apply, which for `pull_request` on public repos are read-only but for private repos default to write). No `timeout-minutes:` on any job (default is 360 minutes / 6 hours). No `concurrency:` group for PR runs.

  A pytest deadlock or hung subprocess (paper_runtime spawns a child process; a lock in shadow scheduler's BEGIN IMMEDIATE could plausibly hang) consumes a runner slot for 6 hours per push. A rapid-fire push to a PR spawns three concurrent runs. Later, when secrets are added to CI for smoke tests, the absence of an explicit `permissions: { contents: read }` block yields unnecessarily broad `GITHUB_TOKEN` scopes.
- **Impact.** Wastes CI minutes; small but real security posture concern once credentials enter the workflow.
- **Why existing tests do not prevent it.** Not a code surface.
- **Recommended correction.** Add `permissions: contents: read` at workflow scope, `timeout-minutes: 30` (or similar) per job, and `concurrency: { group: ${{ github.workflow }}-${{ github.ref }}, cancel-in-progress: true }` for PR builds.

- **Capability blocked.** CI cost / least-privilege posture

### F-066 [Low / CONFIRMED] pytest markers not enforced strict; typos silently un-gate opt-in real-provider tests

- **Area / Dimension.** CI-OPS
- **Affected files.** `pyproject.toml`, `tests/integration/test_external_paper_broker_smoke.py`, `tests/integration/test_paper_broker_smoke.py`
- **Line ranges.** pyproject.toml:42-59; tests/integration/test_external_paper_broker_smoke.py:16-23
- **Observed behavior.**
  `[tool.pytest.ini_options]` (pyproject.toml:42-59) registers custom markers but does not set `addopts = "--strict-markers"` nor set `strict-markers = true`. Skips are enforced by individual `pytest.mark.skipif(not _ENABLED, ...)` decorators inside each test file (e.g., test_external_paper_broker_smoke.py:23). If a future refactor mistypes the RUN_ env-var name inside a specific test file, or forgets the `skipif`, the marker itself provides zero protection.
- **Failure scenario.**
  A developer adds a new opt-in real-broker test decorated only with `pytest.mark.external_paper_brokr` (typo) and no `skipif`. Because markers are not strict, pytest emits only a warning and runs the test. In CI the test currently no-ops due to missing credentials, but on an operator machine with real credentials in the environment (e.g., they ran the paper-runtime child previously) it would actually hit the broker.
- **Impact.** The whole real-provider gating model relies on skipif conventions in every test file; marker registration is decorative. Any drift is silently permitted.
- **Why existing tests do not prevent it.** No existing test enforces the marker/skipif pairing, and pytest itself does not require registered markers unless strict mode is on.
- **Recommended correction.** Add `addopts = ["--strict-markers", "-ra"]` to `[tool.pytest.ini_options]` so unknown markers fail collection, and add a CI grep that requires every `@pytest.mark.(paper_broker|external_paper_broker|claude_api|...)` decorator to co-occur with a `RUN_..._TESTS` skipif in the same file.
- **Recommended regression tests.** Add a test that scans tests/integration/*.py, parses AST, and asserts each opt-in marker is paired with a skipif referencing the required env var.
- **Capability blocked.** Defense-in-depth against accidental live-provider calls
- **Finder confidence.** medium
- **Verification.** 3 votes (2 confirmed, 1 plausible, 0 refuted).

Pytest would therefore only emit PytestUnknownMarkWarning for a 
  - _code-refute_: verdict=CONFIRMED, refuted=False - Verified against pyproject.toml lines 42-59: markers are registered under [tool.pytest.ini_options] but there is no addopts entry, no --strict-markers flag, and no strict-markers = true. In tests/integration/test_external_paper_broker_smoke.py, pytestmark applies the marker (line 14) while the actual gate is a separate pytest.mark.skipif (line 23) computed from the RUN_EXTERNAL_PAPER_BROKER_TESTS

  `submit_external_paper_order` calls `_safety_checks` at external_broker.py:1327 *before* `_order_lease` is acquired at line 1329-1331. Between the safety check and the fenced writes at `_submit_once`/`_reserve_daily_notional`, several potentially blocking operations run (lease acquisition, `_account_check` runtime IPC at line 1332, `_verify_fingerprint_history`, `_require_reconciliation_baseline`, `_validated_preview`). None of them re-run `_safety_checks`. A critical operational alert raised, or shadow safety-pause activation, in that window will not stop this submission from writing SUBMISSION_REQUESTED and calling `runtime.submit_limit_order`. Milestone 11.3.1 Item 4 fenced_write only fences against lease loss and DB-level concurrent writers; it does not re-consult safety state.
- **Failure scenario.**
  Operator issues `external-paper-submit` at t=0. `_safety_checks` at t=0 sees no critical alert. Between t=0 and lease-acquired at t=800ms, a separate monitor process raises a CRITICAL alert (e.g. an ambient reconciliation just flipped critical). Submit proceeds anyway and places the order at the broker.
- **Impact.** Narrow but non-zero: a critical operational condition raised during the submit window is not honored by the in-flight submission, weakening the fail-closed guarantee of `_safety_checks`.
- **Recommended correction.** Re-run `_safety_checks` inside the lease immediately before `_reserve_daily_notional`/`_submit_once`, so any state change during the account-verification window blocks the write.

  - _code-refute_: verdict=CONFIRMED, refuted=False - Reading external_broker.py directly confirms the finding faithfully describes the code:  - `_safety_checks` (lines 585-607) evaluates three blocking conditions from live state: shadow pause (`pause_mod.current_state`), unresolved CRITICAL operational alerts (`list_alerts(..., severity="CRITICAL", unresolved_only=True)`), and latest critical external reconciliation per scope.   - _code-refute_: verdict=PLAUSIBLE, refuted=False - Code confirms the observed asymmetry: lines 83-86 require exact-tuple equality with ("BUY","SELL") and ("LIMIT",), while lines 155-156 do not normalize case, and lines 151-152 do `.strip().lower()` for mode/environment. The observation is faithful. However, this is a documented, intentional fail-closed check with a clear error message ("must be exactly [BUY, SELL]"), and the finding itself concede
  - _reachability_: verdict=CONFIRMED, refuted=False - The code at src/trading_research/runtime/paper_runtime_config.py:83-86 does perform exact-case tuple equality (`self.allowed_sides != ("BUY", "SELL")` and `self.allowed_order_types != ("LIMIT",)`) with no normalization at lines 155-156 (`tuple(pb['allowed_sides'])`, `tuple(pb['allowed_order_types'])`), while lines 151-152 apply `.strip().lower()` to `mode` and `environment`. ### F-070 [Low / CONFIRMED] Layer A `reconcile_paper_account_and_positions` writes each position row in its own autocommit — a crash mid-loop persists a partial account reconciliation

- **Area / Dimension.** CRASH-RECOVERY

Each `save_position_reconciliation` executes `INSERT` then `conn.commit()` (execution_repositories.py:395-406). There is no enclosing BEGIN IMMEDIATE.
- **Failure scenario.**
  A snapshot of 10 broker positions is taken; save_account_reconciliation and 4 of 10 save_position_reconciliation calls commit. Process crashes before positions 5-10 are written. Operator reads `list_latest_position_reconciliations` and sees an inconsistent state — 4 positions look reconciled for this timestamp while 6 have stale rows from a prior reconciliation. This can drive an operator to conclude reconciliation is MATCHED when it wasn't.
- **Impact.** Latest-reconciliation reports may present a partial snapshot as authoritative. Not corrupting, but potentially misleading during incident response.
- **Why existing tests do not prevent it.** Tests never crash mid-loop.
- **Recommended correction.** Wrap the account + all position saves in a single transaction (BEGIN IMMEDIATE) so the persisted snapshot is atomic.
- **Recommended regression tests.** Missing.
- **Capability blocked.** Atomic reconciliation snapshot

The CLI entry point (cli.py:526-537) op
  - _reachability_: verdict=CONFIRMED, refuted=False - Verified against source. `reconcile_paper_account_and_positions` (src/trading_research/services/reconcile_paper.py:30-62) calls `save_account_reconciliation` once at line 43, then loops at lines 59-60 calling `save_position_reconciliation` per broker position. ### F-071 [Low / PLAUSIBLE] Trigger versioning drops immutability triggers outside a transaction, leaving a window with no append-only enforcement

- **Severity note.** Original finder severity: Medium; adjusted after verification to Low.
- **Area / Dimension.** DB-MIGRATION
- **Affected files.** `src/trading_research/storage/paper_books_schema.py`, `src/trading_research/storage/database.py`
- **Line ranges.** src/trading_research/storage/paper_books_schema.py:1537-1563, 1676-1692; src/trading_research/storage/database.py:30-67
- **Observed behavior.**
  `_upgrade_triggers` runs `DROP TRIGGER IF EXISTS <name>` and an `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` on `paper_books_trigger_versions` (paper_books_schema.py:1558-1563) with the connection in autocommit (`isolation_level=None`, database.py:39). Each of those statements commits independently. The CREATE that re-establishes the trigger happens only after `_upgrade_triggers` returns, in a separate `conn.executescript(PAPER_BOOKS_TRIGGERS)` autocommit block (paper_books_schema.py:1690-1691). This window applies for every trigger whose version was bumped (e.g. `trg_paper_external_lookups_no_update` at v2).
- **Failure scenario.**
  A second connection opens the same SQLite database file after this connection has DROPped the trigger but before `PAPER_BOOKS_TRIGGERS.executescript` has recreated it. Between the DROP and the CREATE (or if the process crashes after DROP), another writer (or the same process re-entering) can issue an UPDATE/DELETE against `paper_external_order_lookups` that the immutability trigger would normally ABORT (paper_books_schema.py:1329-1364) — mutating consumed_by_retry_event_id semantics, payload_hash, or the ambiguous_event_id link that `retry_external_paper_order` relies on for its evidence match (external_broker.py:1561-1575).
- **Impact.** The audit-log immutability contract that gates the ambiguous-submission retry path is briefly enforceable-in-code-only during upgrade; concurrent connections or a mid-upgrade crash can leave the trigger dropped indefinitely, silently permitting mutation of otherwise immutable evidence.
- **Recommended correction.** Wrap the DROP, the paper_books_trigger_versions upsert, and the corresponding CREATE TRIGGER in a single `with transaction(conn):` block (or run all trigger recreation via `_upgrade_triggers` itself before returning), so no autocommit boundary exists between them.

Line 52 also enables WAL, which specifically permits a second connection to acquire the write lock while this connection is between autocommit 
  - _reachability_: verdict=PLAUSIBLE, refuted=False - Verified the described window against the actual code. The autocommit setup (database.py:39) and split between _upgrade_triggers (paper_books_schema.py:1537-1563) and the CREATE via executescript (line 1691) are confirmed — DROP TRIGGER commits immediately, then control returns before the recreate runs. The consume_external_lookup UPDATE (paper_books_repositories.py:1606) is the concrete mutation 
  - _severity_: verdict=PLAUSIBLE, refuted=False - The autocommit boundaries are real. `database.py:39` sets `isolation_level=None` (true autocommit), and `_upgrade_triggers` (paper_books_schema.py:1537-1563) issues the `DROP TRIGGER` and the version upsert as separate top-level statements — each commits independently. The recreate happens outside `_upgrade_triggers` via `conn.executescript(PAPER_BOOKS_TRIGGERS)` at paper_books_schema.py:1691, whi

### F-072 [Low / PLAUSIBLE] rename_legacy_research_recommendations runs twice per connect() with no version gate; a race between two processes on a legacy DB can raise "manual reconciliation required"

- **Area / Dimension.** DB-MIGRATION
- **Affected files.** `src/trading_research/storage/migrations.py`, `src/trading_research/storage/trading_schema.py`, `src/trading_research/storage/database.py`
- **Line ranges.** src/trading_research/storage/migrations.py:118-141; src/trading_research/storage/trading_schema.py:427-433; src/trading_research/storage/database.py:55-56
- **Observed behavior.**
  `rename_legacy_research_recommendations` is invoked from both `apply_schema` (migrations.py:139) and `apply_trading_schema` (trading_schema.py:428), each in autocommit outside any BEGIN IMMEDIATE. It raises `RuntimeError('both `recommendations` (legacy research shape) and `research_recommendations` exist — manual reconciliation required')` at migrations.py:129-133 whenever both names exist simultaneously.
- **Failure scenario.**
  Two processes open the same legacy database at almost the same instant. Process A completes the ALTER TABLE rename (migrations.py:134) then runs the trading DDL, which recreates `recommendations` (rec_id shape) at trading_schema.py:429. Between A's rename and A's trading-DDL run, process B calls `rename_legacy_research_recommendations` and now sees `recommendations` (rec_id shape) AND `research_recommendations` — the guard at :127 (`'rec_id' in cols`) makes it return early rather than raise, so this specific race is safe. However, if process A crashes after the rename but before creating the new `recommendations` table, process B's subsequent open sees `research_recommendations` present and legacy `recommendations` absent — correct fall-through, again safe. The concern is that this legacy rename has no schema_version entry, so a hand-restored DB that ends up with both tables (e.g., after a partial file restore) cannot be opened until a human deletes one.
- **Impact.** Recoverable manually, but the code path is un-versioned and can trip on realistic legacy states created by backup/restore.
- **Recommended correction.** Fold the rename into a numbered schema_version migration so it runs at most once per lifetime; call it only from one location, protected by the version gate.
- **Finder confidence.** low
- **Verification.** 3 votes (1 confirmed, 1 plausible, 1 refuted).
  - _code-refute_: verdict=CONFIRMED, refuted=False - Confirmed the mechanical facts against the cited files. migrations.py:118-141 defines rename_legacy_research_recommendations with the RuntimeError guard at :130-133 fired only when legacy `recommendations` (workstream_id shape, no rec_id) coexists with `research_recommendations`. It is invoked from apply_schema (:139) and again from apply_trading_schema (trading_schema.py:428), both called sequent
  - _severity_: verdict=PLAUSIBLE, refuted=False - Verified the cited code. The finding's own text concedes that every realistic race described is safe: the `rec_id in cols` guard at migrations.py:127 short-circuits when process A has already recreated the trading-shape `recommendations`, and the empty-cols check on the same line short-circuits between A's rename and A's CREATE. `check_schema_not_forward_versioned` runs before either rename call (
  - _reachability_: verdict=REFUTED, refuted=True - The titled failure path (two-process race raising 'manual reconciliation required') is not reachable. `connect()` sets `busy_timeout=5_000` and WAL (database.py:47,52), so a concurrent second process waits for the first process's ALTER TABLE to commit rather than observing an intermediate state. Once committed, only two DB states can be seen at migrations.py:126: either `recommendations` is absent

### F-077 [Low / CONFIRMED] `ORDER_MISSING_AT_BROKER` raise in submit path exits without persisting a critical reconciliation row

- **Affected workflow.** External-paper submission — reconciliation evidence

  When `submit_external_paper_order` re-enters against a chain already past PREVIEWED (:1343), it calls `runtime.get_order_by_client_order_id` and, on a None response, raises `ORDER_MISSING_AT_BROKER` (:1345-1347). Unlike `_reconcile_locked`, which is wrapped so any exception surface persists a `paper_external_reconciliations` critical row (`RECONCILIATION_INTERNAL_ERROR` at :1959-1987), this raise happens outside `_reconcile_locked` and does not itself write a critical reconciliation row for the observed missing-order condition.

Operator reruns `submit_external_paper_order`; the flow raises `ORDER_MISSING_AT_BROKER` without leaving audit evidence of the mismatch beyond the raised exception being caught upstream. The invariant `no ambiguous outcome exits without persisted critical evidence` (comment at :1963-1969) is upheld only via the reconciliation path — this raise is not gated on the caller invoking reconciliation afterward.
- **Impact.** Reduces auditability of a specific critical condition. Not a correctness bug for cap accounting (reservation was already advanced by the prior successful submit), but weakens the durable-evidence invariant the module otherwise enforces.
- **Why existing tests do not prevent it.** No test asserts a `paper_external_reconciliations` row is written when this particular raise fires.

- **Recommended regression tests.** State a chain at SUBMITTED, arrange the runtime to return None for the same client_order_id, invoke `submit_external_paper_order`, and assert both the raise and the presence of a critical reconciliation row.

  - _code-refute_: verdict=PLAUSIBLE, refuted=False - Observation reproduced from the cited lines. submit_external_paper_order at external_broker.py:1340-1348 does raise ExternalPaperError("ORDER_MISSING_AT_BROKER", ...) directly when runtime.get_order_by_client_order_id returns None on a re-entry past PREVIEWED, and this raise is outside _reconcile_locked's exception wrapper (:1970-1986), so it does not itself invoke _persist_reconciliation.   - _reachability_: verdict=CONFIRMED, refuted=False - The raise at external_broker.py:1347 is reachable in production. ### F-078 [Low / PLAUSIBLE] Anthropic and Claude Code providers report token_accounting_policy=NOT_APPLICABLE and reasoning_output_tokens=None; controller policy mismatches force AMBIGUOUS

- **Severity note.** Original finder severity: Medium; adjusted after verification to Low.
- **Area / Dimension.** MODEL-TOKEN-COST
- **Affected workflow.** Model-token-cost accounting
- **Affected files.** `src/trading_research/research/anthropic_provider.py`, `src/trading_research/research/claude_code_provider.py`, `src/trading_research/research/usage.py`, `src/trading_research/research/token_budget.py`, `src/trading_research/strategies/research_budget.py`
- **Line ranges.** anthropic_provider.py:176-229; claude_code_provider.py:483-501; usage.py:100, 120, 131, 142, 154, 167; token_budget.py:86-97; research_budget.py:378-410
- **Observed behavior.**
  AnthropicResearchProvider.build_usage_record is called at anthropic_provider.py:224 without `token_accounting_policy` or `reasoning_output_tokens`; usage.py:100 defaults `token_accounting_policy=TOKEN_ACCOUNTING_NOT_APPLICABLE`. ClaudeCodeResearchProvider.build_usage_record at claude_code_provider.py:483-501 likewise passes neither. Codex is the only real provider that sets `TOKEN_ACCOUNTING_REASONING_INCLUDED_IN_OUTPUT` (codex_provider.py:593). settle_research_tokens (research_budget.py:378-388) raises `TOKEN_ACCOUNTING_POLICY_MISMATCH` when `usage.token_accounting_policy != reservation policy`, and token_budget.py:94-96 catches the resulting `TokenBudgetError` and marks the reservation AMBIGUOUS.
- **Failure scenario.**
  A future operator wires `PersistentResearchTokenBudgetController(token_accounting_policy='REASONING_SEPARATE', ...)` (a plausible config for a reasoning-model deployment) with `provider='anthropic'`. `reserve_research_tokens` records the reservation with policy=REASONING_SEPARATE. AnthropicResearchProvider returns a usage record with policy=NOT_APPLICABLE. `settle_research_tokens` raises `TOKEN_ACCOUNTING_POLICY_MISMATCH`, `invoke` marks the reservation AMBIGUOUS, and the next attempt for the same run+symbol+provider+model is rejected with `TOKEN_RESERVATION_RECONCILIATION_REQUIRED` (research_budget.py:303-309). Every attempt for this deployment self-poisons.
- **Impact.** Provider-specific policy is not carried on the wire for Anthropic/Claude Code, so cross-provider deployments cannot enable reasoning-token accounting. In addition, Anthropic's reasoning tokens (if any — the Messages API is beginning to expose these) are never counted toward the daily cap because reasoning_output_tokens is never extracted at anthropic_provider.py:176-179.
- **Recommended correction.** Have AnthropicResearchProvider and ClaudeCodeResearchProvider set `token_accounting_policy` explicitly (typically REASONING_INCLUDED_IN_OUTPUT for models that hide reasoning inside output_tokens) and extract `reasoning_output_tokens` from the response body when present. Fail closed at controller construction if the controller policy is not one of the provider's supported policies rather than deferring the mismatch to settle time.
- **Recommended regression tests.** No test exercises the controller against AnthropicResearchProvider's real usage shape; the deterministic provider path (research_budget.py:378-388) accepts NOT_APPLICABLE and masks the mismatch.
- **Finder confidence.** medium
- **Verification.** 3 votes (1 confirmed, 1 plausible, 1 refuted).
  - _reachability_: verdict=REFUTED, refuted=True - The described self-poisoning failure path is not reachable at this commit. PersistentResearchTokenBudgetController (src/trading_research/research/token_budget.py:22) is the only implementation of the ResearchTokenBudgetController protocol, and a git-grep of src/** shows it is instantiated nowhere in production code — only in tests/unit/test_research_orchestration.py:401.   - _code-refute_: verdict=CONFIRMED, refuted=False - All observations are faithful to the code. anthropic_provider.py:224-229 and claude_code_provider.py:483-501 call build_usage_record without passing token_accounting_policy or reasoning_output_tokens, so usage.py:100's default TOKEN_ACCOUNTING_NOT_APPLICABLE is used and propagated through the return paths at 120/131/142/154/167. codex_provider.py:593 is confirmed as the only provider that sets a n
  - _severity_: verdict=PLAUSIBLE, refuted=False - The mechanics of the finding are confirmed by direct reading of the code:  1. ### F-079 [Low / CONFIRMED] list_budget_reservations scans full history on every reservation; three unfiltered scans per BEGIN IMMEDIATE

- **Area / Dimension.** MODEL-TOKEN-COST
- **Affected workflow.** Token reservation atomic transitions

None of these calls pushes a WHERE clause into SQL — the repository loads every row and Python filters in memory.
- **Failure scenario.**
  On a database with N=10^6 lifetime `shadow_budget_reservations` rows (a plausible steady state after months of scheduled research + shadow cost reservations), each fresh research attempt reads all N rows three times under an exclusive write lock. Concurrent shadow-scheduler writers see a `busy_timeout=5000ms` window; degradation is linear with lifetime table size.
- **Impact.** Correctness is preserved but write throughput degrades over time; the exclusive write lock during the scan can block other writers to `shadow_budget_reservations` (shadow role budget, shadow-scheduler cost reservations).
- **Recommended correction.** Add a filtered accessor (`list_budget_reservations_for(conn, *, reservation_kind, budget_date, statuses)`) that pushes `WHERE reservation_kind=? AND budget_date=? AND status IN (...)` into SQL; use it in all three call sites. An index on `(reservation_kind, budget_date, status)` may already exist — verify at storage/shadow_operations_schema.py.
- **Recommended regression tests.** No performance regression harness exercises the full-history scan; unit tests use tiny databases.

When called with no arguments, it runs `SELECT * FROM shadow_budget_reservations ORDER BY created_at DESC` — a full-table scan returned as Python dicts.  2. Inside `reserve_research_tokens` (research_bud

### F-080 [Low / CONFIRMED] _conservative_input_allowance uses str(dict) repr, not JSON serialization, to estimate schema byte length

- **Area / Dimension.** MODEL-TOKEN-COST
- **Affected workflow.** Model-token-cost accounting
- **Affected files.** `src/trading_research/research/token_budget.py`
- **Line ranges.** 31-37
- **Observed behavior.**
  `_conservative_input_allowance` computes `len(request.system_prompt.encode()) + len(request.user_prompt.encode()) + len(str(request.json_schema).encode())`. `str(dict)` returns Python's repr (`{'key': 'value'}`) not JSON — quote choice, key ordering, and non-ASCII escaping all diverge from what actually goes on the wire. The docstring calls this a 'conservative upper bound' but the reservation is charged in tokens estimated from Python repr bytes, not from the actual API-serialized request.
- **Failure scenario.**
  A schema property adds a Unicode default like `"currency": "€"`. Python `str(dict)` renders it as `€` (or the raw char under Py3+); JSON serialization would render it differently. The reservation byte count no longer matches the request the provider will actually see. Because bytes are 4x-ish oversized vs. tokens the estimate is still conservative on average, but the guarantee is behavioral rather than provable.
- **Impact.** Low — the estimate is generally conservative and Reservation cap breach is flagged only after settlement (breached=1 flag). No under-reservation risk observed for ASCII prompts.
- **Recommended correction.** Use `json.dumps(request.json_schema, sort_keys=True, separators=(',', ':')).encode()` (matching the on-wire canonical form) or a tokenizer-aware estimator when available. Add a comment explicitly documenting the token-per-byte ratio assumed.
- **Recommended regression tests.** No test asserts the estimate closely tracks the provider's actual accounting.
- **Finder confidence.** medium
- **Verification.** 3 votes (2 confirmed, 0 plausible, 1 refuted).
  - _reachability_: verdict=REFUTED, refuted=True - The `_conservative_input_allowance` implementation at src/trading_research/research/token_budget.py:31-37 does use `str(request.json_schema).encode()` as observed. However, tracing reachability: `request.json_schema` is only ever populated in orchestration.py:385 from `_schema_for_role` (orchestration.py:241-242), which returns either `decision_json_schema` or `role_report_json_schema` from output
  - _code-refute_: verdict=CONFIRMED, refuted=False - Verified against src/trading_research/research/token_budget.py lines 31-37. The code literally computes `len(str(request.json_schema).encode())` — Python repr of the dict, not JSON serialization. The docstring at line 33 characterizes this as a "conservative upper bound" via UTF-8 bytes as a proxy for tokens. The finding's factual claim is faithful to the code: the reservation is charged from repr
  - _severity_: verdict=CONFIRMED, refuted=False - Confirmed the code at src/trading_research/research/token_budget.py:31-37: _conservative_input_allowance sums len(system_prompt.encode()) + len(user_prompt.encode()) + len(str(request.json_schema).encode()). The str(dict) call does produce Python repr rather than JSON on-wire form (single quotes, Python literal ordering, etc.), so the observation is factually correct.  However, the practical impac

### F-082 [Low / CONFIRMED] `_safety_checks` in external-paper submit path runs before the order-scope lease is acquired and is never re-run inside the lease

- **Area / Dimension.** OPERATIONAL-WIRING
- **Affected workflow.** external-paper-submit

  `submit_external_paper_order` (external_broker.py:1319-1367) calls `_safety_checks(conn, book_id)` at :1327 BEFORE `_order_lease(...)` at :1329. Inside the lease, only fingerprint/baseline/preview/reservation gates run; no re-check of shadow safety pause, CRITICAL alert, or active critical reconciliation happens before the actual runtime submit at :1354. `retry_external_paper_order` (:1576) exhibits the same ordering.
- **Failure scenario.**
  An operator has typed `external-paper-submit ...`. Between `_safety_checks` (line 1327) and the fenced-write of `SUBMISSION_REQUESTED` (via `_submit_once` at :1354), a concurrent operator raises a CRITICAL alert or a shadow safety-pause becomes blocking. Because there is no re-check inside the lease, the in-flight submit still commits `SUBMISSION_REQUESTED` and calls `runtime.submit_limit_order`. The 'no submit while paused' invariant is violated for this narrow race window.
- **Impact.** Small in practice because the race window is bounded by the lease acquisition + fingerprint verify + preview validate cost; and durable state gates still protect after-the-fact retries. But the safety-pause invariant is described elsewhere as fail-closed; here it is fail-closed only at the entry, not inside the fenced writer.
- **Recommended correction.** Re-invoke `_safety_checks` inside the `_order_lease` block (immediately before `_submit_once` is called), or move the initial call to *after* the lease is held so the block is atomic with the durable event write.
- **Capability blocked.** pause/critical-alert enforcement across race window

### F-084 [Low / CONFIRMED] Historical trading days silently reclassified as holidays: Juneteenth (pre-2022) and MLK Day (pre-1998) applied unconditionally to every year

- **Severity note.** Original finder severity: High; adjusted after verification to Low.
- **Area / Dimension.** POINT-IN-TIME
- **Affected workflow.** Historical Backtesting + Forward-performance evaluation
- **Affected files.** `src/trading_research/evaluation/market_calendar.py`
- **Line ranges.** 78-91
- **Observed behavior.**
  `_federal_holidays(year)` (market_calendar.py:78-91) unconditionally includes `_observed(date(year, 6, 19))` (Juneteenth) and `_nth_weekday_of_month(year, 1, 0, 3)` (MLK Day) for every requested year, with no year-of-first-observance gate. NYSE first observed Juneteenth on 2022-06-20 (the 2021 observed-Monday for 2021-06-19 Saturday was after Juneteenth became federal on 2021-06-17, so its actual NYSE effect started in 2022). MLK Day first became an NYSE holiday in 1998. `is_trading_day(day)` (:102-103) therefore treats real historical trading sessions before those years as closed.
- **Failure scenario.**
  Run a backtest configuration over 2019-01-01..2020-12-31. On 2019-06-19 (Wed, a real NYSE trading session), engine.py:152 builds `sessions` from bars supplied by the provider (which correctly contain the 2019-06-19 bar because it was a live session), but any evaluation call flowing through `market_calendar.is_trading_day(date(2019,6,19))` (e.g., `evaluate_recommendation` at evaluation_service.py:55-56 -> `next_trading_session(exec_date, inclusive=True)` and `add_trading_days`) will skip 2019-06-19. A recommendation executed on 2019-06-18 with `horizon_trading_days=1` gets `anchor=next_trading_session(2019-06-18, inclusive=True)=2019-06-18` then `target_date=add_trading_days(2019-06-18,1)=2019-06-20`, silently rolling one full trading day into the return computation and producing a return that includes 2019-06-19's move on top of the intended 1-day horizon.
- **Impact.** Silently corrupts every horizon-based return computation for historical periods before each holiday's first NYSE observance. Also mis-classifies bars in `add_trading_days` used across evaluation.evaluation_service and any callers of `next_trading_session`.
- **Why existing tests do not prevent it.** The current calendar tests exercise post-2022 dates where every listed holiday is real; no test constructs a pre-2022 backtest horizon that crosses 6/19.
- **Recommended correction.** Gate each holiday by first-observance year (Juneteenth: `if year >= 2022`; MLK Day: `if year >= 1998`; Good Friday is always observed but stagger new holidays only). Add regression tests for pre-2022 date ranges.
- **Recommended regression tests.** Test `is_trading_day(date(2019,6,19))` == True; `is_trading_day(date(1990,1,15))` == True (third Monday). Also test `add_trading_days(date(2019,6,17), 5)` matches an oracle calendar for 2019.
- **Finder confidence.** high
- **Verification.** 3 votes (2 confirmed, 1 plausible, 0 refuted).
  - _severity_: verdict=CONFIRMED, refuted=False - Defect (year-agnostic Juneteenth + MLK Day) is real in market_calendar.py:80-85 — the year gate is absent, so `is_trading_day(date(2021,6,18))` and `is_trading_day(date(1997,1,20))` return False despite those being live NYSE sessions. However, the reported "scenario" and "impact" are overstated: (1) the backtesting engine does NOT use market_calendar at all — engine.py:152 builds sessions from pro
  - _reachability_: verdict=PLAUSIBLE, refuted=False - The underlying code defect is real and confirmed: market_calendar.py:78-91 unconditionally applies Juneteenth (added to NYSE in 2022) and MLK Day (added in 1998) to every year with no year gate. However, the reported reachability scenario is largely wrong. The claim that engine.py:152 is affected is refuted: backtesting/engine.py contains no imports or calls to market_calendar (grep for is_trading
  - _code-refute_: verdict=CONFIRMED, refuted=False - Read src/trading_research/evaluation/market_calendar.py (lines 78-91) and confirmed that `_federal_holidays(year)` unconditionally adds both MLK Day (line 81) and Juneteenth (line 85) for every year, with no year-of-first-observance gate. Traced the scenario through evaluation_service.py:54-56: `add_trading_days(2019-06-18, 1)` will indeed skip 2019-06-19 (a real NYSE trading day) and land on 2019

### F-085 [Low / PLAUSIBLE] `PriceProvider.available_at` is never checked in evaluation_service — a fixture-registered price whose availability post-dates `now` can still be consumed

- **Severity note.** Original finder severity: Medium; adjusted after verification to Low.
- **Area / Dimension.** POINT-IN-TIME
- **Affected workflow.** Forward-performance evaluation
- **Affected files.** `src/trading_research/evaluation/evaluation_service.py`, `src/trading_research/evaluation/price_provider.py`
- **Line ranges.** evaluation_service.py:66-91; price_provider.py:24-67
- **Observed behavior.**
  `PricePoint` carries `available_at: datetime | None` (price_provider.py:30) but `evaluate_recommendation` (evaluation_service.py:69-91) only gates on `target_date > now.date()` (:66) and then consumes `ending_point.close`/`benchmark_end_point.close` without asserting `available_at <= now`. `DeterministicPriceProvider.register` (price_provider.py:54-57) accepts an arbitrary `available_at` without validation, and `get_close` returns it as-is. The Protocol comment tells the provider to enforce point-in-time safety, but the caller does not defensively verify it, so a mis-configured or buggy provider can silently leak future information into an evaluation.
- **Failure scenario.**
  Caller registers a preliminary close for target_date T=2024-11-05 at 08:00 UTC 2024-11-05 (bar not yet closed; `available_at = 2024-11-05T23:00Z`). `evaluate_recommendation(..., now=datetime(2024,11,5,15,00,tzinfo=UTC))` sees `target_date <= now.date()` (both 2024-11-05), calls `price_provider.get_close(symbol, T)`, receives the point, and computes returns using a price whose `available_at` is 8 hours in the future — a silent future-leakage.
- **Impact.** Evaluation-layer look-ahead relies entirely on the provider being well-behaved; any real provider bug or fixture mis-registration bypasses the layer's own point-in-time guarantee. Contradicts the module docstring's `no look-ahead` claim.
- **Why existing tests do not prevent it.** Every test in `tests/unit/test_evaluation_service.py` registers prices whose `available_at` is either None or safely in the past; no test exercises the mis-configured-provider path.
- **Recommended correction.** In `evaluate_recommendation`, when `ending_point.available_at is not None`, reject/return `INCOMPLETE_MISSING_DATA` when `available_at > now`. Same for benchmark points.
- **Recommended regression tests.** Register a price with `available_at` after `now`; assert `status=='INCOMPLETE_MISSING_DATA'` or equivalent — currently the test would pass but with a real return value.
- **Finder confidence.** medium
- **Verification.** 3 votes (1 confirmed, 1 plausible, 1 refuted).

In evaluation_service.py:66-91, `evaluate_recommendation` gates only on `target_date > now.date()` (line 66) and then consumes `ending_point.close`, `benchmark_start_point.close`, `benchmark_end_point.close` (lines 88-91) without any check of `PricePoint.available_at`. In price_provider.py:54-67, `DeterministicPriceProvider.register` accepts an arbitrary `available_at` and
  - _code-refute_: verdict=PLAUSIBLE, refuted=False - Re-reading src/trading_research/evaluation/evaluation_service.py and src/trading_research/evaluation/price_provider.py confirms the finding's observation is faithful:  1. evaluation_service.py:66 gates only on `target_date > now.date()`; lines 69-71 call `price_provider.get_close` three times; lines 88 and 91 consume `.close` without any inspection of `.available_at`. Grepping the evaluation packa

### F-086 [Low / PLAUSIBLE] `execution_completed_at.date()` is used as calendar anchor without exchange-timezone conversion, misaligning the evaluation anchor for after-hours UTC-stamped fills

- **Area / Dimension.** POINT-IN-TIME
- **Affected workflow.** Forward-performance evaluation anchor derivation
- **Affected files.** `src/trading_research/evaluation/evaluation_service.py`
- **Line ranges.** 44-56
- **Observed behavior.**
  `execution_date = execution_completed_at.date()` (evaluation_service.py:54) followed by `anchor = next_trading_session(execution_date, inclusive=True)`. `execution_completed_at` is stored as a timezone-aware datetime that is typically UTC in this codebase, but the market calendar operates in America/New_York (market_calendar.py:20). For fills timestamped between 00:00 UTC and 05:00 UTC (roughly 19:00 ET previous day through midnight ET), the UTC date is one day ahead of the ET session date.
- **Failure scenario.**
  A fill completes at 2024-11-05T02:30Z (2024-11-04 21:30 ET Monday, after-hours). `execution_date = 2024-11-05`; `anchor = next_trading_session(Tuesday 2024-11-05, inclusive=True) = 2024-11-05`; `target_date` for horizon=1 = Wednesday 2024-11-06. The correct ET session anchor is Tuesday 2024-11-05 in this case (the next tradable session after a Monday after-hours fill), which happens to coincide. But for a fill at 2024-11-02T02:30Z (2024-11-01 22:30 ET Friday), `execution_date = 2024-11-02` (Saturday), `anchor = 2024-11-04` (Monday). The correct ET-based anchor is also 2024-11-04, so in this case it's OK. However, for a fill at 2024-11-04T01:00Z (2024-11-03 21:00 ET Sunday) — implausible but not blocked — the UTC date is Monday 2024-11-04 and the ET date is Sunday 2024-11-03; both round up to Monday. The bug bites when the underlying operational clock feeding this field mixes ET-local naive stamps and UTC stamps.
- **Impact.** Possible NeedsVerification. If any caller passes an ET-local (or non-UTC) `execution_completed_at`, `.date()` returns the wrong calendar date, offsetting the evaluation anchor by up to one trading day.
- **Why existing tests do not prevent it.** Existing tests supply UTC datetimes and never test the off-hours boundary.
- **Recommended correction.** Convert `execution_completed_at.astimezone(ZoneInfo('America/New_York')).date()` (or the timezone the calendar operates in) before calling `next_trading_session`. Explicitly document the required timezone at the parameter boundary.
- **Recommended regression tests.** Test `evaluate_recommendation` with `execution_completed_at = datetime(2024,11,5,2,30,tzinfo=UTC)` vs `datetime(2024,11,4,21,30,tzinfo=ZoneInfo('America/New_York'))` and assert identical anchor/target_date.
- **Finder confidence.** medium
- **Verification.** 3 votes (1 confirmed, 1 plausible, 1 refuted).
