  - _reachability_: verdict=PLAUSIBLE, refuted=False - The candidate's stated risk about "mixing ET-local naive stamps and UTC stamps" is refuted — `_require_tz_aware` (execution/models.py:46-48, 183) rejects naive datetimes at ingestion, and every production writer of `completed_at` uses `datetime.now(timezone.utc)` or a UTC-aware event time. However, a residual reachable variant of the same misalignment exists: in the sync/CLI finalization paths (se
  - _code-refute_: verdict=REFUTED, refuted=True - The code at evaluation_service.py:54-56 does call `execution_completed_at.date()` and then `next_trading_session(execution_date, inclusive=True)`, so the observation about the mechanism is factually accurate. However, the claim of an actual misalignment defect cannot be independently reproduced from the codebase:  1) `PaperExecutionResult.completed_at` is required to be tz-aware (`execution/models

### F-087 [Low / PLAUSIBLE] Legacy paper reconciliation compares broker cash to ledger total_cash including unsettled T+1 proceeds

- **Severity note.** Original finder severity: Medium; adjusted after verification to Low.
- **Area / Dimension.** PORTFOLIO-CONCENTRATION
- **Affected files.** `src/trading_research/services/reconcile_paper.py`, `src/trading_research/paper/ledger.py`, `src/trading_research/execution/account_reconciliation.py`
- **Line ranges.** reconcile_paper.py:36-42; ledger.py:105-111; account_reconciliation.py:22-37
- **Observed behavior.**
  reconcile_paper_account_and_positions (reconcile_paper.py:36-42) reads broker cash via BrokerAccountSnapshot.cash = Decimal(account_payload['cash']) and compares it to `Decimal(str(ledger.total_cash()))`. paper/ledger.py:105-111 defines settled_cash(now) as `state['settled']` after applying today's settled deltas, and total_cash() as `settled + sum(a for _, a in state['pending'])`. Pending entries are populated on every sale as `state['pending'].append([settle_date, proceeds])  # T+1` (paper/ledger.py:216). reconcile_account (account_reconciliation.py:22-37) compares broker.cash to the passed-in ledger_cash within a Decimal('0.01') tolerance (reconcile_paper.py:21).
- **Failure scenario.**
  The Milestone-4 credentialed paper flow sells 100 shares at $50 mid-day, netting $5,000 in T+1 pending proceeds in the local ledger. The Alpaca paper account reports `cash` reflecting Alpaca's own settlement semantics for those proceeds (typically not immediately spendable). ledger.total_cash() = settled + $5,000 pending, while broker.cash reflects Alpaca's view; the two differ by up to the pending amount and reconcile_account records status='CASH_MISMATCH' with `difference` far exceeding the $0.01 tolerance, generating spurious critical reconciliation rows every trading day after a sell until settlement clears.
- **Impact.** False-positive CASH_MISMATCH statuses in paper_account_reconciliations after every sale (invariant per docs/milestone-4.md Step 10: 'reports a mismatch'). Because Layer A is a plain compare-and-log path with no repair, false positives dilute operator trust in the mismatch signal and can mask genuine settled-cash divergences. The reconciliation is only reachable via the quarantined `legacy-paper-reconcile` command (cli.py:3087-3092), so exposure is limited but not zero.
- **Why existing tests do not prevent it.** Any existing test likely primes the ledger before pending is created or uses a broker snapshot whose cash equals total_cash by construction. The asymmetric settled-vs-pending path is not exercised.
- **Recommended correction.** Either (a) pass ledger.settled_cash(now) to reconcile_account (matching Alpaca's typical `cash` semantics) with an explicit test that the T+1 pending pool is compared separately against Alpaca's non-marginable buying-power/pending fields, or (b) document that broker.cash and ledger.total_cash() are semantically different and add a settle_pending_before_compare policy switch. Also add a `pending_cash_usd` field to BrokerAccountSnapshot and compare independently.
- **Recommended regression tests.** Add tests/unit/test_reconcile_paper.py case where PaperLedger has non-zero pending proceeds and broker cash matches only settled_cash: assert status='MATCHED' (or a distinct 'PENDING_ONLY_MISMATCH' status), never 'CASH_MISMATCH'.

- **Verification.** 3 votes (0 confirmed, 2 plausible, 1 refuted).

No other in-tree caller invokes `reconcile_paper_account

A divergence up to the pending amount is architecturally possible whenever the broker's ca

### F-088 [Low / CONFIRMED] LedgerPortfolioContextProvider denominator mixes cash with cost basis

- **Area / Dimension.** PORTFOLIO-CONCENTRATION
- **Affected files.** `src/trading_research/evidence_providers/portfolio_context.py`
- **Line ranges.** portfolio_context.py:30-53
- **Observed behavior.**
  LedgerPortfolioContextProvider.fetch computes `denominator = total_cash + position_cost_basis` (line 40) and `symbol_weight = symbol_position['qty'] * symbol_position['avg_cost'] / denominator` (lines 41-44). total_cash is a live PaperLedger balance (settled + pending T+1 proceeds via ledger.total_cash()) — economically a market-value quantity — while position_cost_basis is a *cost-basis* aggregate. The provider labels the result `portfolio_weight_cost_basis` (line 50) and includes it in the evidence bundle shown to Claude. Round-tripping through Python's float `round(symbol_weight, 6)` adds additional precision loss on top of the mixed-units division.
- **Failure scenario.**
  A book has $100,000 cash and one AAPL position with 100 shares at $150 average cost = $15,000 cost basis, current market $250 × 100 = $25,000 market value. denominator = 100,000 + 15,000 = 115,000; weight = 15,000/115,000 ≈ 0.1304. The true cost-basis weight vs total capital-at-cost is defensible, but total_cash is not a cost-basis figure — it moves with cash flows. If the book gains cash from a favorable fill, the same cost-basis exposure will report a *lower* weight, even though the risk exposure to AAPL has not decreased. Because this weight is one of the deterministic factors passed into Claude's prompt, it can subtly bias the model's understanding of book concentration.
- **Impact.** Claude receives a portfolio_weight_cost_basis whose semantics silently vary with cash flows rather than being a pure cost-basis or a pure mark-to-market weight. Since deterministic risk sizing owns final allocation (per module docstring note lines 54-59), no cap is bypassed — but the concentration signal Claude uses to reason about `existing_position_shares`/`portfolio_weight_cost_basis` is inconsistent. Low severity because deterministic risk is authoritative.
- **Why existing tests do not prevent it.** tests/unit/test_evidence_adapters_fail_closed.py exercises the fail-closed path (empty ledger) but does not assert the semantics of the weight under varying cash.
- **Recommended correction.** Compute either (a) a pure cost-basis weight `position_cost_basis / sum_of_all_cost_bases` (rename to `portfolio_weight_cost_basis`), or (b) a pure market-value weight against a properly-marked total, or (c) both and label them distinctly. Use Decimal end-to-end instead of float `round()`.
- **Recommended regression tests.** Add a unit test that fixes cost bases and varies cash-only (e.g., a dividend event), asserting portfolio_weight_cost_basis is invariant when only cash changes.
- **Finder confidence.** high
- **Verification.** 3 votes (2 confirmed, 0 plausible, 1 refuted).
  - _code-refute_: verdict=CONFIRMED, refuted=False - Verified against source. portfolio_context.py:40 computes denominator = total_cash + position_cost_basis, and lines 41-44 divide symbol cost basis by that mixed denominator; the result is labeled portfolio_weight_cost_basis at line 50 and included in the evidence bundle returned by fetch(). ledger.py:109-111 confirms total_cash() = settled + sum(pending) — a live cash balance that fluctuates with 
  - _severity_: verdict=CONFIRMED, refuted=False - Verified at portfolio_context.py:30-60. Line 40 computes `denominator = total_cash + position_cost_basis`, where `total_cash` (ledger.py:109-111) sums settled cash plus pending T+1 proceeds — a live cash quantity — while `position_cost_basis` is a sum of qty*avg_cost. The result is emitted on line 50 as `portfolio_weight_cost_basis`, and the payload's own note (lines 54-59) explicitly asserts the 
  - _reachability_: verdict=REFUTED, refuted=True - Under the reachability lens, the described mixed-units denominator in LedgerPortfolioContextProvider.fetch (portfolio_context.py:30-53) is not exercised in production. ### F-089 [Low / CONFIRMED] Third-party GitHub Actions pinned only to major-version tags, not commit SHAs

- **Area / Dimension.** SECURITY-PRIVACY
- **Affected files.** `.github/workflows/ci.yml`
- **Line ranges.** .github/workflows/ci.yml:17-18,30-31,44-45,73-74,94-95
- **Observed behavior.**
  Every job uses `actions/checkout@v4` and `actions/setup-python@v5`. Tags are mutable references — the maintainer or a compromised maintainer account can retag `v4`/`v5` to a different commit. GitHub's own hardening guide ("Security hardening for GitHub Actions") recommends full 40-character SHA pinning for third-party (and even first-party) actions in security-sensitive workflows.
- **Failure scenario.**
  Attacker compromises a maintainer credential (as with the `tj-actions/changed-files` incident, March 2025). They push a malicious commit and move the `v4`/`v5` tag to it. On the next CI run, the mutated action executes inside this repo's runner and can exfiltrate any secret in scope or (combined with SECURITY-PRIVACY-01) use the GITHUB_TOKEN.
- **Impact.** Amplifies the blast radius of a third-party action compromise from "only the actions I choose to update to" to "the next CI run after the tag is silently moved."
- **Recommended correction.** Pin each `uses:` to a full commit SHA and record the semver in a trailing comment, or adopt Dependabot's actions-pinning mode. Consistent with the existing `pyright-safety.json` posture of aggressive, explicit gating on infra-facing components.
- **Finder confidence.** high
- **Verification.** 3 votes (2 confirmed, 1 plausible, 0 refuted).
  - _reachability_: verdict=CONFIRMED, refuted=False - Factually confirmed: ci.yml pins actions/checkout@v4 and actions/setup-python@v5 at every `uses:` (lines 17, 18, 30, 31, 45, 46, 74, 75, 95, 96) — all mutable major-version tags, no SHA pin. Reachability is unconditional: the workflow triggers on every push to main and every pull_request (lines 8-10), and no `if:` gates any job, so a retagged v4/v5 executes on the very next CI run. Two caveats wor
  - _severity_: verdict=PLAUSIBLE, refuted=False - Verified against .github/workflows/ci.yml (lines 17-18, 30-31, 45-46, 74-75, 95-96 — cited lines are off by one but observation is correct). Every `uses:` in the workflow pins to a mutable major-version tag (`actions/checkout@v4`, `actions/setup-python@v5`) rather than a full 40-char SHA. The technical observation is real and matches GitHub's own hardening recommendation.\n\nHowever, the finding's
  - _code-refute_: verdict=CONFIRMED, refuted=False - Verified .github/workflows/ci.yml directly. All five `uses:` steps reference mutable major-version tags: `actions/checkout@v4` and `actions/setup-python@v5` at lines 17, 18, 30, 31, 45, 46, 74, 75, 95, 96. ### F-090 [Low / PLAUSIBLE] `PAPER_RUNTIME_ENV_FILE` permission-mode check silently no-ops on Windows

- **Area / Dimension.** SECURITY-PRIVACY
- **Affected files.** `paper_runtime/src/trading_paper_runtime/configuration.py`
- **Line ranges.** paper_runtime/src/trading_paper_runtime/configuration.py:103-133
- **Observed behavior.**
  Line 118-121: `mode = resolved.stat().st_mode; if mode & 0o022: return`. On Windows the POSIX group/other-write bits are not reported by `stat()` in a meaningful way — CPython synthesizes st_mode from ACLs but the low bits typically don't reflect actual writability (Python docs: "On Windows, only 0o444 and 0o222 have any effect"). The docstring at :95-96 hedges "on POSIX," but the code does not raise on non-POSIX; it just accepts whatever file the operator points at.
- **Failure scenario.**
  On a Windows operator workstation, an operator points `PAPER_RUNTIME_ENV_FILE` at `C:/Users/Public/alpaca.env`. The file inherits `Public` ACLs (world-writable in practice). `mode & 0o022` returns 0 on Windows and the file is loaded. A malicious local process rewrites the file to point `ALPACA_BASE_URL` at a non-paper endpoint (the URL is still asserted equal to `https://paper-api.alpaca.markets` at paper_runtime/main.py, so this specific override fails closed), or corrupts `ALPACA_IS_PAPER` to a value that still passes `.strip().lower() == 'true'`, or replaces the API key/secret with attacker-controlled ones.
- **Impact.** The advertised POSIX-hardening on the dedicated credential file is silently absent on Windows deployments; the runtime nonetheless documents "a dedicated credential file should be operator-readable only."
- **Recommended correction.** Either (a) explicitly fail closed on non-POSIX platforms (refuse to load `PAPER_RUNTIME_ENV_FILE` unless the check is meaningful), or (b) use an OS-appropriate ACL check on Windows (e.g., `pywin32` DACL scan), or (c) document that Windows is not supported for the isolated runtime credential file.
- **Finder confidence.** medium
- **Verification.** 3 votes (1 confirmed, 1 plausible, 1 refuted).
  - _reachability_: verdict=REFUTED, refuted=True - The finding's technical premise is incorrect. CPython on Windows synthesizes st_mode from file attributes such that non-readonly files return 0o100666 (group/other bits are copied from owner bits), not a value with the 0o022 bits cleared. Therefore `mode & 0o022` evaluates to 0o022 (truthy) for an ordinary Windows file, and the check at configuration.py:120-121 rejects the file — failing closed — 
  - _code-refute_: verdict=CONFIRMED, refuted=False - The finding's observed behavior is faithful to the code. configuration.py:118-121 performs `mode & 0o022` with no platform gating. ### F-091 [Low / CONFIRMED] Central log-redaction registry (`register_secret`) is dead code; CLI never installs the RedactingFormatter

- **Area / Dimension.** SECURITY-PRIVACY

  `register_secret()` has zero call sites in production (Grep confirms only the definition and one deep-dive scratchpad reference). `configure_logging()` is invoked only from `paper_runtime/main.py` (which has its own separate `_RedactingFilter`) and `scripts/inventory_mcp_tools.py`. The current half-implementation is worse than either endpoint.

  - _code-refute_: verdict=CONFIRMED, refuted=False - Verified against the cited files. logging_config.py:20 defines an empty _RUNTIME_SECRETS list; register_secret (lines 23-26) is the sole in-repo call site aside from its definition (Grep found only the definition plus a scratchpad reference).   - _severity_: verdict=CONFIRMED, refuted=False - Verified via direct file reads and grep. logging_config.py:20-26 defines register_secret() and _RUNTIME_SECRETS, but grep across the tree shows zero production callers — only the definition itself and a scratchpad note. configure_logging() at lines 62-73 is invoked only from scripts/inventory_mcp_tools.py:32; paper_runtime uses a completely separate module (paper_runtime/logging_config.py:18-27) w

### F-093 [Low / PLAUSIBLE] event_catalyst content_id omits selected_event identity

- **Severity note.** Original finder severity: Medium; adjusted after verification to Low.
- **Area / Dimension.** STRATEGY-CORRECTNESS
- **Affected files.** `src/trading_research/strategies/event_catalyst.py`, `src/trading_research/strategies/contracts.py`
- **Line ranges.** event_catalyst.py:102-110, 200-213; contracts.py:155-176
- **Observed behavior.**
  `_content_payload` (contracts.py:155-176) includes `data_as_of`, `factor_values`, `reason_codes`, and price references — but never records `selected_event.event_id`, `source`, `source_reference`, `event_type`, or `positive_or_negative`. The only event-related information encoded is `event_age_hours` (see -01) and (indirectly) `data_as_of` which includes `selected_event.published_timestamp` (event_catalyst.py:169-172). Two distinct events with identical `published_timestamp` and identical downstream factor values would collide.
- **Failure scenario.**
  Two positive events for TSLA published at the same second — say a `contract_or_partnership_announcement` (event_id `ev-A`) and an unrelated `regulatory_decision` (event_id `ev-B`) — both make it into `positive_events` and one is chosen deterministically by (published_timestamp desc, event_id asc). If the caller reruns after correcting the event_id / re-classifying the winner, `data_as_of` is unchanged (same published_timestamp), factor_values are unchanged (bars unchanged), so the content_id is identical to the prior run — yet the strategy is now built on a different underlying event, defeating 'event/source identity in dedup' from the review dimension.
- **Impact.** Two economically different signals share the same content ID; dedup that relies on content ID would incorrectly merge them. The `strategy_evaluation_id` (contracts.py:193-204) also inherits this gap because it adds only `signal_timestamp`, not event identity.
- **Recommended correction.** Include the selected event's identity (`event_id` at minimum, ideally `source`+`source_reference`+`event_type`+`positive_or_negative`) in `factor_values`-adjacent metadata that is part of `_content_payload`, or add a dedicated `selected_event` field to `StrategySignal` and hash it inside `_content_payload`.
- **Finder confidence.** medium
- **Verification.** 3 votes (1 confirmed, 2 plausible, 0 refuted).

  - _code-refute_: verdict=PLAUSIBLE, refuted=False - Confirmed by direct reading: `_content_payload` (contracts.py:159-176) hashes `strategy_id`, `strategy_version`, `symbol`, `data_as_of`, `status`, references, `expected_holding_period`, `reason_codes`, sorted `factor_values`, and `configuration_hash` — no event identity anywhere. `StrategySignal` (contracts.py:41-66) has no `selected_event`/`event_id` field. ### F-094 [Low / PLAUSIBLE] Momentum rs_term hardcodes RS scale despite 'arbitrary scale' contract

- **Area / Dimension.** STRATEGY-CORRECTNESS
- **Affected files.** `src/trading_research/strategies/momentum_breakout.py`, `src/trading_research/models/trading_models.py`
- **Line ranges.** momentum_breakout.py:150; models/trading_models.py:111-118
- **Observed behavior.**
  momentum_breakout.py:150 computes `rs_term = clip01((relative_strength + 2.0) / 4.0)` — a linear map assuming RS lies in [-2, +2]. `TechnicalFactorInput.relative_strength` is documented (models/trading_models.py:113) as `# e.g. vs sector/benchmark, arbitrary scale`. There is no validation that upstream providers deliver RS in that band, and `minimum_relative_strength` (config-driven, checked at momentum_breakout.py:91) is on the same 'arbitrary' scale.
- **Failure scenario.**
  If a market-data provider returns RS as a percentile (0..100) or a percent (e.g. 5.0 meaning +5%), the rs_term saturates at 1.0 for essentially every eligible candidate (clip01((5+2)/4)=1.0, clip01((85+2)/4)=1.0). Signal strengths across symbols become indistinguishable on the RS axis and the selector's ranking by `signal_strength` (selector.py:37-45) loses discrimination on that factor.
- **Impact.** Degrades ranking quality but does not violate a fail-closed invariant. Real correctness bug only if `minimum_relative_strength` is set to a value from a different scale than the provider's, which would produce silent misclassification.
- **Recommended correction.** Either (a) pin `TechnicalFactorInput.relative_strength` to a documented, validated scale and enforce it at construction time, or (b) normalize the RS scale in the strategy via a config-driven `relative_strength_center`/`relative_strength_half_width` pair rather than the hard-coded `+2 / 4`.
- **Finder confidence.** medium
- **Verification.** 3 votes (1 confirmed, 1 plausible, 1 refuted).

  - _code-refute_: verdict=CONFIRMED, refuted=False - The finding's observations reproduce faithfully from the cited files:  - momentum_breakout.py:150 literally reads `rs_term = clip01((relative_strength + 2.0) / 4.0)` with an inline comment acknowledging "relative_strength is on an arbitrary scale". This is a hard-coded linear map that assumes RS lies in roughly [-2, +2]. - models/trading_models.py:113 documents `relative_strength: float | None = N
  - _severity_: verdict=PLAUSIBLE, refuted=False - Confirmed by reading momentum_breakout.py:143-157 and models/trading_models.py:110-118. The strategy's `_signal_strength` hardcodes `rs_term = clip01((relative_strength + 2.0) / 4.0)` (line 150) with an inline comment that itself acknowledges `relative_strength is on an arbitrary scale`. The dataclass field docstring (models/trading_models.py:113) explicitly says `arbitrary scale`, and there is no

### F-096 [NeedsVerification / PLAUSIBLE] AMBIGUOUS-prefix scan omits research_run_id-independent lineage: prompt-version bump releases the gate

- **Area / Dimension.** MODEL-TOKEN-COST
- **Affected workflow.** Research reuse / budget accounting
- **Affected files.** `src/trading_research/strategies/research_budget.py`, `src/trading_research/research/orchestration.py`
- **Line ranges.** research_budget.py:292-309; orchestration.py:214-236
- **Observed behavior.**
  `attempt_prefix` at research_budget.py:293-294 is `"{PREFIX}:{research_run_id}:{symbol}:{provider}:{model}:"`. `research_run_id` is derived deterministically from a hash that includes prompt versions and system_prompt_hash (orchestration.py:214-236). A change to any prompt version produces a NEW `research_run_id`; the AMBIGUOUS scan therefore does not see prior-run reservations even for the same symbol+provider+model.
- **Failure scenario.**
  On 2026-07-19 an analyst-role attempt for `(symbol='XYZ', provider='anthropic', model='claude-x')` under `research_run_id=run-A` becomes AMBIGUOUS. An operator bumps a prompt version on 2026-07-19 producing `research_run_id=run-B`. A fresh call for `XYZ`/anthropic/claude-x under `run-B` is not blocked by run-A's ambiguous reservation, since the run-B attempt_prefix has a different `research_run_id`. The two reservations still both count against `daily_token_cap` via `_live_tokens_reserved_for_date`, but the reconciliation-required guard is bypassed.
- **Impact.** Debatable — a genuine prompt/config change legitimately establishes a new research identity; blocking it on stale ambiguous state may be overzealous. But the token/cost dimension is defined by (provider, model, day) not (prompt version), so the current scope is finer than the cost boundary.
- **Recommended correction.** Consider making the AMBIGUOUS-prefix scan match on `(symbol, provider, model)` (or `(symbol, provider, model, utc_date)` after fixing MODEL-TOKEN-COST-02) rather than including `research_run_id`, so prompt-version churn cannot unblock a pending reconciliation. NeedsVerification against the intended blast radius of the reconciliation gate.

  - _reachability_: verdict=PLAUSIBLE, refuted=False - Confirmed reachable: research_budget.py:292-295 builds attempt_prefix with the full research_run_id, and the AMBIGUOUS-prefix scan at :296-302 only matches identical research_run_id values. orchestration.py:214-236 shows compute_research_run_id deterministically hashes each role's prompt version+text_hash and the system_prompt_hash (lines 229, 233), and the docstring at :224-228 states outright th
  - _severity_: verdict=PLAUSIBLE, refuted=False - Observation is factually correct: attempt_prefix at research_budget.py:292-294 contains research_run_id, and orchestration.py:214-236 derives research_run_id from a digest that includes prompt versions and system_prompt_hash, so a prompt bump changes the id and the prefix scan at :296-302 would miss prior-run AMBIGUOUS rows. ## 6. Workflow safety matrix

| Workflow | Entry point | Fail-open/closed | Idempotency | Transaction safety | Recovery | Suspected defects |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Database initialization & schema upgrade/migration for the local SQLite research/paper-trading database. On every process that opens the DB, `storage.database.connect(db_path)` is the single entry point: it opens a SQLite connection with autocommit (`isolation_level=None`), sets `busy_timeout=5000ms`, enables foreign keys, WAL journal_mode, and `synchronous=NORMAL`, then in a fixed order runs (1) `check_schema_not_forward_versioned` (creates the `schema_version` ledger if absent, and refuses to open a DB whose stored MAX(version) is > the code's `CURRENT_SCHEMA_VERSION`); (2) idempotent additive DDL scripts for every subsystem (`apply_schema` for research runs/batches/etc., then trading, execution, evaluation, research, evidence_provider, research_cycle, corporate_status, shadow_operations, shadow_alerts, paper_books); (3) `apply_pending_schema_migrations` which iterates every migration in `_MIGRATIONS` not already recorded in `schema_version`, runs each inside its own `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction, appends the new row, and logs the version+description. Two subsystem `apply_*_schema` calls also perform their own inline upgrades: `paper_books_schema.apply_paper_books_schema` runs `_ensure_columns` (ALTER TABLE ADD COLUMN for any missing additive column in `_PAPER_BOOKS_COLUMN_UPGRADES`), a one-shot legacy external reservation backfill guarded by `_external_reservation_migration_needed` (inside its own BEGIN IMMEDIATE/rollback), the `_upgrade_triggers` versioned drop/recreate against `paper_books_trigger_versions`, and finally re-runs the trigger DDL. `migrations.apply_schema` additionally executes a special one-time rename (`rename_legacy_research_recommendations`) that renames a legacy `recommendations` table shape to `research_recommendations` and refuses if both exist. | src/trading_research/storage/database.py:30 `connect(db_path)` (also exposed as `session()` at line 70). Callers include CLI/tests (see ci.yml:106,125,146 and many `tests/**` imports of `trading_research.storage.database.connect`). | fail-closed | Two ledgers act as idempotency keys: (a) the `schema_version(version PRIMARY KEY, description, applied_at)` table — `apply_pending_schema_migrations` computes `pending = sorted(v for v in _MIGRATIONS if v not in applied)` (schema_version.py:323-324), so any migration whose version integer is present is skipped; (b) `paper_books_trigger_versions(trigger_name PRIMARY KEY, version)` (paper_books_schema.py:1538-1541) — `_upgrade_triggers` compares recorded vs current `_TRIGGER_VERSIONS` and only drops+recreates when `recorded < current_version`. All `apply_*_schema` DDL relies on `CREATE TABLE/INDEX/TRIGGER IF NOT EXISTS` as an implicit idempotency mechanism (documented at schema_version.py:7-11). | Each schema-version migration runs inside a dedicated `with transaction(conn):` (`storage.transactions.transaction`, transactions.py:57-75) which issues `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`, commits on clean exit, and rolls back on any `BaseException` — see schema_version.py:333-338 (migration_fn + INSERT of the version row happen inside the same transaction). Legacy external-reservation backfill uses an explicit `conn.execute("BEGIN IMMEDIATE")` with try/except `conn.rollback()`/`conn.commit()` (paper_books_schema.py:1680-1688). The subsystem `apply_*_schema` DDL scripts (e.g. research_schema.py:240-244, paper_books_schema.py:1676-1692) execute their `executescript` blocks outside an explicit transaction (SQLite auto-commits each DDL statement) and finish with `conn.commit()` — but since the connection is `isolation_level=None` autocommit, DDL is already committed statement-by-statement and the trailing `conn.commit()` is a no-op safeguard. | Recovery is stateless — the persisted `schema_version` table (and `paper_books_trigger_versions`) is the sole authoritative record of what has been applied. On reopen, `apply_pending_schema_migrations` reads `SELECT version FROM schema_version` (not `MAX`), so even a legacy DB with gaps (e.g. version 3 present but 2 absent) will still detect and re-run the missing versions (schema_version.py:320-324). `_external_reservation_migration_needed` (paper_books_schema.py:1652-1673) additionally detects any legacy active reservation not yet mirrored and re-runs the backfill until every eligible legacy row has an equivalent attempt-reservation row. | NeedsVerification: `apply_pending_schema_migrations` runs after every `apply_*_schema` DDL block already committed (each subsystem `apply_*_schema` ends with `conn.commit()`). If a migration transaction rolls back on failure, prior additive DDL from this same `connect()` call is already committed. That is the documented design (schema_version.py:315-319), but a partial `connect()` failure will still leave the DB partially upgraded relative to the code path that observed the failure — safe on the next connect thanks to CREATE IF NOT EXISTS, but worth confirming for a case where the additive DDL introduces a column referenced by a rolled-back migration.; NeedsVerification: `paper_books_schema._backfill_active_legacy_external_reservations` fetches with `LIMIT 100000` (paper_books_schema.py:1593). If a legacy database has more than 100k active RESERVED/RECONCILIATION_REQUIRED/SUBMITTED rows, only the first 100k (ordered by client_order_id) will be backfilled per `connect()` invocation — the `_external_reservation_migration_needed` predicate will still return True next time, so it will eventually complete over multiple connections, but a single-shot connect() cannot guarantee completeness on very large legacy datasets.; NeedsVerification: Migration 2 backfill (`_migration_2_backfill_execution_namespace_claims`, schema_version.py:71-93) iterates `SELECT book_id, paper_order_intent_id FROM paper_book_orders` and performs N × up-to-4 sub-SELECTs plus one INSERT per row. On a large legacy database this is O(N) work inside a single BEGIN IMMEDIATE transaction with no batching — could exceed the `busy_timeout=5000ms` window for other concurrent connections during upgrade. |
| Scheduled research cycle (scheduler -> activation). Two coupled surfaces: (a) `shadow/scheduler.py::run_due_shadow_cycle` drives one at-most intended slot: enabled check -> `schedule.resolve_due_status` (schedule/catch-up) -> pause/kill check -> idempotency check against `shadow_scheduler_runs` -> lease acquire -> budget reserve -> `save_scheduler_run` (RUNNING) -> injected `run_cycle` (which is `research/scheduled_cycle.py::run_scheduled_research_cycle`) -> settle budget -> health evaluation & hysteresis -> `update_scheduler_run` (final status) -> lease release in finally. Inner `run_scheduled_research_cycle` derives a deterministic `cycle_id` (universe_id+as_of+config_hash), writes `research_cycles` row (RUNNING), iterates bounded symbols with per-symbol try/except, and per symbol saves `research_cycle_symbol_results`, then marks final cycle status. (b) `paper_books/recurring_scheduler.py::run_recurring_paper_scheduler` is the *activation-gated* recurring loop over completed cycles that only runs when `current_activation_state(conn).state == STATE_ACTIVE` (an ACTIVATION_REQUESTED event followed by an ACTIVATED event exists in `paper_recurring_activation_events`); it acquires its own singleton `paper-recurring-local` lease, claims `paper_recurring_cycle_queue` items keyed on frozen-state hash, invokes `run_controlled_soak_day`, and finalizes the queue+scheduler run. | CLI `run-due-shadow-cycle` -> `trading_research/cli.py::run_due_shadow_cycle_cli` (src/trading_research/cli.py:1641-1882) which calls `shadow/scheduler.py::run_due_shadow_cycle` (src/trading_research/shadow/scheduler.py:551) passing `run_scheduled_research_cycle` (src/trading_research/research/scheduled_cycle.py:318) as `run_cycle`. Config comes from `load_strategy_config()` at `src/trading_research/strategies/config.py:160-283`. In this repo no production entry point in `src/` invokes `select_shortlist` — only tests do (verified by Grep on `select_shortlist(` and on `MomentumBreakoutStrategy(` / `MeanReversionStrategy(` / `EventDrivenCatalystStrategy(`). | fail-closed | - `select_shortlist`: none (pure function; deterministic on inputs). - `StrategySignal` content identity: `derive_strategy_signal_content_id` returns `"strat-sig-" + sha256(content_payload)[:32]` — excludes `signal_timestamp`, so identical economic content across evaluations shares one ID (`contracts.py:155-190`). | Two parallel submission stacks share the "ambiguous submission / crash recovery" pattern.  STACK A - Milestone 3/4 credentialed paper-broker submission (isolated runtime). Entry: submit_credentialed_paper_order (src/trading_research/services/submit_credentialed_paper_order.py:58-179). Idempotency: `paper_broker_submissions` row written PENDING_SUBMISSION *before* the runtime is contacted (lines 97-98; storage/execution_repositories.py:313-331 is a no-op on replay). Client-order-id derived deterministically from intent_id (runtime/client/models.py:17-24). Intent uniqueness enforced by intent_id PK + UNIQUE(recommendation_id, execution_version) (storage/execution_schema.py:16-36). Pre-submit recovery lookup: lines 104-128 always call client.get_order(client_order_id) before ever calling submit_order; found -> UPSERT status and return SUBMITTED without resubmit. Ambiguous submit outcome: on RuntimeUnavailable/Timeout/RuntimeOperationError (lines 131-170), performs exactly one bounded recovery get_order lookup (line 143); recovered -> SUBMITTED; recovered=None or lookup raised -> submission_status set to "SUBMISSION_UNKNOWN" and paper_execution_failures appended (lines 159-166). Never blind-resubmits. Poll/recovery loop: services/sync_paper_orders.py::sync_paper_orders (lines 56-63) is a bounded single-pass sweep invoked by CLI (cli.py:483-513); reads non-terminal submissions (storage/execution_repositories.py:354-361 filters out FILLED/CANCELLED/REJECTED/ERROR) and calls get_order per row. Fills become PaperExecutionEvent (sync_paper_orders.py:107-123) with event_id = intent_id-sync-{event_type}-{cumulative_filled}; incremental delta = cumulative - sum(prior events) (lines 84-94). Terminal states persist PaperExecutionResult (lines 126-135). Ledger boundary: execution/ledger_events.py::apply_paper_execution_event first persists event row (save_event no-ops on duplicate event_id, storage/execution_repositories.py:130-149), then applies to PaperLedger inside DuplicateOrderError trap, then flips ledger_applied flag (execution/ledger_events.py:26-69) -- belt-and-suspenders idempotency across the ledger write and the flag update. Transport-level crash defenses in runtime/client/process_client.py: any request timeout marks the transport permanently unhealthy (lines 176-241, 282-297); a mutating operation is never automatically retried on a fresh restart, only allow-listed read-only follow-ups like get_order are (lines 38-44, 259-273); a process EOF during a request is never interpreted as an order outcome (lines 299-306).  STACK B - Milestone 11 external Alpaca paper order coordinator. Entry: submit_external_paper_order (src/trading_research/paper_books/external_broker.py:1319-1366) via CLI external_paper_submit_cli (cli.py:361-372). Local state machine STATE_NOT_SUBMITTED -> STATE_PREVIEWED -> STATE_SUBMISSION_REQUESTED -> {SUBMITTED, PARTIALLY_FILLED, FILLED, CANCELLED, REJECTED, EXPIRED, STATE_UNKNOWN=UNKNOWN_REQUIRES_RECONCILIATION} enforced by _TRANSITIONS (lines 64-86). Authoritative validation: _intent (lines 502-582) reverifies frozen intent, risk/exit approval, terminal-status refusal, execution-namespace claim, TIF DAY, LIMIT, quantity/side, oversell for SELL, stale/future timestamps. _safety_checks (lines 585-607) refuses if a shadow safety pause is active, any CRITICAL alert unresolved, or any critical reconciliation still outstanding (except the specific allow-listed retry evidence path). _require_reconciliation_baseline (lines 729-745) fails closed if no baseline is on file or fingerprint drifted. Preview: preview_external_paper_order (lines 918-985) claims the EXTERNAL_PAPER execution namespace (fenced write; ExecutionNamespaceConflictError => INTENT_NOT_ELIGIBLE_FOR_EXTERNAL), performs read-only runtime preview, writes preview row + PREVIEWED event under fenced write. Submit path: acquires order-scope lease keyed on (book_id, client_order_id) (lines 875-916 via storage/paper_books_repositories.py:2035-2062 acquire_external_order_lease; ttl per PaperBooksConfiguration.external_broker.order_lease_ttl_seconds, default 45s, heartbeat 10s). Fresh account fingerprint verify + baseline check + validated preview. _reserve_daily_notional (lines 230-344) atomically claims an attempt-scoped reservation keyed by (client_order_id, attempt_number, account_fingerprint, reservation_date) inside its own BEGIN IMMEDIATE transaction, enforcing external_broker.maximum_daily_notional_usd. _submit_once (lines 1188-1286): under lease.fenced_write, reserves cash/shares (cash_ledger.reserve_for_order or positions.reserve_shares_for_sell) + appends SUBMISSION_REQUESTED event in one transaction (durable crash-checkpoint before runtime.submit_limit_order is called). Then calls runtime.submit_limit_order. On any exception -> _record_unknown appends STATE_UNKNOWN event and settles reservation to RECONCILIATION_REQUIRED (lines 1171-1185, 361-374). On success: _validate_order_response enforces shape/notional/timestamp invariants (lines 1118-1168), _state_from_order maps broker->local status (lines 1011-1022), fenced-writes success event + updates order status + settles reservation, then apply_external_fills sweeps normalized fills (lines 1369-1543) idempotently by pebf_ hashed external_fill_id (repo.fill_exists guard), applying buy/sell fills, cash settle, position release atomically per-fill under fenced_or_plain_write, then post-submit reconciliation via _reconcile_locked. Duplicate detection: _detect_duplicate_broker_order (lines 1028-1116) scans up to 100 recent broker orders and flags same-shape orders within a 300s window regardless of client-order-id prefix. Ambiguous / crash recovery: recover_stranded_submission (lines 1635-1696) is a dedicated, read-only recovery entry for an order stranded at SUBMISSION_REQUESTED (crash between the reservation+checkpoint commit and any broker response); delegates to _reconcile_locked, which calls _bridge_stranded_submission_requested to append a STATE_UNKNOWN event before persisting authoritative lookup evidence (lines 2014-2082) so retry_external_paper_order can eventually match on the correct event id. Retry: retry_external_paper_order (lines 1546-1632) requires current state STATE_UNKNOWN, requires a fresh, authoritative, unconsumed broker NOT_FOUND lookup whose ambiguous_event_id/attempt_number/payload_hash/account_fingerprint/book_id/client_order_id all match (lines 1561-1575), requires attempt_number < maximum_retry_attempts, re-checks account fingerprint, requires a matching APPROVED preview, transitions the prior reservation to SUPERSEDED_BY_RETRY atomically with creating the new attempt reservation (lines 294-327), then calls _submit_once with the incremented attempt_number and consumes the lookup row (lines 1623-1626). Preview-refresh escape hatch: refresh_retry_preview (lines 1699-1768) is a read-only DB-only rewrite of a preview that has expired but for which fresh authoritative NOT_FOUND evidence still exists; never calls the broker/runtime; never consumes the lookup. Reconciliation: reconcile_external_paper_order (lines 1925-1956) is the public entry that resolves the target order and acquires the lease; _reconcile_locked (lines 1959-1986) wraps _run_reconciliation and, on any unexpected exception, persists a RECONCILIATION_INTERNAL_ERROR critical evidence row -- never exits silently. _run_reconciliation (lines 1989-2282) fetches broker order, saves authoritative peol_ lookup evidence, bridges SUBMISSION_REQUESTED->UNKNOWN before the lookup save so ambiguous_event_id matches the freshly appended event, transitions the local state if _TRANSITIONS allows, applies fills, checks account fingerprint history, symbol/side/quantity/price mismatch, fill quantity mismatch, cash-and-position baseline-delta comparison, and duplicate detection. _persist_reconciliation (lines 2285-...) stamps critical=1 when any of CRITICAL_RECONCILIATION_STATUSES (lines 53-62) applies.  Cross-cutting: PaperReconciliationReport account/position reconciliation lives in services/reconcile_paper.py + execution/account_reconciliation.py + execution/reconciliation.py; these are pure comparators that never repair mismatches, they only report and persist (paper_account_reconciliations, paper_position_reconciliations tables via storage/execution_repositories.py:367-424). | Two entry points share the ambiguous/crash-recovery pattern: (1) services/submit_credentialed_paper_order.py::submit_credentialed_paper_order (:58) and its poller services/sync_paper_orders.py::sync_paper_orders (:56); (2) paper_books/external_broker.py::submit_external_paper_order (:1319), the dedicated recovery hook recover_stranded_submission (:1635), retry_external_paper_order (:1546), refresh_retry_preview (:1699), and reconcile_external_paper_order (:1925). CLI wrappers in src/trading_research/cli.py:361-513 (external_paper_submit_cli, external_paper_reconcile_cli, external_paper_retry_cli, external_paper_refresh_retry_preview_cli, sync_paper_orders_cli, reconcile_paper_cli). | fail-closed | Stack A: client_order_id = intent_id (runtime/client/models.py:17-24). intent_id = "intent-" + 32-hex derived from (recommendation_id, execution_version) (see runtime/client/models.py comment and execution/models.py::derive_intent_id). paper_broker_submissions is PK by intent_id with UNIQUE(client_order_id) (storage/execution_schema.py:91-93). paper_execution_events keyed by event_id (typically intent_id-sync-{type}-{cumulative_filled}, sync_paper_orders.py:117-123). Stack B: derive_external_order_identity (external_broker.py:431-444) builds a broker-safe client_order_id `epb-{book_id}-` + sha256 hex of frozen immutable payload; payload_hash is sha256 of the same. Attempt reservation id = derive_external_attempt_reservation_id(client_order_id, attempt_number, account_fingerprint, reservation_date) (:183-186 and storage/paper_books_schema.py). External event id = peoe_ digest of client_order_id/prev/new/now/request_id/attempt_number (:463-466). External broker fill id = pebf_ digest of book_id + fill_id (:1399-1400). Preview id = pepv_ digest. | Stack A: every save_* helper (storage/execution_repositories.py) calls conn.commit() individually. save_intent (line 89), create_pending_submission (line 313), update_submission_status (line 334), save_event (line 130), mark_event_ledger_applied (line 152), save_result (line 194), save_reconciliation (line 234) — each is its own commit. There is no single wrapping transaction across create_pending_submission + get_order + update; each step is independently durable, which is how idempotency after a crash is preserved. Stack B: order-scope lease + fenced writes. `_order_lease` (external_broker.py:875-916) acquires a single-row conditional INSERT ... ON CONFLICT (paper_books_repositories.py:2035-2062) and releases in finally. `OrderLeaseHandle.fenced_write` (:846-862) issues BEGIN IMMEDIATE, verifies lease ownership+generation+expiry inside the write lock, yields, and commits on success or rollbacks on exception. `_reserve_daily_notional` (:230-344) intentionally runs its own top-level BEGIN IMMEDIATE / commit outside the caller's lease-transaction so cross-book/process races against the same account/day are serialized. `_submit_once` bundles the pre-submit reservation write + SUBMISSION_REQUESTED checkpoint into one fenced_write transaction (:1210-1226) — either both commit or neither, so no unaccompanied reservation orphan can result from a crash. | Stack A: submit_credentialed_paper_order always calls get_order before submit (:104-128), so a crash between the PENDING_SUBMISSION insert and the runtime call is harmless. Stack B: recover_stranded_submission (external_broker.py:1635-1696) is the crash-recovery hook for an order stranded at SUBMISSION_REQUESTED. It only calls read-only get_order_by_client_order_id via _reconcile_locked and never submit_limit_order/preview_limit_order. If the broker still doesn't have the order (or the lookup itself errors), _bridge_stranded_submission_requested appends a STATE_UNKNOWN event (lines 2024-2038, 2263-2273) so that the fully-gated retry path becomes reachable. The bridging is emitted *before* the authoritative peol_ lookup row is written so lookup.ambiguous_event_id references the new STATE_UNKNOWN event, matching retry's later match criteria. Idempotent: a second call finds the chain already moved off SUBMISSION_REQUESTED and returns already_recovered. | POSSIBLE (NeedsVerification): sync_paper_orders._sync_one does not check the fenced-write invariant used by the paper_books stack; apply_all_new_events -> save_event and mark_event_ledger_applied each conn.commit() independently (storage/execution_repositories.py:130-156) while update_submission_status is a separate commit (sync_paper_orders.py:96-99). A crash between save_event/apply and update_submission_status can leave the ledger correctly applied but the submission row still showing the previous status. Because the poller re-reads via get_order each pass this is likely self-healing, but there is no explicit transaction bracketing.; POSSIBLE (NeedsVerification): submit_credentialed_paper_order.py:159-166 sets submission_status='SUBMISSION_UNKNOWN' and increments attempt_count on a runtime error, but there is no maximum retry cap or backoff for a caller that re-invokes this service after a lookup returned None. Re-invocation triggers the pre-submit get_order branch (line 104) which finds nothing and proceeds to submit_order again — this is the intended 'never blindly resubmit' contract via the pre-lookup, but a persistently ambiguous broker (get_order timing out) will oscillate: each call is a fresh submit attempt, distinct from the Stack B retry gate that requires authoritative NOT_FOUND evidence. Verify whether operational cadence assumes the outer CLI is invoked only after a human confirms.; POSSIBLE (NeedsVerification): sync_paper_orders._sync_one line 82 raises RuntimeOperationError with retryable=False on any status outside EVENT_TYPES. Stack B's runtime status set is a superset (includes ACCEPTED, EXPIRED, CANCEL_REQUESTED); if the credentialed runtime ever emits one of those statuses, the poller aborts on that intent with no state update, leaving the paper_broker_submissions row in whatever non-terminal state it was in and no failure row (record_failure is only called for transport-level errors on lines 70-73). A stuck submission then depends entirely on human intervention. |
| Account & reservation reconciliation runs in two distinct layers.  LAYER A — Milestone 4 simple paper account/position reconciliation - Entry: `reconcile_paper_cli(db_path)` at src/trading_research/cli.py:516-550 (dispatched from the `legacy-paper-reconcile` command at src/trading_research/cli.py:3087-3092). - Orchestrator: `reconcile_paper_account_and_positions(...)` at src/trading_research/services/reconcile_paper.py:30-62. Builds a runtime client (started/shutdown around a single `session(db_path)` block, cli.py:525-537), pulls `client.get_account()` and `client.list_positions()`, materializes `BrokerAccountSnapshot`/`BrokerPositionSnapshot`, compares to `PaperLedger.total_cash()`/`ledger.positions()` via pure comparators in src/trading_research/execution/account_reconciliation.py:22-88 (`reconcile_account`, `reconcile_all_positions`, `reconcile_position`). - Persistence: `save_account_reconciliation` and `save_position_reconciliation` at src/trading_research/storage/execution_repositories.py:367-406 (append-only rows into `paper_account_reconciliations` / `paper_position_reconciliations`, defined at src/trading_research/storage/execution_schema.py:102-128). - Terminal statuses: `MATCHED`, `CASH_MISMATCH`, `POSITION_MISMATCH`, `MISSING_BROKER_POSITION`, `MISSING_INTERNAL_POSITION` (account_reconciliation.py:26-61). Never repairs — only records mismatches.  LAYER B — External paper broker reservation-aware reconciliation (Milestone 11 / 11.3.1 / 23 / 24 / 25) - Public entries:   * `reconcile_external_paper_order(...)` at src/trading_research/paper_books/external_broker.py:1925-1956 (CLI shim: `external_paper_reconcile_cli` at src/trading_research/cli.py:375-384).   * `recover_stranded_submission(...)` at external_broker.py:1635-1696 for the SUBMISSION_REQUESTED crash-recovery path.   * Inline reconciliation is also called by `submit_external_paper_order` (external_broker.py:1319-1366), `retry_external_paper_order` (external_broker.py:1546-1632) and `cancel_external_paper_order` (external_broker.py:1771-1864) after their own broker calls settle. - Core state machine: `_reconcile_locked` (external_broker.py:1959-1987) → `_run_reconciliation` (external_broker.py:1989-2282). Reads current event, verifies lease, validates frozen intent notional (`_validate_frozen_notional`, external_broker.py:142-173), runs `_account_check` (external_broker.py:610-623), loads baseline (external_broker.py:2058-2063), does authoritative `get_order_by_client_order_id` broker lookup (external_broker.py:2066-2068), saves the lookup evidence (repo.save_external_lookup, external_broker.py:2083-2095), transitions attempt reservation to a target state (`_reconcile_attempt_reservation`, external_broker.py:377-420, called at 2097-2100, 2134-2137, 2141-2144), optionally sweeps fills (`apply_external_fills`, external_broker.py:1369-1543), performs delta-baseline cash/position comparison (external_broker.py:2225-2254), bridges stranded SUBMISSION_REQUESTED chains to STATE_UNKNOWN (external_broker.py:2024-2038, 2273), and finally persists a `paper_external_reconciliations` row via `_persist_reconciliation` (external_broker.py:2285-2303). - Daily reservation life-cycle: `_reserve_daily_notional` (external_broker.py:230-344), `_settle_daily_reservation` (external_broker.py:347-374), `_reconcile_attempt_reservation` (external_broker.py:377-420) drive transitions RESERVED → SUBMITTED / RELEASED / RECONCILIATION_REQUIRED / SUPERSEDED_BY_RETRY through `repo.transition_attempt_reservation_state` (storage/paper_books_repositories.py:1853-1883) with allowed-source predicates. | Layer A: `reconcile_paper_cli` (src/trading_research/cli.py:516-550) → `reconcile_paper_account_and_positions` (src/trading_research/services/reconcile_paper.py:30-62). Layer B (external paper): `external_paper_reconcile_cli` (src/trading_research/cli.py:375-384) → `reconcile_external_paper_order` (src/trading_research/paper_books/external_broker.py:1925-1956) → `_reconcile_locked` → `_run_reconciliation` (external_broker.py:1959-2282). Recovery entry: `recover_stranded_submission` (external_broker.py:1635-1696). Reconciliation is also called after submit/retry/cancel at external_broker.py:1362-1365, 1628-1631, 1953-1955. | fail-closed | - Layer A: none — every call appends a fresh row (rowid auto-increment) in paper_account_reconciliations / paper_position_reconciliations (execution_schema.py:102-128); `list_latest_position_reconciliations` (execution_repositories.py:409-424) picks the most recent per symbol. - Layer B:   * Reconciliation record: `reconciliation_id = _digest("per_", [book_id, client_order_id, statuses, details, now.isoformat()], 40)` (external_broker.py:2292-2294), inserted via `INSERT OR IGNORE` into `paper_external_reconciliations` (repo.save_external_reconciliation, storage/paper_books_repositories.py:1614-1628; table + immutability trigger at storage/paper_books_schema.py:683-696, 1368-1372).   * Attempt reservation: `derive_external_attempt_reservation_id(client_order_id, attempt_number, account_fingerprint, reservation_date)` (used at external_broker.py:183-186, 263).   * Order lease: `lease_key = f"{book_id}:{client_order_id}"` with per-owner UUID and monotonic `generation` (external_broker.py:897-908).   * Lookup: `_digest("peol_", [client_order_id, current["external_order_event_id"], lookup_result, request_id], 40)` (external_broker.py:2085-2088).   * External order events: `_digest("peoe_", [client_order_id, previous, new_state, now.isoformat(), runtime_request_id, attempt_number], 40)` (external_broker.py:463-476).   * Baseline: PRIMARY KEY (book_id) with INSERT OR IGNORE (paper_books_repositories.py:1649-1663) — captured exactly once per book. | - Layer A: `session(db_path)` context in cli.py:526-537 wraps the read + save calls; each `save_*_reconciliation` runs a bare `conn.execute(...); conn.commit()` (execution_repositories.py:367-406), so each row commits independently. There is no BEGIN IMMEDIATE — position rows commit one at a time inside a single Python-level for-loop (services/reconcile_paper.py:59-60). - Layer B:   * Every mutation inside `_run_reconciliation` is gated via `lease.fenced_write()` which wraps a `begin_immediate(conn)` + `verify_or_raise` + commit/rollback (external_broker.py:846-862). All commit=False writes inside a fenced block commit atomically at block exit.   * `_reserve_daily_notional` deliberately opens its OWN `transaction(conn)` (BEGIN IMMEDIATE) at external_broker.py:265, NOT inside a `fenced_write` — the docstring at 254-259 explains this is required so the read-then-write budget check is atomic across books, and its superseding of a prior reservation is inside the same transaction as the new insert.   * `_persist_reconciliation` uses `_fenced_or_plain_write` (external_broker.py:2301-2302) — a plain `transaction(conn)` when called without a lease (recovery-time only) and fenced otherwise.   * `_reconcile_attempt_reservation` runs with `commit=False` inside fenced blocks (external_broker.py:2097-2100, 2134-2137, 2141-2144), and `transition_attempt_reservation_state` (paper_books_repositories.py:1865-1875) is a single conditional UPDATE with state predicate. | - Crash between `_submit_once`'s reservation+SUBMISSION_REQUESTED commit and any broker response leaves the chain stranded at SUBMISSION_REQUESTED forever without the bridge; `recover_stranded_submission` (external_broker.py:1635-1696) is the operator entry point: acquires order lease, re-reads state under the lease, and delegates to `_reconcile_locked` which uses `_bridge_stranded_submission_requested` (external_broker.py:2024-2038, 2079-2081, 2273) to transition to STATE_UNKNOWN and mark the reservation RECONCILIATION_REQUIRED — retaining exposure until the operator retries or cancels. - The reservation itself is never released on trust: `_release_terminal_reservation` (external_broker.py:1294-1316) excludes FILLED so the release only happens inside `apply_external_fills` when the full approved quantity is durably applied (external_broker.py:1506-1517). - Recovery is idempotent — a second call sees the chain already moved off SUBMISSION_REQUESTED and returns `already_recovered` (external_broker.py:1690-1692). - Milestone 24 baseline is created only via `activate_external_reconciliation_baseline` preflight (docstring at external_broker.py:2054-2057) — reconciliation never invents a baseline; without one, delta compare is skipped and critical `RECONCILIATION_BASELINE_MISSING` is recorded (external_broker.py:2058-2063). - Cross-day retry: `_reserve_daily_notional` at external_broker.py:230-344 keeps prior-day reserved budget accounted for on either the old date (until superseded) or the new date, via atomic supersede-plus-new-insert within one BEGIN IMMEDIATE transaction (external_broker.py:294-343). | Layer A `reconcile_paper_account_and_positions` (src/trading_research/services/reconcile_paper.py:30-62) has no concurrency protection: two overlapping runs of `legacy-paper-reconcile` would both call `client.get_account()`/`client.list_positions()` and `save_*_reconciliation` interleaved. Each `save_*_reconciliation` (execution_repositories.py:367-406) runs a bare `execute + commit` outside a BEGIN IMMEDIATE, so position rows commit one-at-a-time in a Python loop (reconcile_paper.py:59-60). Not evidently unsafe (rows are append-only) but the report result surfaced to the operator can silently reflect a mix of two snapshots. NeedsVerification for any operational impact.; `reconcile_paper_cli` (src/trading_research/cli.py:526-537) opens a DB `session(...)` and only *then* starts the runtime client; a `client.start()` failure returns `{'error': ...}` without persisting anything (cli.py:527-530). If a partial `_build_runtime_client()` allocates OS resources before start, they are only released by `client.shutdown()` inside the `finally` gated by successful start — verify no resource leak on start failure. NeedsVerification.; Layer B `_run_reconciliation`'s tail bridge (external_broker.py:2263-2273) runs after all mismatches are collected, but the tail bridge always runs regardless of whether an earlier `_bridge_stranded_submission_requested(...)` already ran at external_broker.py:2079-2081. The re-check inside the helper (external_broker.py:2027-2028) makes the second call a no-op *only* if the chain has already advanced off SUBMISSION_REQUESTED. In practice the first call advances the chain, but if a mid-flight step raised before appending its own event (e.g. a broker lookup returned a valid order and then `_append_event` at 2127 failed), stranded_at_submission_requested would remain True and the tail bridge would append an UNKNOWN event even after we saw a valid broker response — worth verifying that the two invocations cannot double-append. NeedsVerification. |
| Cross-day retry & UTC-day reservation transitions ========================================================  WHAT THE WORKFLOW DOES - Enforces a per-UTC-day, per-Alpaca-paper-account cap on external-paper notional using per-attempt reservations that live in `paper_external_attempt_reservations` (schema: `src/trading_research/storage/paper_books_schema.py:937-948`, immutability triggers `1434-1443`, unique-active index `1101-1105`). - Reservation identity is deterministic: `derive_external_attempt_reservation_id(client_order_id, attempt_number, account_fingerprint, reservation_date)` (`paper_books_schema.py:1571-1585`). - Legal state graph in `paper_books_repositories.py:28-33`:     RESERVED -> SUBMITTED/RELEASED/RECONCILIATION_REQUIRED/SUPERSEDED_BY_RETRY     RECONCILIATION_REQUIRED -> SUBMITTED/RELEASED/SUPERSEDED_BY_RETRY (never back to RESERVED)   Terminal-for-budget: RELEASED, SUPERSEDED_BY_RETRY. `sum_attempt_reservations_by_date` excludes those (repo `1799-1804`).  DAY 1 - INITIAL AMBIGUOUS SUBMISSION (attempt_number=0) - `submit_external_paper_order` (`external_broker.py:1319-1366`):    1. `_intent(...)` validates intent, config, risk, side, TIF, quantity, staleness, position (`502-582`).    2. `_safety_checks` (`585-644`) blocks on shadow pause / CRITICAL alert / unresolved critical reconciliation.    3. Acquires `_order_lease` (`876-915`) keyed by `(book_id, client_order_id)`, TTL from `external_broker.order_lease_ttl_seconds` (default 45s); every subsequent DB write is inside `lease.fenced_write()` which does `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` + fresh-clock generation verify before yielding (`846-862`).    4. Fingerprint history verified (`_verify_fingerprint_history` `759-774`), baseline required (`_require_reconciliation_baseline`), preview validated.    5. `_reserve_daily_notional` (`230-344`) computes `reservation_date = now.astimezone(UTC).date().isoformat()`, opens its OWN `transaction(conn)` (`begin_immediate`), does a sum-against-cap read, inserts the new RESERVED row, and commits. Uniqueness enforced by `idx_paper_external_attempt_one_active` (schema `1101-1105`).    6. `_submit_once` (`1188-1286`) then, inside a SEPARATE `lease.fenced_write()`: writes cash/share reservation + SUBMISSION_REQUESTED event and commits.    7. Calls broker via `runtime.submit_limit_order`. If that call raises, `_record_unknown` (`1171-1185`) appends STATE_UNKNOWN and settles the daily reservation to RECONCILIATION_REQUIRED via `_settle_daily_reservation` (`347-374`).  RECONCILIATION LOOKUP (may run on day 2) - `reconcile_external_paper_order` -> `_reconcile_locked` -> `_run_reconciliation` (`1959-2199`), inside a fresh order-scope lease. - Calls read-only `runtime.get_order_by_client_order_id`. Records evidence in `paper_external_order_lookups` including `ambiguous_event_id`, `attempt_number`, `payload_hash`, `account_fingerprint`, `authoritative` (only true when order is None AND no lookup errors) (`2083-2095`). - If NOT_FOUND: `_reconcile_attempt_reservation(target=RECONCILIATION_REQUIRED)` (`2096-2100`, helper `377-420`). Because the row is already RECONCILIATION_REQUIRED, the transition is idempotent (`paper_books_repositories.py:1876-1880`). - If FOUND: reservation target is derived from broker state (`_reservation_target_for_broker_state` `423-428`): REJECTED -> RELEASED, UNKNOWN -> RECONCILIATION_REQUIRED, else SUBMITTED. Applied inside `lease.fenced_write` alongside the state-transition event (`2122-2137`). - Bridging: if the chain was stranded at SUBMISSION_REQUESTED after a crash, `_bridge_stranded_submission_requested` (`2024-2038`) appends a SYSTEM_RECOVERY UNKNOWN event BEFORE saving the lookup, so `retry_external_paper_order`'s later match on `ambiguous_event_id`/`attempt_number` sees the correct current event.  DAY 2 - REFRESH THE STALE PREVIEW (optional) - `refresh_retry_preview` (`external_broker.py:1699-1770`) is a read-only DB operation: requires current chain UNKNOWN, requires fresh authoritative NOT_FOUND lookup (unconsumed), acquires order-scope lease, then inserts a new approved preview row with `expires_at = now + require_recent_preview_seconds`. Never touches the runtime.  DAY 2 - CROSS-DAY RETRY (attempt_number += 1) - `retry_external_paper_order` (`1546-1632`):    1. Same intent/config/safety validation as submit.    2. Order-scope lease acquired (operation="RETRY").    3. Current event must be STATE_UNKNOWN (`RETRY_NOT_ALLOWED` otherwise) (`1558-1560`).    4. Lookup must be `authoritative=1`, `result="NOT_FOUND"`, unconsumed, and bound to current ambiguous event (matches `ambiguous_event_id`, `attempt_number`, `payload_hash`, `account_fingerprint`, `client_order_id`, `book_id`) (`1561-1575`).    5. `_safety_checks` with the confirmed-NOT_FOUND allowance for the same client_order_id (`1576-1578`).    6. Retry cap check: `retries = max event.attempt_number`; if >= `external_broker.maximum_retry_attempts` -> RETRY_LIMIT_REACHED (`1579-1583`).    7. `_account_check` + fingerprint identity match against current event + baseline required (`1584-1588`).    8. Preview lookup: any APPROVED preview row for this intent, then `_validated_preview` (still enforces `expires_at`, payload/fingerprint/config-hash match).    9. `new_attempt_number = retries + 1`.    10. `prior_reservation = load_active_attempt_reservation(client_order_id, current["attempt_number"], fingerprint, book_id)` (`1600-1609`) - must exist (else ATTEMPT_RESERVATION_MISSING); returns dup-detection error (ATTEMPT_RESERVATION_CONFLICT) if the unique-active index is bypassed and 2 lineage rows exist (`paper_books_repositories.py:1759-1775`).    11. `_reserve_daily_notional(supersede_reservation_id=prior_reservation.reservation_id, supersede_attempt_number=current.attempt_number, attempt_number=new_attempt_number)`:         - Opens its own `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction (`external_broker.py:265`).         - Uses new reservation_id derived with today's UTC reservation_date + new attempt_number.         - If that new reservation_id already exists (crash-replay), returns it after immutable-field validation (`266-273`).         - Else, verifies the supersede-target reservation still holds `client_order_id`/`attempt_number`/fingerprint/`book_id`/notional and is in `{RESERVED, RECONCILIATION_REQUIRED}` (`294-322`).         - Transitions prior to `SUPERSEDED_BY_RETRY` with `commit=False` (`323-327`).         - `sum_attempt_reservations_by_date` for TODAY only (superseded prior does not count) + new notional must fit `external_broker.maximum_daily_notional_usd`, else EXTERNAL_DAILY_NOTIONAL_LIMIT (`328-335`). Legacy-migration double-count guard in repo `1812-1831`.         - Inserts new RESERVED row; single commit atomically supersedes prior + creates new (`336-344`).    12. `_submit_once` runs (separate fenced_write) - broker submit is called only once; on ambiguous outcome the new reservation goes to RECONCILIATION_REQUIRED and STATE_UNKNOWN is appended; on success it goes to SUBMITTED and the broker-state event is appended.    13. After success, `repo.consume_external_lookup` marks the NOT_FOUND evidence used (`1623-1626`), preventing a second retry from reusing it.    14. If broker outcome != STATE_UNKNOWN, `_reconcile_locked` runs synchronously to sync state/fills.  TERMINAL RESERVATION STATES - RELEASED (definite non-executing terminal: REJECTED) or after `_release_terminal_reservation` cash/share-side sweep (`1289-1316`). - SUPERSEDED_BY_RETRY (prior attempt after a valid retry). - SUBMITTED (broker acknowledged - keeps the hold until fills clear via `apply_external_fills` `1369-1543`). - RECONCILIATION_REQUIRED is NOT terminal - retains cap load until authoritative broker outcome is applied. Immutability triggers (`paper_books_schema.py:1434-1443`) enforce only state/updated_at may change; DELETE is forbidden. | CLI: `external-paper-retry` -> `trading_research.cli.external_paper_retry_cli` -> `retry_external_paper_order` (`src/trading_research/paper_books/external_broker.py:1546`). Companion entry points: `external-paper-reconcile` -> `reconcile_external_paper_order` (`external_broker.py:~1920`); `external-paper-refresh-retry-preview` -> `refresh_retry_preview` (`external_broker.py:1699`). Day-1 origin of a cross-day flow: `external-paper-submit` -> `submit_external_paper_order` (`external_broker.py:1319`). Crash-recovery entry: `recover_stranded_submission` (`external_broker.py:1635`) - not surfaced in the CLI. | fail-closed | `reservation_id = derive_external_attempt_reservation_id(client_order_id, attempt_number, account_fingerprint, reservation_date)` (`paper_books_schema.py:1571-1585`) - SHA-256 over the canonical JSON of those four fields. It is both the primary key and the deterministic idempotency key: a replay of `_reserve_daily_notional` with the same `(client_order_id, attempt_number, fingerprint, UTC-date)` recomputes the same `reservation_id`, sees `existing is not None`, revalidates immutable fields, and returns without re-inserting (`external_broker.py:266-273`). Secondary lineage-uniqueness key: `idx_paper_external_attempt_one_active` on `(client_order_id, attempt_number, account_fingerprint, book_id)` filtered `WHERE state IN (RESERVED, RECONCILIATION_REQUIRED, SUBMITTED)` (`paper_books_schema.py:1101-1105`). External-event chain idempotency: `uq_paper_external_events_scope_sequence(book_id, client_order_id, scope_sequence)` (`schema:1069-1070`). Authoritative-evidence one-shot consumption: `paper_external_order_lookups.consumed_by_retry_event_id` transitions NULL -> event_id exactly once via a versioned trigger (`_TRIGGER_VERSIONS` v2 in `schema:1526-1534`). | Multiple, non-nested transaction boundaries within a single retry call: - T1 (order-scope lease acquisition): a single conditional UPDATE via `acquire_external_order_lease` (`external_broker.py:900-903`), autocommitted by SQLite (isolation_level=None per `storage/transactions.py:7-14`). - T2 (reservation transaction): `_reserve_daily_notional` opens its OWN `transaction(conn)` / `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` (`external_broker.py:265`) and commits after inserting the new reservation and transitioning the prior one to SUPERSEDED_BY_RETRY. Explicitly documented as never nested inside a `lease.fenced_write` (`254-260`). - T3 (SUBMISSION_REQUESTED checkpoint): first `lease.fenced_write` inside `_submit_once` (`1210-1226`) covers cash/share hold + SUBMISSION_REQUESTED event. - T4 (broker success write): second `lease.fenced_write` (`1241-1253`) writes the broker-state event, updates order status, and settles the daily reservation to SUBMITTED or RECONCILIATION_REQUIRED. - T5 (lookup consumption): third `lease.fenced_write` (`1623-1626`) marks the authoritative NOT_FOUND lookup as consumed. - T6 (reconciliation): `_run_reconciliation` runs several sequential `lease.fenced_write` transactions (`2083-2137`), each atomic; state event + reservation transition are in one commit. `fenced_write` always does `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`, then a fresh-clock lease-generation check, then yields, then commits (or rolls back on any BaseException) (`external_broker.py:846-862`). `TransactionAlreadyActiveError` (`storage/transactions.py:26-53`) prohibits nesting - inner participants pass `commit=False`. | - SUBMISSION_REQUESTED stranded chains (crash between the `_submit_once` fenced_write commit and any broker response commit): `recover_stranded_submission` (`external_broker.py:1635-1696`) re-acquires the order lease and delegates to `_reconcile_locked`. On authoritative FOUND: normalizes state, applies fills, releases the reservation appropriately. On NOT_FOUND / timeout / malformed response: `_bridge_stranded_submission_requested` (`2024-2038`) writes a SYSTEM_RECOVERY UNKNOWN event first (so the lookup evidence references the freshly appended event id), the lookup evidence is saved, and reservation transitions to RECONCILIATION_REQUIRED. That unblocks the ordinary retry path. - Preview TTL expired for a confirmed-safe-to-retry order: `refresh_retry_preview` writes a fresh approved preview row without touching the runtime (`1699-1770`). - Legacy reservation rows (pre-attempt schema): `_backfill_active_legacy_external_reservations` + `_enforce_one_active_external_attempt_reservation` at schema application time (`paper_books_schema.py:1588-1650, 1676-1697`); `_sync_legacy_attempt_zero_reservation` keeps the deprecated legacy row in sync so the daily sum cannot double-count during a rolling upgrade (`repo:1886-1904`). - Broken reservation lineage (multiple active rows for the same attempt tuple): `load_active_attempt_reservation` fails closed with `AttemptReservationIntegrityError`/`ATTEMPT_RESERVATION_CONFLICT` (`repo:1759-1775`), which surfaces as `ExternalPaperError` and blocks the retry. - Reconciliation catchall: `_reconcile_locked` guarantees a `paper_external_reconciliations` row is persisted even for unexpected exceptions (`1975-1986`). | Suspected atomicity gap between the daily-notional reservation commit and the SUBMISSION_REQUESTED event commit on retry. `_reserve_daily_notional` opens and commits its OWN `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction (`external_broker.py:254-260, 265`), so on retry the prior attempt-N reservation is atomically transitioned to SUPERSEDED_BY_RETRY and the new attempt-(N+1) RESERVED row is inserted in T2, THEN a separate `_submit_once` fenced_write commits SUBMISSION_REQUESTED in T3 (`1610-1622`, first fenced_write at `1210-1226`). A process crash between T2 and T3 leaves the event chain still at STATE_UNKNOWN at attempt N while attempt N's reservation is already SUPERSEDED_BY_RETRY and attempt (N+1)'s reservation exists in RESERVED. On restart `recover_stranded_submission` refuses (chain is not at SUBMISSION_REQUESTED, `1679-1684`), and a fresh `retry_external_paper_order` fails at `load_active_attempt_reservation(current["attempt_number"]=N, ...)` because the prior row is now SUPERSEDED_BY_RETRY -> raises ATTEMPT_RESERVATION_MISSING (`1600-1609`). The stranded new reservation still counts against the day's cap. NeedsVerification via an integration test that crashes between the two commits; the in-code docstring's atomicity claim (`1199-1208`) refers only to the cash/share hold + event pair, not the daily-notional reservation.; Suspected UTC-date carry-forward gap in `_reserve_daily_notional` (`external_broker.py:280-293`). When an active RESERVED reservation exists for the same `(client_order_id, attempt_number, fingerprint, book_id)` but a DIFFERENT `reservation_date`, the function returns the prior-day row unchanged and never charges today's cap. Combined with the day-1 flow where an initial submit can create a RESERVED row but the SUBMISSION_REQUESTED commit that follows lives in a separate transaction (see previous finding), a partial write could leave attempt-0 RESERVED against yesterday's date. A same-attempt replay today would carry forward that reservation and not consume today's cap even though today's broker submission is the one that actually runs. NeedsVerification against tests to confirm whether attempt-0 can legitimately reach today without a prior SUBMISSION_REQUESTED event.; Suspected staleness of the retry-attempt counter after a crash-bridged UNKNOWN event. In `retry_external_paper_order` (`1579-1581`) `retries = max(event["attempt_number"] for event in list_external_order_events(...))`. If a crash before T3 leaves attempt (N+1) with no event but with an existing RESERVED reservation, a fresh retry recomputes `new_attempt_number = N+1` and hits the deterministic reservation_id in the existing-row fast path (`266-273`) - correct - but the prior-active check at `1600-1609` still targets attempt N whose reservation is now SUPERSEDED_BY_RETRY, and the gate blocks (see first finding). Operators have no un-jam path short of manually mutating the reservation row, which the immutability triggers explicitly forbid (`paper_books_schema.py:1441-1443`). NeedsVerification. |
| Local paper intent creation & approval workflow (isolated paper_books subsystem, Milestone 8/8.1/9). Sequence: (1) A scheduled research cycle previously froze one or two per-arm recommendations (baseline/enhanced) and persisted them in `recommendations` (see `recommendations/builder.py`; not entered again here). (2) `paper_books/scheduled_integration.py::integrate_scheduled_cycle_into_paper_books` is the primary entry point (also called from `paper_books/lifecycle.py::run_paper_book_lifecycle` when `integrate_cycle_ids` is supplied, and mirrored by `paper_books/cli_support.py::paper_book_run_cycle_cli` for fixture-mode CLI). (3) It enumerates each symbol row from `research_cycles`, precomputes deterministic `paper_order_intent_id`s for each arm (`derive_paper_order_intent_id(recommendation_id, book_id, EXECUTION_VERSION)` in `paper_books/models.py:98-103`), persists the experiment assignment (`paper_books/experiment_assignment.py::save_assignment`), then per (arm, book) calls `_process_arm` (scheduled_integration.py:220-411). (4) `_process_arm` runs a cascade of authoritative validations: `is_book_enabled`, `may_submit`, presence/shape of recommendation (`load_recommendation`), symbol/`as_of` alignment, active buy_candidate side, evidence-snapshot presence + symbol match + `screening_completeness == STATUS_COMPLETE_FOR_SCREENING`. (5) The book is lazily opened idempotently via `cash_ledger.open_book` (cash_ledger.py:71-88). (6) A per-`as_of` portfolio snapshot is built (`valuation.build_portfolio_snapshot`), a point-in-time-safe reference price is chosen (`valuation.select_valuation_price`), and if ATR sizing is enabled the requested quantity is capped by `compute_atr_position_plan`. (7) If authoritative daily-risk is enabled, `reconciliation.reconcile_book` + `daily_risk.calculate_and_persist_daily_risk_state` are computed and `save_economic_blackout_decision` is written. (8) `risk.evaluate_paper_risk` (risk.py:69-191) performs pure deterministic sizing, always producing a `PaperRiskDecision` (APPROVED/APPROVED_REDUCED or one of many REJECTED codes) — this is the authoritative approval step. (9) `order_intent.persist_risk_decision` (order_intent.py:24-39) writes the decision row (idempotent by hashed decision id — `sha256(book_id:cycle_id:recommendation_id:symbol)[:32]` — regardless of approval outcome, giving a queryable audit trail for rejects too), and if the reject code is DAILY_LOSS/DRAWDOWN a `safety_pause.pause_for_risk_state` is applied. (10) If decision is rejected (not in APPROVED_RISK_DECISIONS), the outcome is OUTCOME_REJECTED_BY_RISK and no intent is built. (11) If approved, `order_intent.build_order_intent` (order_intent.py:42-58) constructs a `PaperBookOrderIntent` — BUY-side LIMIT only, `limit_price = approved_notional_usd / approved_quantity`, notional derived from approved_notional_usd, deterministic `paper_order_intent_id`; frozen dataclass validates positivity/whole-share/tz-awareness/notional=qty*price (models.py:240-294). (12) Routing branch: (a) External-broker route (`cfg.external_broker.enabled` and this book is in `enabled_book_ids`) — `persist_order_intent` writes the intent as PENDING_SUBMISSION (no reservation, no claim), then `enqueue_external_submission` with deterministic queue_id `peqs_ + sha256(book_id:intent_id:scheduled)[:40]`; outcome is AWAITING_OPERATOR_EXTERNAL_SUBMISSION (out of scope for local approval). (b) Local route with no market-simulation input available — `persist_order_intent` writes intent as PENDING_SUBMISSION and outcome is INTENT_CREATED_PENDING_FILL (no reservation, no claim). (c) Local route with market input — `execution.submit_and_simulate` (paper_books/execution.py:99-232) atomically (`transaction(conn)` = BEGIN IMMEDIATE) claims LOCAL_SIMULATED namespace, inserts the intent, and (BUY only) inserts a `reserve:{intent_id}` cash-ledger row via `cash_ledger.reserve_for_order` (which itself takes a book-scoped BEGIN IMMEDIATE if not already inside a transaction); it then simulates a fill deterministically and commits fill + position + settlement + release + status=FILLED in a second atomic transaction. (13) Per-book reconciliations run at the end of `integrate_scheduled_cycle_into_paper_books` for every touched book. All persistence is SQLite; every save is idempotent by deterministic primary key or `IF EXISTS` guard. | Primary entry point: `paper_books.scheduled_integration.integrate_scheduled_cycle_into_paper_books` (src/trading_research/paper_books/scheduled_integration.py:414-523). Called by the lifecycle service `paper_books.lifecycle.run_paper_book_lifecycle` (src/trading_research/paper_books/lifecycle.py:536-663, iteration loop at 561-569) and by the recurring scheduler `paper_books.recurring_scheduler.run_recurring_paper_scheduler` (src/trading_research/paper_books/recurring_scheduler.py:872). CLI (fixture-mode) equivalent: `paper_books.cli_support.paper_book_run_cycle_cli` (src/trading_research/paper_books/cli_support.py:140-219), wired through `cli.py:3365-3369` under the `paper-book-run-cycle` subcommand.
