Same recommendation submitted to two different books produces two different intent IDs. - `risk_decision_id`: `pb-risk-` + `sha256(book_id:cycle_id:recommendation_id:symbol)[:32]` (order_intent.py:31-34). - Cash-ledger idempotency: `reserve:{paper_order_intent_id}` (cash_ledger.py:124) and `release:{intent_id}:{reason}` (cash_ledger.py:159); enforced by `paper_book_cash_ledger` UNIQUE `(book_id, idempotency_key)` via `cash_ledger_entry_exists` (paper_books_repositories.py:107-131). - Order-intent row: primary-key uniqueness on `(book_id, paper_order_intent_id)` — `save_order_intent` returns False for a duplicate (paper_books_repositories.py:190-207). - External submission queue: `queue_id = "peqs_" + sha256(f"{book_id}:{intent_id}:scheduled")[:40]` (scheduled_integration.py:371-373); lifecycle mirrors it with `":lifecycle"`/`":exit"` suffixes. - Fill idempotency: `fill_id = "pb-fill-{paper_order_intent_id}"` (execution.py:91) with `fill_exists` guards on both sides of the fill transaction (execution.py:181-198). | The approval step (`persist_risk_decision`) is its OWN top-level commit (paper_books_repositories.py:164 unconditionally calls `conn.commit()` after inserting the decision) — deliberately, so a rejected decision has a durable audit row even if downstream fails. The PENDING intent-create-only path (`persist_order_intent` on lines 370 and 387 of scheduled_integration.py, and lines 458 and 475 of lifecycle.py) is likewise a single-statement autocommit via `save_order_intent`'s default `commit=True` (paper_books_repositories.py:206). The atomic transactional boundary is inside `execution.submit_and_simulate`: - New-intent path: one `transaction(conn)` context (execution.py:119-133) commits {namespace claim, intent row, BUY reservation ledger row} atomically. This closes the pre-Item-6 gap where a crash between insert and reservation could leave an un-reserved BUY intent. - Fill path: a second explicit `begin_immediate` block (execution.py:194-231) commits {fill row, position/lot update, settlement ledger entry, fee/slippage entries, reservation release, order status=FILLED} atomically or rolls back on any exception. - Cash reservation `reserve_for_order` (cash_ledger.py:111-134) uses `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` book-scoped serialization when `commit=True` so concurrent BUYs can't both observe headroom. - `storage/transactions.py::begin_immediate` refuses to enter a nested transaction — instead raises `TransactionAlreadyActiveError` — so nested calls must use `commit=False` and participate in the caller's transaction. | Resumability is achieved through deterministic IDs and idempotent inserts: - A retried `_process_arm` will read the same recommendation, produce the same `risk_decision_id` and `paper_order_intent_id`, and inserts are no-ops. - Intent inserted but no reservation: `submit_and_simulate`'s "existing" branch attempts a lazy `claim_execution_namespace` (only if `has_external_execution_evidence` is false) and then verifies `remaining_buy_reservation == intent.notional_usd` — if the pre-existing BUY intent has status PENDING_SUBMISSION and no reservation, it FAILS CLOSED rather than fabricating one (execution.py:158-168). - Interrupted fill: on replay, `fill_exists` inside and outside the atomic block short-circuits to a `{status:FILLED, fill:None}` idempotent no-op (execution.py:181, 196-198). - Cash-ledger release keyed by fill_id (`release_settled_buy_reservation`, cash_ledger.py:186-200) never double-releases. - Lifecycle-level "one book's failure never mutates the other" (lifecycle.py:643-645) means an in-progress arm crash rolls back inside its own transaction while the other arm proceeds. - Recurring scheduler: `TERMINAL_RUN_STATUSES` check returns cached results (recurring_scheduler.py:910-922), so a restart after termination doesn't re-run the slot. | NeedsVerification: `_process_arm` writes a BUY intent with **no** cash reservation on the `OUTCOME_INTENT_CREATED_PENDING_FILL` branch when the market-simulation input is unavailable (scheduled_integration.py:385-391, `persist_order_intent(conn, intent)` alone, no `reserve_for_order`). On a subsequent invocation (e.g. next lifecycle `_process_pending_orders` at lifecycle.py:187-232), `submit_and_simulate` takes the `existing is not None` branch (execution.py:117-168) and — because `existing['side']==BUY` and status==PENDING_SUBMISSION — evaluates `remaining_buy_reservation(...) != intent.notional_usd` (0 vs positive notional), which raises `FillSimulationError('existing reservation does not match the frozen intent notional -- refusing to proceed rather than fabricate a release or adjustment')`. That would strand any BUY intent that was ever persisted via the no-market-input path and later re-processed. This appears to contradict Milestone 11.3.1 Item 6 Part A's stated intent that a new intent's insert + BUY reservation be atomic — the guarantee only holds when `submit_and_simulate` created the intent, not when `persist_order_intent` did. Worth deeper investigation via tests to confirm reachability. Files: src/trading_research/paper_books/scheduled_integration.py:385-391 and lifecycle.py:458 (also mirrored at 475), vs src/trading_research/paper_books/execution.py:158-168.; NeedsVerification: `paper_books.risk.evaluate_paper_risk` accepts `context.valuation_status in (VALUATION_COMPLETE, VALUATION_PARTIAL_STALE_PRICE)` (risk.py:139-144) — i.e. it will approve sizing off a portfolio whose valuation is only 'partial stale'. Combined with the fact that `_process_arm`'s ATR gate demands a live entry-day bar (scheduled_integration.py:125 requires `bars[-1].session_date == as_of.date()`), the two staleness policies are slightly asymmetric: the same run can reject on ATR staleness but approve on stale portfolio valuation. Worth confirming this is intentional per ADR 0006.; NeedsVerification: `order_intent.build_order_intent` derives `limit_price = approved_notional_usd / approved_quantity` (order_intent.py:50) as an exact Decimal division. Because `risk.evaluate_paper_risk` computes `final_notional = approved_quantity * reference_price` (risk.py:183), the round-trip `final_notional / approved_quantity == reference_price` holds exactly; but `PaperBookOrderIntent.__post_init__` re-checks `notional_usd == quantity * limit_price` (models.py:277). This is only guaranteed if Decimal division of `(qty*reference_price)/qty` returns bit-identical `reference_price` — which it does for finite-quantity integer division. However, if any upstream ATR/policy path in the future substitutes a different `approved_notional_usd` that is not an exact multiple of `approved_quantity`, the constructor will fail closed. Not a bug today, but a brittleness worth noting as construction is one hop removed from the sizing math. |
| Fresh research invocation flows: a CLI or scheduler builds an EvidenceSnapshot for one symbol and calls the only provider-invoking function analyze_with_research_committee (src/trading_research/research/orchestration.py:691-924). (1) Fail-closed pre-check at orchestration.py:727-733: when require_decision=True and MANAGER_ROLE not in configuration.roles, raise ManagerNotConfiguredError before any provider call. (2) Compute deterministic research_run_id = 'run-' + sha256(snapshot_id, provider, model_name, roles, run_mode, config_hash, per-role prompt versions+hashes, system_prompt_hash)[:32] via compute_research_run_id (orchestration.py:214-236, invoked at 735-738). (3) Reuse gate at orchestration.py:740-762: if research_repository.get_run_status returns RUN_STATUS_COMPLETED, RUN_STATUS_ANALYSIS_INCOMPLETE, or RUN_STATUS_ANALYST_REPORTS_COMPLETE_NO_MANAGER, return persisted decision/role_reports with reused_existing_run=True and no provider call is made. RUN_STATUS_RUNNING and RUN_STATUS_FAILED are NOT short-circuited. (4) Snapshot preflight validate_snapshot_preconditions (orchestration.py:764-767). (5) Persist run header only when get_run_status returned None: save_run_started INSERTs a research_committee_runs row with status='RUNNING' using INSERT OR IGNORE and commits (research_repositories.py:173-183; schema at research_schema.py:37-48). If preflight rejected, mark_run_finished(RUN_STATUS_ANALYSIS_INCOMPLETE) and return with incomplete_reasons (orchestration.py:775-782). (6) For each analyst role (excluding manager) the orchestrator queries role reports already persisted for (research_run_id, role); if present it reuses that report; otherwise it calls _run_role_with_retries (orchestration.py:790-839). (7) _run_role_with_retries (orchestration.py:352-673) loops up to configuration.max_attempts_per_role. Each attempt: build system+user prompt via build_system_prompt/build_user_prompt with the JSON schema chosen by _schema_for_role, form a ResearchModelRequest, derive attempt_id via _attempt_id (research_run_id-role-attempt_number, with a scheduler_run_id suffix for scheduled correlation, orchestration.py:245-252). If an attempt_controller is supplied (shadow/attempt_controller.py adapter), invoke before_attempt; a not-allowed decision persists a STAGE_BUDGET_GATED / role-gated / paused-or-killed failure row, records a synthetic ResearchAttemptRecord with usage marked USAGE_NOT_RETURNED, breaks the retry loop, and never calls the provider (orchestration.py:395-442). Otherwise the provider call is provider.generate_structured(request) or, when a ResearchTokenBudgetController is injected, token_budget_controller.invoke(provider, request, provider_name=...) (orchestration.py:445-450). (8) Provider-side (e.g., AnthropicResearchProvider.generate_structured, anthropic_provider.py:102-233) forces a strict tool_use structured output, catches APITimeout/RateLimit/APIConnection/APIStatus into ProviderTimeout/RateLimit/Transient/Unavailable errors, validates that exactly one matching tool_use block was returned, extracts usage tokens, and returns a ResearchModelResponse. Deterministic and Claude-code/Codex providers implement the same protocol (research/provider_protocol.py:17-27). (9) Exception handling in the orchestrator: ProviderUnavailableError -> record failure, break (non-retryable termination, orchestration.py:451-474); ProviderTimeoutError/RateLimit/Transient/Malformed/SchemaValidation -> classify to structured failures via _failure_from_exc/schema_errors, decide retry eligibility from the structured failure's retryable flag (Milestone 12.1.1 change: not from the exception class), rebuild validation_feedback and continue, else break (orchestration.py:475-522). (10) Successful response is fed to build_decision/build_role_report + validate_decision/validate_role_report; EvidenceValidationError/SchemaValidationError produce classified retryable failures; claim-level failures produce STAGE_CLAIM_EVIDENCE_VALIDATION rows (orchestration.py:524-629, _claim_validation_failures at 314-349). Each attempt appends a ResearchAttemptRecord to the in-memory list; the outer analyze_with_research_committee then persists them one-by-one via save_attempt (research_repositories.py:185-219, orchestration.py:807-811). (11) A validated analyst report is persisted with save_role_report (research_repositories.py:248-258) and appended to valid_reports. If any required analyst role produced no valid report, the orchestrator appends a CODE_MISSING_REQUIRED_ROLE failure, records a manager-skipped failure (only when a manager was configured, orchestration.py:850-863), calls mark_run_finished(RUN_STATUS_ANALYSIS_INCOMPLETE), and returns without ever calling the manager (orchestration.py:841-871). (12) When all analysts succeeded and no manager is configured (require_decision=False), the run finishes with RUN_STATUS_ANALYST_REPORTS_COMPLETE_NO_MANAGER (orchestration.py:873-887). Otherwise _run_role_with_retries is invoked for MANAGER_ROLE (orchestration.py:889-901) with valid_reports fed to the prompt. (13) On a valid ResearchDecision, save_decision persists a research_decisions row (research_repositories.py:260-270) and mark_run_finished writes RUN_STATUS_COMPLETED (or RUN_STATUS_ANALYSIS_INCOMPLETE when decision.rating=='ANALYSIS_INCOMPLETE'); the OrchestrationResult is returned with attempts, failures, role_reports, decision (orchestration.py:913-923). The scheduled cycle wrapper run_scheduled_research_cycle/_run_symbol (scheduled_cycle.py:318-412, 415-588) is the outermost orchestrator that computes cycle_id, persists evidence snapshot, computes evidence-completeness gate, calls analyze_with_research_committee when the gate does not block (scheduled_cycle.py:516-521), records provider provenance rows (scheduled_cycle.py:526-546), applies overlay/save_overlay_decision, saves frozen recommendations, and only then optionally submits a baseline paper order. | Programmatic entry: analyze_with_research_committee at src/trading_research/research/orchestration.py:691. Scheduler/cycle entry: run_scheduled_research_cycle at src/trading_research/research/scheduled_cycle.py:318 which calls analyze_with_research_committee via _run_symbol at scheduled_cycle.py:516. | fail-closed | Deterministic research_run_id = 'run-' + sha256(snapshot_id, provider_name, model_name, roles, run_mode, config_hash, per-role {version,text_hash}, system_prompt_hash)[:32] (orchestration.py:214-236). Attempt idempotency key is _attempt_id(research_run_id, role, attempt_number[, scheduler_run_id]) (orchestration.py:245-252). Downstream storage keys: research_committee_runs.research_run_id PRIMARY KEY (research_schema.py:38); research_attempts.attempt_id PRIMARY KEY (research_schema.py:52); research_role_reports.report_id PK + UNIQUE(research_run_id, role) (research_schema.py:99-108); research_decisions.decision_id PK + UNIQUE research_run_id (research_schema.py:112-119); research_attempt_failures.failure_id deterministic via failure_taxonomy.new_failure and INSERT OR IGNORE (research_repositories.py:221-239). save_run_started uses INSERT OR IGNORE (research_repositories.py:177-182). | No overarching transaction. SQLiteResearchRepository calls self._conn.commit() after every single write: save_run_started (research_repositories.py:183), save_attempt (:219), save_attempt_failure (:239), save_role_report (:258), save_decision (:270), mark_run_finished (:277). The orchestrator issues these commits one at a time as it progresses through analysts and manager (orchestration.py:807-811, 826-827, 838-839, 862-865, 897-901, 914-917). Consequences: (i) an attempt row is durably visible before the failure rows tied to it; (ii) if the process crashes between save_role_report and mark_run_finished the run stays at status='RUNNING' with no cleanup on resume. There is no BEGIN IMMEDIATE / lease / row-level lock in the research repository (verified by absence of any lease/lock/BEGIN/isolation_level references in research_repositories.py). | Resume behavior when a prior invocation crashed mid-run: (i) if the run reached COMPLETED / ANALYSIS_INCOMPLETE / ANALYST_REPORTS_COMPLETE_NO_MANAGER, the reuse gate returns the persisted result with reused_existing_run=True and no provider call is made (orchestration.py:740-762). (ii) If it is still RUNNING or FAILED, the code re-enters preflight and, for each analyst role, checks research_repository.get_role_reports_for_run and reuses any (research_run_id, role) role report already persisted (orchestration.py:791-796); un-covered roles re-run through _run_role_with_retries. save_run_started uses INSERT OR IGNORE so re-entering does not overwrite the header. Attempt IDs remain deterministic per (run, role, attempt_number), so save_attempt on a re-entry will raise UniqueConstraint on the primary key (research_attempts.attempt_id) since save_attempt uses plain INSERT (research_repositories.py:187-197) rather than INSERT OR IGNORE — potential resume defect. There is no scavenger/janitor for orphan RUNNING rows. | Save_attempt uses plain INSERT (research_repositories.py:187) — a resume of a still-RUNNING run whose _attempt_id collides with an already-persisted attempt will raise sqlite3.IntegrityError inside analyze_with_research_committee. save_run_started (:178), save_attempt_failure (:226), save_role_report (:250), save_decision (:262) all use INSERT OR IGNORE, so save_attempt is the odd one out. Worth deeper investigation given the design intent that attempts be idempotently replayable on resume.; No lease or concurrency protection on research_committee_runs (research_repositories.py:152-277). Two processes with the same research_run_id can both pass the reuse gate (both see status NULL or RUNNING) and race to call the provider; save_run_started's INSERT OR IGNORE only deduplicates the header row, not the provider calls or the attempt inserts. mark_run_finished blindly overwrites status/completed_at with no WHERE status='RUNNING' predicate (research_repositories.py:272-277). No mitigation via SQLite BEGIN IMMEDIATE is visible.; RUN_STATUS_FAILED constant is declared (orchestration.py:60) but never written. A process crash between save_run_started and any terminal mark_run_finished leaves an orphan row at status='RUNNING' forever; there is no janitor and no code path that transitions RUNNING -> FAILED, so run bookkeeping is silently misleading after crashes. The reuse gate (orchestration.py:740-762) also does not act on RUNNING/FAILED, so a fresh caller will re-enter and attempt to re-insert attempts (interacts with finding #1). |
| Research-reuse (reused vs fresh, budget accounting) is implemented as two loosely-coupled layers, both anchored to the same SQLite database.  LAYER 1 - Pre-flight shortlist gate (strategies/research_budget.py) - `decide_research_action(entry, state, config)` (src/trading_research/strategies/research_budget.py:62-90) is a pure deterministic function that returns one of SEND_TO_RESEARCH / SKIP_LOW_SIGNAL / SKIP_DAILY_CANDIDATE_CAP / SKIP_DAILY_TOKEN_CAP based on caller-supplied in-memory `DailyResearchBudgetState` (symbols_sent_today, fresh_cycles_today, tokens_spent_today, daily_token_budget) (:40-52) and `ShortlistConfig` (config/strategies.yaml:62-64 loaded by strategies/config.py:120-138,265-279). - After the orchestrator is invoked, `record_research_outcome(symbol, reused_existing_run)` (:93-98) returns REUSE_RESEARCH vs RUN_FULL_RESEARCH, derived from `OrchestrationResult.reused_existing_run` (research/orchestration.py:193, 747/754/761 = True, 781/869/885/910/922 = False). - These two functions are unreferenced in src/ (only tests/unit/test_research_budget.py imports them); the scheduled cycle does not call the pre-flight gate.  LAYER 2 - Orchestrator run-identity reuse (research/orchestration.py) - `compute_research_run_id` (:214-236) hashes {snapshot_id, provider, model, roles, run_mode, config_hash, prompt versions/hashes, system_prompt_hash} to a stable `run-<sha256[:32]>` identity. Prompt-version bump or config-hash change forces a fresh run. - `analyze_with_research_committee` (:691-924) queries `ResearchRepository.get_run_status(research_run_id)` at :741. If status is COMPLETED, ANALYSIS_INCOMPLETE, or ANALYST_REPORTS_COMPLETE_NO_MANAGER, it returns the persisted decision/role_reports with `reused_existing_run=True` and never calls the provider, never invokes the token-budget controller, never invokes the attempt controller (:742-762). - Otherwise it calls `save_run_started(...)` (:769-773) with INSERT OR IGNORE (storage/research_repositories.py:173-183) and runs analyst roles then the manager. Per-role reuse also happens at :791-797 - a role whose report was previously persisted is short-circuited before any provider call. Terminal state is written by `mark_run_finished` (:777, 865, 881, 905, 917; storage/research_repositories.py:272-277).  LAYER 3 - Persistent token budget (strategies/research_budget.py + research/token_budget.py) - Wired via `ResearchTokenBudgetController` Protocol (research/orchestration.py:172-181) and only called inside `_run_role_with_retries` when the controller is provided (:445-449). `PersistentResearchTokenBudgetController` (research/token_budget.py:22-97) is the concrete implementation. - Per attempt: `reserve_research_tokens` (strategies/research_budget.py:235-340) is called with `research_attempt_identity=f"{role}:{attempt_number}"` (token_budget.py:49). Idempotency key = `research_token_budget:{run_id}:{symbol}:{provider}:{model}:{attempt_identity}:{utc_date}` (:158-166). - Inside a `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction (storage/transactions.py:38-74) it: (a) looks up existing key via `load_budget_reservation_by_idempotency_key` and returns the existing row if compatible (:281-291); (b) scans `list_budget_reservations(conn)` for any AMBIGUOUS row sharing the run/symbol/provider/model prefix and rejects with TOKEN_RESERVATION_RECONCILIATION_REQUIRED (:292-309); (c) rejects if any RESEARCH_TOKENS row for the same budget_date has emergency_margin_breached=1 (:215-221, 310-314); (d) sums live counted tokens (RESERVED + SETTLED + AMBIGUOUS) for the UTC date (:203-212), rejects if requested_total > cap (:315-321); (e) INSERTs a new `shadow_budget_reservations` row with `reservation_kind='RESEARCH_TOKENS'`, `budget_date`, `token_accounting_policy`, status=RESERVED (:322-334). - Then invokes `provider.generate_structured` (token_budget.py:66). `ProviderUnavailableError` -> `release_research_tokens` (:70-71 -> strategies/research_budget.py:413-421 RESERVED->RELEASED). Any other BaseException including timeouts, malformed output, process interruption -> `mark_research_tokens_ambiguous` (:72-77 -> strategies/research_budget.py:424-431 RESERVED->AMBIGUOUS). Missing authoritative usage -> mark AMBIGUOUS + raise ProviderUnavailableError TOKEN_USAGE_EVIDENCE_REQUIRED (:79-85). Otherwise `settle_research_tokens` (:86-93 -> strategies/research_budget.py:366-410 RESERVED->SETTLED with consumed_* columns and breached flag). - Reused runs skip the controller entirely: `analyze_with_research_committee`'s early return path at :742-762 fires before `_run_role_with_retries` is ever entered, so a reused run consumes zero token budget. | Production entry: `research/scheduled_cycle.py:516` (`analyze_with_research_committee(...)` inside the per-symbol cycle loop) and `cli.py:833` (single-snapshot `analyze` command). Both eventually reach `research/orchestration.py::analyze_with_research_committee` (line 691). The pre-flight shortlist gate `strategies/research_budget.py::decide_research_action` has no production caller in src/. The persistent token-budget wrapper `research/token_budget.py::PersistentResearchTokenBudgetController` has no production instantiation in src/ (only tests/unit/test_research_orchestration.py:401). | fail-closed | Two independent keys operate at different layers. - Research-run identity (reuse key): `compute_research_run_id` = `run-<sha256[:32]>` over sorted-JSON of {snapshot_id, provider, model_name, roles, run_mode, config_hash, per-role prompt version+hash, system_prompt_hash} (research/orchestration.py:214-236). | External paper submission (isolated Alpaca-paper account) is the "external" execution namespace inside the paper_books module. It is orchestrated in-process by the trading_research host via a Protocol-defined runtime client (ExternalPaperRuntime) that speaks normalized JSON to the isolated `trading_paper_runtime` sibling process. The end-to-end flow is:  1. Operator preview (`preview_external_paper_order`, src/trading_research/paper_books/external_broker.py:918-985)    - Validates config/book (line 486-499), loads and freezes intent (_intent, 502-582): checks terminal-state exclusivity (519-524), execution-namespace claim (530-535), book ACTIVE (537-538), risk/exit decision approval (539-552), order type/TIF (553-556), whole-share quantity/side (557-561), notional recomputation and caps (_validate_frozen_notional, 142-173), timestamp freshness (563-575), oversell against confirmed available position (576-580).    - Runs `_safety_checks` (585-607): shadow pause not blocking, no unresolved CRITICAL alert, no active-critical external reconciliation (unless explicit retry evidence).    - Derives immutable `client_order_id` = "epb-{book_id}-" + sha256(book_id, intent_id, symbol, side, qty, limit_price, POLICY_VERSION) (derive_external_order_identity, 431-444). This is the durable idempotency key for the whole flow.    - Acquires order-scope lease via `_order_lease` (865-916, backed by acquire_external_order_lease at storage/paper_books_repositories.py:2035-2062).    - Fenced write: claim EXECUTION_NAMESPACE_EXTERNAL (940-947), verify runtime account_check + fingerprint history (610-623, 759-774), call runtime.preview_limit_order, persist paper_external_order_previews row + append event STATE_PREVIEWED atomically (978-984).  2. Operator submit (`submit_external_paper_order`, 1319-1366)    - Re-validates config/book/intent, runs `_safety_checks` (before lease acquisition; not re-run inside lease).    - Acquires order-scope lease, runs account_check, verifies fingerprint history, requires reconciliation baseline (_require_reconciliation_baseline, 729-745).    - `_validated_preview` (988-1008): confirms an APPROVED preview exists, matches book/intent/client_order_id/payload_hash/fingerprint, not expired, matching config_hash.    - Idempotency guard on the event chain (1340-1348): if current state is UNKNOWN raises AMBIGUOUS_SUBMISSION; if current state is not PREVIEWED, performs `runtime.get_order_by_client_order_id` and returns the existing state rather than resubmitting.    - Reservation (attempt_number=0): `_reserve_daily_notional` (230-344) opens its own BEGIN IMMEDIATE transaction (per docstring 253-260), reserves account-wide daily notional against `paper_external_attempt_reservations` (storage/paper_books_schema.py:937-948) whose PK is a deterministic hash of (client_order_id, attempt_number, account_fingerprint, reservation_date). Sums existing non-released reservations against cfg.external_broker.maximum_daily_notional_usd and inserts a RESERVED row.    - `_submit_once` (1188-1286):      a) Under `lease.fenced_write`, atomically records the cash/share reservation (cash_ledger.reserve_for_order or positions.reserve_shares_for_sell) plus the STATE_SUBMISSION_REQUESTED event (Milestone 11.3 Part 36/37 comment at 1199-1209).      b) Calls `runtime.submit_limit_order` OUTSIDE any fenced_write.      c) On success: validates response shape (_validate_order_response, 1118-1168), maps status via `_state_from_order`, fenced_write appends the new event, updates paper_book_orders status, and settles the reservation via `_settle_daily_reservation` (347-374). Then runs `apply_external_fills` (1369-1543) inside a `_fenced_or_plain_write`, applying fills to paper_external_broker_fills + paper_book_fills + cash_ledger + positions + lifecycle rows; releases remaining reservation if fully filled.      d) On runtime exception (RuntimeUnavailableError/Timeout/OperationError, or any Exception): calls `_record_unknown` (1171-1185) which appends STATE_UNKNOWN and moves the reservation to RECONCILIATION_REQUIRED (never RELEASED — the hold on the daily cap is retained). Returns status=UNKNOWN.    - On non-UNKNOWN outcome (1360-1365) calls `_reconcile_locked` post-submit to snapshot broker positions/account, validate fills, and mark any critical discrepancy.  3. Recovery / retry    - `recover_stranded_submission` (1635-1696): read-only broker lookup path for chains stuck at STATE_SUBMISSION_REQUESTED after a crash; delegates to `_reconcile_locked`, which bridges to STATE_UNKNOWN via `_bridge_stranded_submission_requested` (2024-2038, 2263-2273).    - `retry_external_paper_order` (1546-1632): requires current=STATE_UNKNOWN plus a fresh unconsumed authoritative NOT_FOUND lookup matching the exact ambiguous event/attempt/payload/fingerprint (1561-1575); enforces retry limit; re-validates preview; loads prior reservation and calls `_reserve_daily_notional` with supersede args (atomic supersede-of-prior + creation-of-new inside one transaction). Consumes the lookup after the new event is appended (1623-1626).    - `refresh_retry_preview` (1699-1768): pure-DB preview refresh — no runtime call.  4. Reconciliation (`reconcile_external_paper_order` / `_reconcile_locked` / `_run_reconciliation`, 1925-2282) is the authoritative repair path: takes the same order-scope lease, saves a paper_external_order_lookups row with authoritative flag = (order is None and "UNKNOWN" not in statuses), transitions the reservation via `_reconcile_attempt_reservation` (377-420) to SUBMITTED/RELEASED/RECONCILIATION_REQUIRED per broker outcome, and always persists a `paper_external_reconciliations` row (2285-2303) whose `critical` flag drives BLOCKED_BY_RECONCILIATION queue status. | CLI `external-paper-submit` -> `external_paper_submit_cli` in src/trading_research/cli.py:361-370 -> `submit_external_paper_order` in src/trading_research/paper_books/external_broker.py:1319-1366. The preview counterpart is `preview_external_paper_order` (918-985). Recovery entry points: `recover_stranded_submission` (1635-1696), `retry_external_paper_order` (1546-1632), `reconcile_external_paper_order` (1925-1956), `refresh_retry_preview` (1699-1768). Runtime IPC boundary: `RuntimeClient.preview_limit_order`/`submit_limit_order`/`get_order_by_client_order_id`/`cancel_external_order`/`list_order_fills` in src/trading_research/runtime/client/process_client.py:362-395. | fail-closed | Two composed keys anchor idempotency: 1. `client_order_id` = `epb-{book_id_lower}-` + first 32 hex of sha256(book_id, paper_order_intent_id, symbol, side, quantity, limit_price, POLICY_VERSION). Source: `derive_external_order_identity`, external_broker.py:431-444. Used as the durable order identity across every operation (preview, submit, retry, cancel, reconcile, lookup, lease, reservation). Uniquely indexed via lease PK (lease_key = f"{book_id}:{client_order_id}") and the scope-sequence unique index above. 2. `reservation_id` = "resvn_" + first 40 hex of sha256(client_order_id, attempt_number, account_fingerprint, reservation_date). Source: `derive_external_attempt_reservation_id`, storage/paper_books_schema.py:1571-1585. Primary key of `paper_external_attempt_reservations`; a same-day/same-attempt replay reuses the existing row rather than creating a duplicate (external_broker.py:266-273). Runtime IPC also carries a per-call `runtime_request_id` (`m11_<uuid4>`, e.g. external_broker.py:1192) that is persisted on each event row; this is a correlation ID, not an idempotency key. | Multiple nested/sequential transaction scopes: - `_reserve_daily_notional` (external_broker.py:230-344) opens its own `with transaction(conn)` (line 265, executes BEGIN IMMEDIATE via storage.database.begin_immediate) — deliberately NOT nested inside the lease's fenced_write (docstring 253-260). This is a separately-committed transaction that runs the read-then-write daily-cap check and (optionally) supersedes the prior reservation in the same commit. - `_submit_once`'s reservation+SUBMISSION_REQUESTED write (1210-1226) is a single `lease.fenced_write()` transaction — BEGIN IMMEDIATE followed by verify_or_raise + reservation+event insert + COMMIT. Milestone 11.3 Part 36/37 documents that these two writes are now atomic (previously two independently-committed writes). - The broker submit call (`runtime.submit_limit_order`, line 1228) runs OUTSIDE any transaction (network I/O must never occur under BEGIN IMMEDIATE, per `_order_lease` docstring 891-892). - Post-response commit (1241-1253): another `lease.fenced_write()` bundles the terminal-or-transitional event, `update_order_status`, and `_settle_daily_reservation`. - `apply_external_fills` (1369-1543) uses one `_fenced_or_plain_write` per fill (1454-1484), one per state transition, and one for terminal reservation release. - Lease acquire/heartbeat/release each self-commit (paper_books_repositories.py:2055, 2077, 2121). | - `recover_stranded_submission` (1635-1696): explicit read-only recovery for chains stuck at SUBMISSION_REQUESTED after a crash. Never calls submit_limit_order or preview_limit_order; only calls get_order_by_client_order_id (via `_reconcile_locked`). Idempotent — a second call sees the chain already advanced and returns `already_recovered=True` (1690-1692). Safe from a fresh DB connection. - `_bridge_stranded_submission_requested` (2024-2038, 2263-2273): appends UNKNOWN when broker lookup produces no validated definitive state, using the ORIGINAL SUBMISSION_REQUESTED event's payload_hash and attempt_number so the resulting UNKNOWN carries the correct scope; this makes the existing retry gate reachable. - `refresh_retry_preview` (1699-1768): pure DB — replaces an expired preview for an order confirmed UNKNOWN with a fresh authoritative NOT_FOUND lookup. No broker call. Requires same NOT_FOUND-lookup preconditions and retry-limit check as retry. - `reconcile_external_paper_order` (1925-1956) is the general repair path. Every reconciliation always writes a `paper_external_reconciliations` row (2285-2303) and the reservation is re-driven to the correct state via `_reconcile_attempt_reservation` (377-420), which will not downgrade exposure (never releases an already-SUBMITTED reservation, 405-408). - Rolling-upgrade recovery: `apply_paper_books_schema` (schema 1676-1692) backfills legacy `paper_external_daily_reservations` rows into `paper_external_attempt_reservations` under BEGIN IMMEDIATE, enforcing lineage uniqueness at migration time. | `submit_external_paper_order` runs `_safety_checks` (external_broker.py:1327) BEFORE acquiring the order-scope lease at 1329, and does NOT re-run it inside the lease. A new CRITICAL alert or shadow safety-pause becoming blocking between the two calls will not stop this in-flight submit; only the durable state gates (event chain / reservation) protect the subsequent write. NeedsVerification: confirm whether that race is intentionally tolerated or whether Milestone 11.3.1 Item 4-style fencing was intended to include a re-check.; `submit_external_paper_order` at 1345-1347 raises `ORDER_MISSING_AT_BROKER` when the local chain already advanced past PREVIEWED but the broker lookup returns None. Unlike the reconciliation path, this raise does not itself persist a `paper_external_reconciliations` critical row for the caller; the invariant that 'no ambiguous outcome exits without persisted critical evidence' (Part 8 comment at 1963-1969) is upheld only when reconciliation is reached. NeedsVerification: whether the caller is required to invoke `reconcile_external_paper_order` after this raise or whether the bare raise leaves the system without recorded reconciliation evidence for this particular condition.; `_reserve_daily_notional` (230-344) commits its own BEGIN IMMEDIATE transaction (line 265) SEPARATE from `_submit_once`'s fenced_write reservation+SUBMISSION_REQUESTED transaction. If the process crashes between the reservation commit and the SUBMISSION_REQUESTED commit, an active RESERVED row exists with no matching event chain past PREVIEWED. Same-day re-invocation of `submit_external_paper_order` self-heals (existing reservation is reused at 267-273), but cross-day retry with a different reservation_date would require an explicit supersede path that only `retry_external_paper_order` provides — and that path is gated on the chain being at UNKNOWN, which this stranded state is not. NeedsVerification: whether any operational path exists to recover such an orphan reservation without operator DB intervention. |

### 6a. Workflow trace details

#### Database initialization & schema upgrade/migration for the local SQLite research/paper-trading database. On every process that opens the DB, `storage.database.connect(db_path)` is the single entry point: it opens a SQLite connection with autocommit (`isolation_level=None`), sets `busy_timeout=5000ms`, enables foreign keys, WAL journal_mode, and `synchronous=NORMAL`, then in a fixed order runs (1) `check_schema_not_forward_versioned` (creates the `schema_version` ledger if absent, and refuses to open a DB whose stored MAX(version) is > the code's `CURRENT_SCHEMA_VERSION`); (2) idempotent additive DDL scripts for every subsystem (`apply_schema` for research runs/batches/etc., then trading, execution, evaluation, research, evidence_provider, research_cycle, corporate_status, shadow_operations, shadow_alerts, paper_books); (3) `apply_pending_schema_migrations` which iterates every migration in `_MIGRATIONS` not already recorded in `schema_version`, runs each inside its own `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction, appends the new row, and logs the version+description. Two subsystem `apply_*_schema` calls also perform their own inline upgrades: `paper_books_schema.apply_paper_books_schema` runs `_ensure_columns` (ALTER TABLE ADD COLUMN for any missing additive column in `_PAPER_BOOKS_COLUMN_UPGRADES`), a one-shot legacy external reservation backfill guarded by `_external_reservation_migration_needed` (inside its own BEGIN IMMEDIATE/rollback), the `_upgrade_triggers` versioned drop/recreate against `paper_books_trigger_versions`, and finally re-runs the trigger DDL. `migrations.apply_schema` additionally executes a special one-time rename (`rename_legacy_research_recommendations`) that renames a legacy `recommendations` table shape to `research_recommendations` and refuses if both exist.

- **Entry point.** src/trading_research/storage/database.py:30 `connect(db_path)` (also exposed as `session()` at line 70). Callers include CLI/tests (see ci.yml:106,125,146 and many `tests/**` imports of `trading_research.storage.database.connect`).
- **Authoritative validation.** Forward-version guard: `schema_version.check_schema_not_forward_versioned` (schema_version.py:296-308) reads `SELECT MAX(version) FROM schema_version` and raises `SchemaVersionError` if `stored > CURRENT_SCHEMA_VERSION` (line 293: `CURRENT_SCHEMA_VERSION = max(_MIGRATIONS)`; currently 11). Migration 10 (`_migration_10_advanced_risk_controls`, schema_version.py:225-242) additionally validates that the Milestone 13 tables actually exist after DDL, raising `SchemaVersionError` if any are missing. Paper-books legacy backfill (`_backfill_active_legacy_external_reservations`, paper_books_schema.py:1588-1632) raises `sqlite3.IntegrityError("LEGACY_ATTEMPT_RESERVATION_CONFLICT: ...")` if an existing attempt-reservation row's immutable fields do not match the legacy row, or `_enforce_one_active_external_attempt_reservation` raises `ATTEMPT_RESERVATION_CONFLICT` if multiple active lineage rows exist. `rename_legacy_research_recommendations` (migrations.py:118-135) raises `RuntimeError` if both legacy and current tables coexist.
- **Idempotency key.** Two ledgers act as idempotency keys: (a) the `schema_version(version PRIMARY KEY, description, applied_at)` table — `apply_pending_schema_migrations` computes `pending = sorted(v for v in _MIGRATIONS if v not in applied)` (schema_version.py:323-324), so any migration whose version integer is present is skipped; (b) `paper_books_trigger_versions(trigger_name PRIMARY KEY, version)` (paper_books_schema.py:1538-1541) — `_upgrade_triggers` compares recorded vs current `_TRIGGER_VERSIONS` and only drops+recreates when `recorded < current_version`. All `apply_*_schema` DDL relies on `CREATE TABLE/INDEX/TRIGGER IF NOT EXISTS` as an implicit idempotency mechanism (documented at schema_version.py:7-11).
- **Transaction boundary.** Each schema-version migration runs inside a dedicated `with transaction(conn):` (`storage.transactions.transaction`, transactions.py:57-75) which issues `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`, commits on clean exit, and rolls back on any `BaseException` — see schema_version.py:333-338 (migration_fn + INSERT of the version row happen inside the same transaction). Legacy external-reservation backfill uses an explicit `conn.execute("BEGIN IMMEDIATE")` with try/except `conn.rollback()`/`conn.commit()` (paper_books_schema.py:1680-1688). The subsystem `apply_*_schema` DDL scripts (e.g. research_schema.py:240-244, paper_books_schema.py:1676-1692) execute their `executescript` blocks outside an explicit transaction (SQLite auto-commits each DDL statement) and finish with `conn.commit()` — but since the connection is `isolation_level=None` autocommit, DDL is already committed statement-by-statement and the trailing `conn.commit()` is a no-op safeguard.
- **Lease or concurrency.** No application-level lease guards `connect()`. Concurrency is delegated to SQLite: `PRAGMA busy_timeout=5000ms` (database.py:47) plus `PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL` (database.py:52). Each migration acquires the SQLite write lock via `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` (transactions.py:54, paper_books_schema.py:1680), so two concurrent processes calling `connect()` on the same file will serialize on the reserved write lock; a process that cannot acquire the lock within 5 s raises `sqlite3.OperationalError("database is locked")`. `begin_immediate` fails closed with `TransactionAlreadyActiveError` (transactions.py:38-53) if a caller-owned transaction is already open, so migration code never silently discards outer work.
- **State transitions.** Per-migration: absent-from-schema_version → applied (row inserted with `version, description, applied_at`) — schema_version.py:335-338. `paper_books_trigger_versions`: recorded<version → drop trigger + upsert version to `current_version` (paper_books_schema.py:1557-1563); the trigger DDL is then re-executed at line 1691 which recreates it under current behavior. Legacy `recommendations` → `research_recommendations` one-way rename (migrations.py:134). Legacy `paper_external_daily_reservations` rows in RESERVED/RECONCILIATION_REQUIRED/SUBMITTED → mirrored `paper_external_attempt_reservations` rows with `attempt_number=0` (INSERT OR IGNORE), followed by creation of the partial UNIQUE INDEX `idx_paper_external_attempt_one_active`.
- **Persistent records.** (1) `schema_version(version, description, applied_at)` — one row per applied migration (schema_version.py:43-49). (2) `paper_books_trigger_versions(trigger_name, version)` — one row per versioned trigger (paper_books_schema.py:1538-1541). (3) All the subsystem DDL creates the persistent table set for research runs, batches, sources, results, claims, recommendations, conflicts, errors, artifacts (migrations.py DDL), plus the paper-book tables (paper_books_schema.py:22-1013) and their triggers. (4) `paper_external_attempt_reservations` rows backfilled from `paper_external_daily_reservations` (paper_books_schema.py:1607-1617).
- **External side effects.** None. This workflow only touches the local SQLite file on disk. It does not open network sockets, call brokers/providers, write to the filesystem outside the DB (aside from creating the DB's parent directory via `Path(db_path).parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)` at database.py:31), or emit anything beyond an INFO log line ("schema_version migration applied: version=%s description=%s", schema_version.py:341) that logs only the version number and static description, never row contents.
- **Failure behavior.** Fail-closed at every gate: (a) forward-version guard raises `SchemaVersionError` and aborts the connection before any DDL runs (schema_version.py:303-308); (b) each migration in `apply_pending_schema_migrations` runs inside `with transaction(conn)`, which rolls back on any `BaseException` (transactions.py:70-72) and re-raises — the `schema_version` insert is in the same transaction, so a partial migration cannot leave a "version marked applied but work not done" state; (c) legacy reservation backfill wraps its work in `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` + explicit rollback/re-raise (paper_books_schema.py:1680-1688); (d) `rename_legacy_research_recommendations` raises `RuntimeError` on ambiguous state (migrations.py:130-133); (e) `begin_immediate` fails closed if a caller-owned transaction is already open (transactions.py:45-53).
- **Retry behavior.** Retry is implicit: because every migration callable is documented as idempotent (schema_version.py:246-248) and the version ledger is checked on each `connect()`, re-invoking `connect()` after a failure simply re-attempts any pending migration. Failed migrations do not leave a `schema_version` row (rolled back with their work), so the next `connect()` finds them still pending and retries them. The SQLite `busy_timeout=5000ms` gives implicit retry against write contention. Column-adder helpers (`_migration_3/4/6/7/8/11`, `_ensure_columns`) inspect `PRAGMA table_info` before `ALTER TABLE ADD COLUMN` so a repeat run is a no-op.
- **Recovery behavior.** Recovery is stateless — the persisted `schema_version` table (and `paper_books_trigger_versions`) is the sole authoritative record of what has been applied. On reopen, `apply_pending_schema_migrations` reads `SELECT version FROM schema_version` (not `MAX`), so even a legacy DB with gaps (e.g. version 3 present but 2 absent) will still detect and re-run the missing versions (schema_version.py:320-324). `_external_reservation_migration_needed` (paper_books_schema.py:1652-1673) additionally detects any legacy active reservation not yet mirrored and re-runs the backfill until every eligible legacy row has an equivalent attempt-reservation row.
- **Terminal states.** A `schema_version` row is terminal (immutable in practice — no DELETE/UPDATE path). A `paper_books_trigger_versions` row is upserted forward-only (paper_books_schema.py:1560-1562: `ON CONFLICT(trigger_name) DO UPDATE SET version = excluded.version`). The connection is either returned to the caller (success) or `SchemaVersionError`/other exception propagates out of `connect()` and the caller receives no connection.
- **Fail open or closed.** fail-closed
- **File refs.** `src/trading_research/storage/database.py:30-67`, `src/trading_research/storage/schema_version.py:43-49`, `src/trading_research/storage/schema_version.py:52-291`, `src/trading_research/storage/schema_version.py:296-343`, `src/trading_research/storage/transactions.py:26-75`, `src/trading_research/storage/migrations.py:8-142`, `src/trading_research/storage/paper_books_schema.py:1451-1508`, `src/trading_research/storage/paper_books_schema.py:1520-1563`, `src/trading_research/storage/paper_books_schema.py:1571-1673`, `src/trading_research/storage/paper_books_schema.py:1676-1692`, `src/trading_research/storage/research_schema.py:240-244`
- **Suspected defects.**
  - NeedsVerification: `apply_pending_schema_migrations` runs after every `apply_*_schema` DDL block already committed (each subsystem `apply_*_schema` ends with `conn.commit()`). If a migration transaction rolls back on failure, prior additive DDL from this same `connect()` call is already committed. That is the documented design (schema_version.py:315-319), but a partial `connect()` failure will still leave the DB partially upgraded relative to the code path that observed the failure — safe on the next connect thanks to CREATE IF NOT EXISTS, but worth confirming for a case where the additive DDL introduces a column referenced by a rolled-back migration.
  - NeedsVerification: `paper_books_schema._backfill_active_legacy_external_reservations` fetches with `LIMIT 100000` (paper_books_schema.py:1593). If a legacy database has more than 100k active RESERVED/RECONCILIATION_REQUIRED/SUBMITTED rows, only the first 100k (ordered by client_order_id) will be backfilled per `connect()` invocation — the `_external_reservation_migration_needed` predicate will still return True next time, so it will eventually complete over multiple connections, but a single-shot connect() cannot guarantee completeness on very large legacy datasets.
  - NeedsVerification: Migration 2 backfill (`_migration_2_backfill_execution_namespace_claims`, schema_version.py:71-93) iterates `SELECT book_id, paper_order_intent_id FROM paper_book_orders` and performs N × up-to-4 sub-SELECTs plus one INSERT per row. On a large legacy database this is O(N) work inside a single BEGIN IMMEDIATE transaction with no batching — could exceed the `busy_timeout=5000ms` window for other concurrent connections during upgrade.
  - NeedsVerification: `_upgrade_triggers` INSERTs into `paper_books_trigger_versions` outside a transaction context manager (paper_books_schema.py:1558-1562). Because the connection is autocommit (`isolation_level=None`), the drop and the version upsert are two separate autocommitted statements — if the second one fails (unlikely, but e.g. disk-full), the trigger has been dropped but the version bump not recorded; on retry the trigger will be recreated at line 1691 (which is versionless CREATE IF NOT EXISTS), but with the previous CREATE-IF-NOT-EXISTS semantics guaranteed only when the DROP already executed. Confirm this hand-off is safe under crash-mid-upgrade.
  - NeedsVerification: `_migration_10_advanced_risk_controls` (schema_version.py:225-242) validates the presence of Milestone-13 tables. Those tables come from `apply_paper_books_schema` earlier in `connect()`. If a future refactor renames or moves any of the five listed tables without updating this migration, upgrade will fail-closed for every existing database — check whether the required-tables list is kept in sync with `paper_books_schema.PAPER_BOOKS_DDL` by any test.
  - NeedsVerification: `rename_legacy_research_recommendations` (migrations.py:118-135) runs before `_SCHEMA_VERSION_DDL`? Actually it runs at `apply_schema` (database.py:55), which is AFTER `check_schema_not_forward_versioned` (line 54) — but the forward-version guard only opens the `schema_version` table, not `recommendations`. Renaming behavior therefore has no version ledger row; a partial rename left behind by an older interrupted upgrade cannot be detected via `schema_version`. Consider whether this legacy rename should also be recorded via a `_MIGRATIONS` version marker for auditability.

#### Scheduled research cycle (scheduler -> activation). Two coupled surfaces: (a) `shadow/scheduler.py::run_due_shadow_cycle` drives one at-most intended slot: enabled check -> `schedule.resolve_due_status` (schedule/catch-up) -> pause/kill check -> idempotency check against `shadow_scheduler_runs` -> lease acquire -> budget reserve -> `save_scheduler_run` (RUNNING) -> injected `run_cycle` (which is `research/scheduled_cycle.py::run_scheduled_research_cycle`) -> settle budget -> health evaluation & hysteresis -> `update_scheduler_run` (final status) -> lease release in finally. Inner `run_scheduled_research_cycle` derives a deterministic `cycle_id` (universe_id+as_of+config_hash), writes `research_cycles` row (RUNNING), iterates bounded symbols with per-symbol try/except, and per symbol saves `research_cycle_symbol_results`, then marks final cycle status. (b) `paper_books/recurring_scheduler.py::run_recurring_paper_scheduler` is the *activation-gated* recurring loop over completed cycles that only runs when `current_activation_state(conn).state == STATE_ACTIVE` (an ACTIVATION_REQUESTED event followed by an ACTIVATED event exists in `paper_recurring_activation_events`); it acquires its own singleton `paper-recurring-local` lease, claims `paper_recurring_cycle_queue` items keyed on frozen-state hash, invokes `run_controlled_soak_day`, and finalizes the queue+scheduler run.

- **Entry point.** CLI `run-due-shadow-cycle` -> `trading_research/cli.py::run_due_shadow_cycle_cli` (src/trading_research/cli.py:1641-1882) which calls `shadow/scheduler.py::run_due_shadow_cycle` (src/trading_research/shadow/scheduler.py:551) passing `run_scheduled_research_cycle` (src/trading_research/research/scheduled_cycle.py:318) as `run_cycle`. Recurring paper-book activation entrypoints: `paper_books/recurring_scheduler.py::request_recurring_activation` (line 382), `activate_recurring` (line 415), and the scheduler entrypoint `run_recurring_paper_scheduler` (line 872).
- **Authoritative validation.** Multiple layered gates, all fail-closed. In `run_due_shadow_cycle`: (1) `shadow_config.shadow_operations.enabled` (src/trading_research/shadow/scheduler.py:643); (2) `schedule.resolve_due_status` (src/trading_research/shadow/schedule.py:138-289) — computes intended slot & catch-up eligibility; (3) `pause_mod.current_state` for KILL/PAUSE (:699-733); (4) `find_scheduler_run_by_intended_schedule` idempotency check requiring `status == STATUS_COMPLETED` (:686-696); (5) lease acquire on `shadow-scheduler:<intended_schedule_id>` (:736-759); (6) `budget_mod.estimate_cycle_cost` + `budget_mod.reserve_budget` with pricing lookup (:790-872); (7) optional `provider_coverage_policy.unavailable_required_categories` gate before cycle (:897-902). In `run_scheduled_research_cycle`: `ScheduledResearchConfiguration.__post_init__` validates provider_mode / experiment_policy / bounds (:110-115); per-symbol evidence-completeness gate via `evaluate_completeness` and `configuration.require_complete_evidence` (:490-509). In `run_recurring_paper_scheduler`: `evaluate_pre_run_safety_gates` (src/trading_research/paper_books/recurring_scheduler.py:696-772) enforces `recurring.enabled`, activation state ACTIVE, `validate_activation_review` (:231-362 — verifies campaign attempt COMPLETED_READY, hashes match, final_recommendation == READY, `qualifying_real_provider_cycles >= minimum_successful_real_provider_cycles`, age <= `activation_review_max_age_market_days`, no newer unresolved CRITICAL alerts, no blocking pause/mismatched reconciliation/unsafe snapshot/lifecycle failure), shadow kill/pause, advanced-risk book pauses, unresolved CRITICALs, `evaluate_controlled_soak_readiness`, real-provider-history minimum, cross-book verification freshness, DB availability, due slot, lease. Gates are re-evaluated a second time under lease (`under_lease_gates`, :936-941).
- **Idempotency key.** Shadow scheduler: `intended_schedule_id` = `shadow-cycle-<UTC-ISO intended_time>` (src/trading_research/shadow/schedule.py:91-97); scheduler-run existence keyed on it via `find_scheduler_run_by_intended_schedule` (:686). Lease key = `shadow-scheduler:<intended_schedule_id>` (src/trading_research/shadow/scheduler.py:736). Research cycle id = `cycle-<sha256(universe_id, as_of, config_hash)[:32]>` (`derive_cycle_id`, src/trading_research/research/scheduled_cycle.py:168-170); per-symbol resumption keyed on `(cycle_id, symbol)` in `research_cycle_symbol_results` (:366-369 — pre-existing COMPLETED/SKIPPED rows are returned unchanged). Health-check id `hcheck-<sha256(scheduler_run_id|check_name|policy_version)>` (:460-468). Recurring paper scheduler: `intended_schedule_id` = `paper-recurring:<local_date>:<HH:MM>:<recurring_config_hash>` (src/trading_research/paper_books/recurring_scheduler.py:609-612); scheduler_run_id = `prun-<sha256(intended_schedule_id)>` (:620-621); activation event id `prae-<sha256(payload)>` (:377); queue item id `prq-<sha256(cycle_id+frozen_state_hash+retry_of)>` (:552-554).
- **Transaction boundary.** SQLite auto-commit style with explicit `begin_immediate` only around the mutex-critical writes. Shadow lease uses `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` around SELECT + INSERT/UPDATE of `shadow_run_leases` (src/trading_research/shadow/lease.py:76-100). Individual repositories (`save_scheduler_run`, `update_scheduler_run` in src/trading_research/storage/shadow_operations_repositories.py:15-40) each `conn.commit()` per statement — no wrapping cycle-wide transaction. `run_scheduled_research_cycle` performs many independent commits per symbol (snapshot save, provenance, symbol result). Recurring paper `_claim_queue_items` and `acquire_recurring_lease` use `begin_immediate` + rollback-on-exception (src/trading_research/paper_books/recurring_scheduler.py:630-656, 784-826).
- **Lease or concurrency.** Shadow scheduler: TTL-bounded lease in `shadow_run_leases` via `shadow/lease.py::acquire` (UNIQUE(lease_key) + `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction; stale reclaim when `expires_at < now`) (src/trading_research/shadow/lease.py:1-100). Lease `ttl_seconds = shadow_operations.lease_ttl_seconds`; owner token generated via `lease_mod.generate_owner_token()` if not supplied (src/trading_research/shadow/scheduler.py:737). Lease is always released in `finally` via `lease_mod.release` when the current holder still matches (:1294-1298). Recurring paper scheduler: singleton lease `paper-recurring-local` in `paper_recurring_scheduler_leases` with owner/heartbeat/expires_at (src/trading_research/paper_books/recurring_scheduler.py:624-686). Cycle-level idempotency (deterministic cycle_id) is complementary defense-in-depth per ADR-0005 Decision 3.
- **State transitions.** Scheduler run: (no row) -> INSERT RUNNING (src/trading_research/shadow/scheduler.py:874-890) -> UPDATE to {COMPLETED, PARTIALLY_COMPLETE, FAILED} (:1032-1042). Cycle: RUNNING via `save_cycle_started` on first entry (research/scheduled_cycle.py:358-362) -> COMPLETED / PARTIALLY_COMPLETE / FAILED via `mark_cycle_finished` (:400-407). Symbol result: COMPLETED / SKIPPED / FAILED via `save_symbol_result` (:397). Recurring paper activation state: INACTIVE -> ACTIVATION_REQUESTED (via `request_recurring_activation`) -> ACTIVE (via `activate_recurring`) -> {PAUSED_BY_SAFETY (via `pause_recurring_for_safety`) or DEACTIVATED (via `deactivate_recurring`)} -> ACTIVATION_REQUESTED again from PAUSED_BY_SAFETY/DEACTIVATED (src/trading_research/paper_books/recurring_scheduler.py:189-213, 41-49). Queue item: QUEUED -> CLAIMED -> {PROCESSED | FAILED | CANCELLED} (:56-61, 779-853). Lease: HELD -> RELEASED (or stale->reclaimed) (src/trading_research/shadow/lease.py:35-100).
- **Persistent records.** Tables: `shadow_scheduler_runs` (row per scheduler invocation that reached lease/budget stage) (src/trading_research/storage/shadow_operations_repositories.py:15-30); `shadow_run_leases` (mutex state); `shadow_budget_reservations` (via budget module); `shadow_run_summaries` (row per invocation including no-ops, via `_save_no_op_summary`/`_save_health_summary`); `shadow_health_checks` (field-level diagnostics); `shadow_alerts` (via `alerts_mod.raise_alert`); `shadow_health_hysteresis_state` (persistent multi-cycle streak). Research: `research_cycles`, `research_cycle_symbol_results`, `research_cycle_symbol_evidence_status`, `research_cycle_provider_provenance`, `research_evidence_snapshots`, `research_evidence_completeness_results`, `corporate_status_evidences`, `research_attempts`/`research_attempt_failures`, `recommendations`, `experiment_assignments`, `overlay_decisions` (see src/trading_research/research/scheduled_cycle.py:441-570). Provider requests recorded via `provider_persistence_mod.provider_request_context` with scheduler_run_id (:945-948). Recurring paper tables: `paper_recurring_activation_events`, `paper_recurring_cycle_queue`, `paper_recurring_scheduler_leases`, `paper_recurring_scheduler_runs`.
- **External side effects.** Inside `run_scheduled_research_cycle` per symbol: outbound calls to configured evidence providers (SEC EDGAR, Alpaca market data, news/sentiment) when `provider_mode == "real"` via `build_real_candidate_input` and `build_real_evidence_snapshot` (src/trading_research/research/scheduled_cycle.py:179-307), and a research-model provider call via `analyze_with_research_committee` (:516-521) which can invoke Anthropic API, Claude Code CLI, or Codex CLI depending on config. Optional paper-order submission via injected `paper_submitter(rec_id)` gated by `configuration.submit_paper_orders`, `experiment_policy.may_submit_baseline`, and `side == SIDE_BUY_CANDIDATE` (:571-580). Health-triggered pause requests may write to `shadow_pause_state`. No email/webhook sinks are configured (only `PersistenceOnlyAlertSink` and `LogAlertSink` per `_default_alert_sinks`, src/trading_research/shadow/scheduler.py:199-206).
- **Failure behavior.** Fail-closed on every preflight (enabled flag, schedule window, market-day gate, kill/pause, lease conflict, idempotency, budget/pricing, provider coverage, activation review). Per-symbol failures are isolated (does not tear down cycle unless `continue_on_symbol_failure=false`). Cycle-level exceptions are captured as `failure_reason`, run persisted as FAILED, lease released, alert raised. CLI wraps the orchestrator with a broad `except Exception` returning `INTERNAL_ERROR`.
- **Retry behavior.** No automatic retries at scheduler level. Missed slots within `max_catch_up_cycles` become MISSED_WITHIN_CATCHUP and are attempted on the next invocation, oldest-first (src/trading_research/shadow/schedule.py:229-280). Slots older than the actionable window are surfaced as MISSED_TOO_OLD with a WARNING alert (`ALERT_TYPE_SCHEDULER_MISSED_RUN`, src/trading_research/shadow/scheduler.py:660-666). Per-symbol resumption: pre-existing COMPLETED/SKIPPED symbol rows are returned unchanged when the same cycle_id is entered again (research/scheduled_cycle.py:366-369). Per-role/per-attempt budget retries happen inside `ResearchAttemptController` when supplied (`max_attempts_per_role`, `max_roles_per_symbol` — src/trading_research/shadow/scheduler.py:924, 774). No exponential backoff in the scheduler itself.
- **Recovery behavior.** Idempotent resumption: the same `intended_schedule_id` yields the same `cycle_id` (derive_cycle_id is deterministic on universe_id+as_of+config_hash), so a re-invocation after crash between lease release and cycle-completion picks up existing symbol_results and only re-runs incomplete symbols (src/trading_research/research/scheduled_cycle.py:353-369). Stale-lease reclaim in `shadow/lease.py::acquire` (lease.py:90-100). Recurring paper `_claim_queue_items` recovers abandoned CLAIMED items (same run id, terminal FAILED run, or expired orphans) back to QUEUED (src/trading_research/paper_books/recurring_scheduler.py:787-793). `_release_claims_after_failure` returns CLAIMED items to QUEUED on cycle-level exception (:855-862). Health hysteresis state persists across process restarts to distinguish a genuine outage from one noisy cycle (src/trading_research/shadow/scheduler.py:1083-1197).
- **Terminal states.** Shadow scheduler ShadowCycleRunResult.status terminals: DISABLED, NOT_DUE, MARKET_HOLIDAY, OUTSIDE_RUN_WINDOW, MISSED_TOO_OLD, ALREADY_COMPLETED, LEASE_HELD (all `NO_OP_STATUSES`); KILLED, PAUSED, BUDGET_REJECTED (`BLOCKED_STATUSES`); COMPLETED, PARTIALLY_COMPLETE, FAILED (cycle-ran statuses) (src/trading_research/shadow/scheduler.py:87-109). ResearchCycleResult.status terminals: COMPLETED, PARTIALLY_COMPLETE, FAILED (src/trading_research/research/scheduled_cycle.py:78-81). SymbolCycleResult.status: COMPLETED, SKIPPED, FAILED (:83-85). Recurring paper RecurringPaperSchedulerResult.status: COMPLETED, COMPLETED_WITH_WARNINGS, SKIPPED_INACTIVE, SKIPPED_NOT_DUE, SKIPPED_ALREADY_COMPLETED, SKIPPED_LEASE_HELD, BLOCKED_SAFETY, FAILED (src/trading_research/paper_books/recurring_scheduler.py:63-74). Activation state terminals: ACTIVE, PAUSED_BY_SAFETY, DEACTIVATED (:46-49).
- **Fail open or closed.** fail-closed
- **File refs.** `C:/workspace/other/ai_model/src/trading_research/cli.py:1641-1882`, `C:/workspace/other/ai_model/src/trading_research/shadow/scheduler.py:551-1298`, `C:/workspace/other/ai_model/src/trading_research/shadow/schedule.py:81-289`, `C:/workspace/other/ai_model/src/trading_research/shadow/lease.py:64-100`, `C:/workspace/other/ai_model/src/trading_research/research/scheduled_cycle.py:78-588`, `C:/workspace/other/ai_model/src/trading_research/paper_books/recurring_scheduler.py:41-1077`, `C:/workspace/other/ai_model/src/trading_research/storage/shadow_operations_repositories.py:15-70`
- **Suspected defects.**
  - shadow/scheduler.py `_no_op` for the ALREADY_COMPLETED idempotent hit (lines 686-696) skips writing a `shadow_scheduler_runs` row for the re-entry; the `_save_no_op_summary` uses a synthetic id but the `shadow_run_summaries.scheduler_run_id` for this idempotent skip is `shadow-no-op-<intended_schedule_id>` — collides on repeated ALREADY_COMPLETED calls because the intended_schedule_id is the same, so `save_run_summary` may insert duplicate summary rows or fail on primary-key conflict (verify PK behavior in `save_run_summary`). Real-Claude scheduled runs rely on per-attempt charging by `shadow/attempt_controller.py`; if the attempt controller is not wired (e.g., `research_roles is None` at :913), no per-attempt charging happens either — the reservation is settled at zero consumption even for a real API call. NeedsVerification.

#### Historical Backtesting: signals + configuration + point-in-time HistoricalDataProvider ⇒ deterministic daily-bar engine that processes existing positions (stop-first ambiguity, ATR trailing stop, partial-profit stages, max-hold) then admits next-session limit-order entries (risk-cap sized, blackout-guarded) ⇒ daily equity states + fills + rejected-signal ledger ⇒ report + (optional) SQLite persistence into backtest_runs / backtest_daily_states / backtest_fills / backtest_metrics inside a single BEGIN IMMEDIATE transaction, keyed by an input_hash of (configuration + signal set + code version).

- **Entry point.** `run_backtest` at src/trading_research/backtesting/engine.py:123-399 (public API re-exported in src/trading_research/backtesting/__init__.py:4-7). Strategy layer wraps it via `run_strategy_backtest` at src/trading_research/strategies/backtest_adapter.py:63-89, which validates signal set homogeneity and forwards strategy identity to persistence.
- **Authoritative validation.** 1) Configuration invariants: BacktestConfiguration.__post_init__ at src/trading_research/backtesting/configuration.py:38-49 (date range, positive cash/atr_period, risk fractions in (0,1), non-negative slippage/fees, only CONSERVATIVE_STOP_FIRST ambiguity policy). 2) Bar shape: HistoricalBar.__post_init__ at src/trading_research/backtesting/models.py:26-34 (tz-aware available_at, point_in_time_safe True, OHLC positivity, open/close inside [low,high]). 3) Data provider re-checks look-ahead: FixtureHistoricalDataProvider.bars at src/trading_research/backtesting/data_provider.py:20-31 (tz-aware as_of, filters by available_at ≤ as_of, refuses look-ahead/unsafe). 4) Engine cross-checks: empty dataset per symbol and future-available bar raise BacktestError at engine.py:146-149; empty session universe raises at engine.py:152-154. 5) EntrySignal.__post_init__ at models.py:49-59 rejects non-positive/non-integral quantities and non-positive references. 6) Strategy adapter enforces ELIGIBLE status and unique strategy_version/configuration_hash across the signal set at backtest_adapter.py:32-38 and 74-80. 7) At entry time, engine rejects strategy-supplied stop ≥ fill or target ≤ fill (engine.py:327-336) — strategy exits are never silently coerced. 8) At persistence, run-id / input-hash collision with a different input hash raises rather than overwrites (engine.py:420-427).
- **Idempotency key.** `run_id = "backtest-" + sha256(input_hash)[:32]` where `input_hash = hash_config({configuration_hash, signal_set_hash, code_version})` (engine.py:130-139). `configuration_hash` covers every dataclass field of BacktestConfiguration including partial-profit stages and blackout parameters (engine.py:67-92). `signal_set_hash` sorts signals by signal_id and hashes their full content, so distinct signal sets cannot collapse (engine.py:46-64). Both hashes and input_hash are persisted on backtest_runs (columns added via _PAPER_BOOKS_COLUMN_UPGRADES at paper_books_schema.py:1459-1465). Replay of an identical run short-circuits at engine.py:428-429 (`existing is not None: return`).
- **Transaction boundary.** A single `with transaction(conn):` (engine.py:416) wraps the existence check plus all inserts into backtest_runs, backtest_daily_states, backtest_fills, backtest_metrics (engine.py:416-464). `transaction` is `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` with commit on clean exit and rollback on any BaseException, and refuses to run when a caller-owned transaction is already open (src/trading_research/storage/transactions.py:38-74). All engine compute (fetching bars, iterating sessions, generating fills/daily-states) happens purely in memory before the transaction opens.
- **Lease or concurrency.** None. The backtest engine takes no lease and does not consult the fencing infrastructure used elsewhere in the repo (paper_external_order_leases etc., paper_books_schema.py:1500-1502). Concurrency safety relies solely on SQLite's BEGIN IMMEDIATE serialising two parallel `_persist_result` calls; two concurrent runs with the same run_id then collapse via the "existing row → return" idempotent path (engine.py:428-429).
- **State transitions.** In-memory per session (engine.py:194-370): (a) mark start_equity at bar.open ⇒ (b) evaluate open positions in symbol order: STOP_GAP (bar.open ≤ stop), HARD_OR_TRAILING_STOP (bar.low ≤ stop), MAXIMUM_HOLDING_PERIOD (held_sessions ≥ maximum_holding_sessions) — first match wins under stop-first ambiguity; then partial-profit stage promotion when high_r ≥ trigger and partial-close quantity > 0 (engine.py:230-255), FINAL_TARGET when bar.high ≥ target, trailing-stop ratchet via `max(position.stop, highest − atr*multiple)` (engine.py:268-275) ⇒ (c) risk gates against daily_fraction and current_drawdown (engine.py:277-290) ⇒ (d) admit eligible next-session signals: blackout, LIMIT_NOT_FILLED, ATR_UNAVAILABLE, INVALID_STRATEGY_STOP/TARGET, ZERO_SHARES_AT_RISK_CAP checks (engine.py:281-342) ⇒ (e) create BacktestFill and _Position (engine.py:343-359) ⇒ (f) mark equity at close, update peak/drawdown and previous_closes (engine.py:361-370). Persistence status transitions once from absent to COMPLETED (engine.py:435).
- **Persistent records.** Only when `conn` is supplied (engine.py:393-398, 402-464): row in `backtest_runs` (schema paper_books_schema.py:950-964 + upgrade columns input_hash / signal_set_hash / strategy_id / strategy_version / strategy_config_hash at paper_books_schema.py:1459-1465), one row per session in `backtest_daily_states` (paper_books_schema.py:966-976), one row per fill in `backtest_fills` (paper_books_schema.py:991-1006 + upgrade columns fill_sequence / position_id at paper_books_schema.py:1503-1506), one row in `backtest_metrics` (paper_books_schema.py:1008-1012). `backtest_orders` table exists (paper_books_schema.py:978-989) but the engine never writes to it.
- **External side effects.** None. No broker submission, no HTTP/MCP call, no filesystem write. Fills are simulated with slippage_bps and fee_per_order (engine.py:107-118, 343-352). Data comes from an in-process HistoricalDataProvider (Protocol at data_provider.py:10-13; fixture at data_provider.py:16-31). Only side effect is optional SQLite persistence into the same connection the caller passes.
- **Failure behavior.** Fails closed by raising `BacktestError` (subclass of ValueError, models.py:9-10): empty dataset per symbol (engine.py:146-147), future-available bar (engine.py:148-149), no sessions (engine.py:152-154), missing point-in-time mark for an open position (engine.py:189), invalid `_sell` quantity (engine.py:106), missing benchmark bars when benchmark_symbol set (engine.py:375-376), run-id/input-hash collision with a different input hash (engine.py:425-427). Provider layer raises for tz-naive as_of or unsafe bars (data_provider.py:24-30). Configuration/model validators raise on construction. On any exception the persistence transaction rolls back (transactions.py:69-72).
- **Retry behavior.** None inside the engine — no retry loop, no exponential backoff, no scheduling. Idempotency is the substitute: a caller can safely re-invoke `run_backtest` with identical configuration+signals and the persistence layer will no-op via the "existing row → return" branch at engine.py:428-429. Non-hashable transient errors (e.g., a provider raising) simply propagate up.
- **Recovery behavior.** Compute is fully re-derivable from inputs (deterministic engine, point-in-time provider, code_version pinned at engine.py:26). If persistence fails, no partial rows survive (BEGIN IMMEDIATE + rollback). Re-running is safe because the run_id is a pure function of inputs. There is no separate STARTED/RUNNING state and no reconciliation record — a run either exists as COMPLETED or is absent.
- **Terminal states.** Persistent: `backtest_runs.status = 'COMPLETED'` written unconditionally on successful persistence (engine.py:435, hard-coded literal). No FAILED / PARTIAL / CANCELED states are emitted by this workflow. In-memory rejected-signal reasons act as per-signal terminal outcomes: UNKNOWN_SYMBOL, NO_NEXT_SESSION, POSITION_ALREADY_OPEN, DAILY_LOSS_LIMIT, DRAWDOWN_LIMIT, ECONOMIC_EVENT_BLACKOUT, LIMIT_NOT_FILLED, ATR_UNAVAILABLE, INVALID_STRATEGY_STOP, INVALID_STRATEGY_TARGET, ZERO_SHARES_AT_RISK_CAP (engine.py:161-342). Per-position exit reasons: STOP_GAP, HARD_OR_TRAILING_STOP, MAXIMUM_HOLDING_PERIOD, PARTIAL_PROFIT, FINAL_TARGET (engine.py:208-263).
- **Fail open or closed.** fail-closed
- **File refs.** `C:/workspace/other/ai_model/src/trading_research/backtesting/engine.py:26`, `C:/workspace/other/ai_model/src/trading_research/backtesting/engine.py:46-64`, `C:/workspace/other/ai_model/src/trading_research/backtesting/engine.py:67-92`, `C:/workspace/other/ai_model/src/trading_research/backtesting/engine.py:95-97`, `C:/workspace/other/ai_model/src/trading_research/backtesting/engine.py:100-120`, `C:/workspace/other/ai_model/src/trading_research/backtesting/engine.py:123-399`, `C:/workspace/other/ai_model/src/trading_research/backtesting/engine.py:402-464`, `C:/workspace/other/ai_model/src/trading_research/backtesting/configuration.py:15-49`, `C:/workspace/other/ai_model/src/trading_research/backtesting/data_provider.py:10-31`, `C:/workspace/other/ai_model/src/trading_research/backtesting/models.py:9-103`, `C:/workspace/other/ai_model/src/trading_research/backtesting/reports.py:9-40`, `C:/workspace/other/ai_model/src/trading_research/strategies/backtest_adapter.py:29-89`, `C:/workspace/other/ai_model/src/trading_research/storage/paper_books_schema.py:950-1012`, `C:/workspace/other/ai_model/src/trading_research/storage/paper_books_schema.py:1455-1506`, `C:/workspace/other/ai_model/src/trading_research/storage/transactions.py:38-74`
- **Suspected defects.**
  - Legacy rows silently swallow new runs (engine.py:420-429): if a backtest_runs row already exists with the same backtest_run_id but `input_hash IS NULL` (created before Milestone 24 added the column via _PAPER_BOOKS_COLUMN_UPGRADES at paper_books_schema.py:1459-1465), the mismatch guard is skipped (`existing[0] is not None` is False) and the persistence path returns as an idempotent replay, so the current run's daily-states, fills and metrics are never written even though the previous run's identity may differ. Worth verifying whether any pre-upgrade rows can still be encountered in real databases.
  - Benchmark symbol must be pre-included in `config.symbols` but this is not enforced by BacktestConfiguration. `run_backtest` only fetches bars for `config.symbols` (engine.py:141-150) and then `bars_by_symbol.get(config.benchmark_symbol)` at engine.py:373-376 raises 'benchmark dataset is unavailable' when it wasn't fetched — a foot-gun that fails the run for a data-availability reason unrelated to the strategy under test. NeedsVerification against callers.
  - `_sell` computes slippage as `price * (1 - slippage_bps/10000)` unconditionally, including PARTIAL_PROFIT and FINAL_TARGET exits where the trigger price is inside the day's range, but STOP_GAP uses `bar.open` (which may already be below `position.stop`, i.e., an adverse gap). Slipping bar.open further downward can produce a fill price worse than any observed market price on that bar. NeedsVerification whether that is intentional conservative modelling.
  - `daily_fraction` risk gate at engine.py:278 marks both start_equity and pre_entry_equity at `bar.open` (engine.py:195, 277). Yesterday's positions therefore contribute the same open-price valuation to both terms, so `daily_fraction` reflects only realized P/L from today's exits, not any open drawdown on positions still held. A large gap-down on a held position that does not touch its stop is not counted toward the max_daily_loss_fraction gate. NeedsVerification against the intended semantics of the daily-loss circuit breaker.
  - `_configuration_hash` (engine.py:67-92) omits `EntrySignal.strategy_signal_id` and the strategy identity forwarded through backtest_adapter (`strategy_id`, `strategy_version`, `strategy_config_hash`). Two runs that differ only in the persisted strategy identity but share configuration + signal payload will collide onto the same run_id; the mismatch guard fires only if the input hash differs, which it will not when signal payload hashes match. NeedsVerification for the strategy-backtest path where different strategies could conceivably produce content-identical signal sets.
  - No lease or advisory lock (contrast with paper_external_order_leases at paper_books_schema.py:1500-1502). Two concurrent processes running the same backtest race on `_persist_result`; SQLite BEGIN IMMEDIATE serialises them but there is no external progress record before commit, so a long compute followed by a rollback (e.g., disk-full at COMMIT) leaves no trace that work was attempted.
  - `backtest_orders` table (paper_books_schema.py:978-989) is declared but never populated by the engine — no INSERT anywhere in engine.py:402-464. Rejected entries are only reflected in the report_json under backtest_metrics/report; there is no queryable per-signal row explaining why an entry did not fire. NeedsVerification whether downstream dashboards depend on backtest_orders.
  - `INSERT OR IGNORE` on backtest_daily_states, backtest_fills and backtest_metrics (engine.py:443-464) means that if the outer `existing is not None → return` short-circuit is ever bypassed while some children rows already exist for the same run_id, silent partial writes are possible. Guarded today by the short-circuit, but fragile if the existence check is ever changed.

#### Strategy evaluation & shortlist selection is a deterministic, zero-LLM prefilter that: (1) accepts per-strategy `StrategySignal` tuples produced by `MomentumBreakoutStrategy` / `MeanReversionStrategy` / `EventDrivenCatalystStrategy`; (2) inside `selector.select_shortlist` filters to `StrategyStatus.ELIGIBLE`, ranks by (`-signal_strength`, `data_quality_rank`, `-data_as_of.timestamp()`), caps per-strategy at `ShortlistConfig.maximum_candidates_per_strategy`, deduplicates across strategies per symbol, ranks symbols by their best signal, and drops symbols that would breach `maximum_symbol_allocation_fraction` given the supplied `PortfolioState`; (3) hands each surviving `ShortlistEntry` to `research_budget.decide_research_action`, which returns a `ResearchDecision` (SEND_TO_RESEARCH / SKIP_LOW_SIGNAL / SKIP_DAILY_CANDIDATE_CAP / SKIP_DAILY_TOKEN_CAP) purely from a caller-supplied `DailyResearchBudgetState`. Only for symbols routed to the research committee does `PersistentResearchTokenBudgetController.invoke` (in `research/token_budget.py`) then hit the `shadow_budget_reservations` table via `reserve_research_tokens` / `settle_research_tokens` / `release_research_tokens` / `mark_research_tokens_ambiguous` from `strategies/research_budget.py`. The full evaluation + shortlist step itself is pure/in-memory. Notably, no production caller in `src/` currently wires the three strategy scanners into `select_shortlist` — Grep shows only unit tests (`tests/unit/test_strategy_selector.py`) drive this path, consistent with `config/strategies.yaml` being pinned at `activation_stage: STAGE_2_HISTORICAL_BACKTEST` and `enabled: false`.

- **Entry point.** Nominally: caller iterates over configured strategies (each a `CandidateStrategy` per `strategies/contracts.py:142-152`), calls `strategy.evaluate(symbol, StrategyMarketData, StrategyContext)` to build `dict[str, tuple[StrategySignal, ...]]`, then calls `selector.select_shortlist(signals_by_strategy, ShortlistConfig, portfolio)` at `src/trading_research/strategies/selector.py:82-119`.
