### Coverage limitations

- **Phases run to completion.** Inventory (architecture + 11 workflow traces + invariant audit), Find (16 parallel dimensions, 105 raw candidate findings), Verify (315 verdict votes = 3 per finding).
- **Phases stopped early by user request.** Completeness Critic and free-form Synthesis were skipped; this report was rendered deterministically from the verified finder outputs.
- **Files not opened.** The audit relied on targeted Grep/Read within finder agents; whole-directory reads were deliberately avoided per the token-efficient working method. Files under `paper_runtime/` third-party clients, `.streamlit/`, and rarely-exercised `scripts/` variants were not inspected line-by-line.
- **Tests not executed.** All tests were deliberately skipped for safety and time. Static analysis (pyright/mypy/lint) was not run.
- **Git history.** Only local history was inspected; no remote fetch was performed.

## 14. Completeness critic - additional findings

_Run after the Critic phase (5 targeted lenses: cli-entry-points, schemas-init, scheduler-activation, provider-boundary, runtime-ipc). Single-vote verified (adversarial refute-first). 17 added, 13 refuted._

### F-097 [Medium / CONFIRMED] paper-promotion-status hardcodes ops-health/reconciliation OK, so the NOT_PROMOTABLE gate is unreachable
- **Area / Dimension.** critic:cli-entry-points
- **Affected files.** `src/trading_research/cli.py`, `src/trading_research/paper_books/cli_support.py`, `src/trading_research/paper_books/promotion_evidence.py`
- **Line ranges.** cli.py:3402-3412; cli_support.py:242-275; promotion_evidence.py:28,61-69
- **Observed behavior.** The `paper-promotion-status` subparser (cli.py 2801-2805) only accepts `--experiment-id`/`--min-comparable-cycles`/`--min-trading-days`/`--min-closed-trades`. The dispatch at cli.py 3406-3410 calls `paper_promotion_status_cli(...)` without operational_health_ok/reconciliation_ok. cli_support.py:244 defaults both to `True`, and evaluate_promotion_evidence (promotion_evidence.py:61) branches to `RESULT_ENHANCED_OUTPERFORMS_NOT_PROMOTABLE` only when either flag is False. From the CLI those flags are literally always True.
- **Failure scenario.** Operator runs `paper-promotion-status --experiment-id EXP-1` after a soak in which reconciliation has an unresolved mismatch or the operational health monitor is red. The persisted `enhanced_metrics.cumulative_return` delta is positive and sample floors are met, so evaluate_promotion_evidence returns `PROMOTION_REVIEW_ELIGIBLE` and save_promotion_evidence persists that result (cli_support.py:271). Downstream reviewers pick up a `PROMOTION_REVIEW_ELIGIBLE` row despite active operational/reconciliation blockers - the CLI has no path to ever emit `ENHANCED_OUTPERFORMS_NOT_PROMOTABLE`.
- **Impact.** Misleading, over-permissive promotion-evidence record persisted in a state that is by design supposed to block promotion review. Confuses the human-approval gate and silently downgrades the ops/reconciliation guardrail intended by promotion_evidence.py.
- **Recommended correction.** Either derive operational_health_ok/reconciliation_ok from persisted state (shadow alerts, latest reconciliation status) inside `paper_promotion_status_cli`, or accept explicit CLI flags and fail closed when they are not supplied. Never call `evaluate_promotion_evidence` with unconditional True defaults from an entry point.
- **Verification.** 1 vote - CONFIRMED: Confirmed by direct reading. cli.py:3402-3412 dispatches to paper_promotion_status_cli without passing operational_health_ok/reconciliation_ok; the argparse spec (2801-2805) has no such flags. cli_support.py:242-244 defaults both to True and passes them straight into evaluate_promotion_evidence (line 266-270). promotion_evidence.py:61-69 only returns RESULT_ENHANCED_OUTPERFORMS_NOT_PROMOTABLE when either flag is False. Therefore the NOT_PROMOTABLE branch is unreachable from the CLI.

### F-098 [Medium / CONFIRMED] paper-experiment-compare and paper-promotion-status persist evidence even when paper_books.enabled=false
- **Area / Dimension.** critic:cli-entry-points
- **Affected files.** `src/trading_research/paper_books/cli_support.py`, `src/trading_research/paper_books/comparison.py`
- **Line ranges.** cli_support.py:222-239, 242-275; comparison.py:44-101
- **Observed behavior.** Every other mutating paper-book CLI checks `if not cfg.enabled: return {'error': ...}` (see cli_support.py:98, 129, 155, 294, 356, 398, 536, 627, 684, 721, 780). `paper_experiment_compare_cli` (222) and `paper_promotion_status_cli` (242) omit that gate entirely - they read cfg only to obtain baseline/enhanced book ids. `build_comparison` calls `save_book_metrics` and `save_experiment_comparison`, and `paper_promotion_status_cli` calls `save_promotion_evidence` (line 271).
- **Failure scenario.** An operator flips `paper_books.enabled: false` (or a fresh checkout where enabled is not yet true) and then runs `paper-experiment-compare --experiment-id X ...` or `paper-promotion-status --experiment-id X`. Both commands succeed and write `paper_book_daily_metrics`, `paper_book_experiment_comparisons`, and `paper_book_promotion_evidence` rows, even though the whole subsystem is administratively disabled.
- **Impact.** Breaks the documented `paper_books.enabled=false` fail-closed contract for mutating commands (cli_support.py module docstring, lines 6-9) and pollutes the promotion-evidence tables with rows produced under an off configuration, which later readers cannot distinguish from valid on-configuration evidence.
- **Recommended correction.** Add the same `if not cfg.enabled: return {'error': ...}` gate at the start of `paper_experiment_compare_cli` and `paper_promotion_status_cli`, matching every other mutating command in the same module.
- **Verification.** 1 vote - CONFIRMED: Verified against source. paper_experiment_compare_cli (lines 222-239) loads cfg but omits the `if not cfg.enabled` gate; paper_promotion_status_cli (lines 242-275) does not even load the config. Both then persist rows.

### F-099 [Medium / CONFIRMED] paper-book-exit-request idempotency key is derived from a now() timestamp, so retries create duplicate requests
- **Area / Dimension.** critic:cli-entry-points
- **Affected files.** `src/trading_research/cli.py`, `src/trading_research/paper_books/cli_support.py`
- **Line ranges.** cli.py:2840-2846, 3437-3446; cli_support.py:386-427
- **Observed behavior.** The CLI accepts only `--book-id/--symbol/--operator/--reason` (cli.py:2843-2846). In `paper_book_exit_request_cli` the default `requested_at = now()` (409-410) and the default idempotency key is `sha256(book_id:symbol:operator:reason:requested_at.isoformat())[:32]` (411-413). Because `requested_at` is regenerated on every invocation, the derived idempotency key is different every call, and `save_manual_exit_request` (which uses that key to dedupe) inserts a fresh row.
- **Failure scenario.** An operator runs `paper-book-exit-request --book-id ENHANCED --symbol AAPL --operator alice --reason "stop-out"`, is unsure whether the command succeeded, and runs it again. Two distinct `manual_exit_request_id` rows are persisted for the same intent. The next `paper-book-lifecycle-run` consumes both - the second attempt is at best a no-op that clutters audit tables, at worst it re-fires an exit if the position has been reopened by then.
- **Impact.** Idempotency guarantee promised by the code path (cli_support.py:411-414) is unreachable from the CLI. Operator retries after transient failures pollute the manual_exit_requests table and can double-invoke exit evaluation.
- **Recommended correction.** Expose `--idempotency-key` (or `--requested-at`) on the subparser and require it, or derive the key deterministically from (book_id, symbol, operator, reason) plus a bounded operator-supplied nonce; do not use `now()` as a default input to the hash.
- **Verification.** 1 vote - CONFIRMED: CLI parser at cli.py:2840-2846 exposes only book-id/symbol/operator/reason; dispatcher at cli.py:3437-3446 calls paper_book_exit_request_cli without idempotency_key or requested_at. In cli_support.py:409-413, requested_at defaults to _utc_now() and the default idempotency key hashes requested_at.isoformat(). save_manual_exit_request dedupes on (book_id, idempotency_key), so retries insert new rows.

### F-100 [Low / CONFIRMED] paper-book-reconcile does not check is_book_enabled, allowing reconciliation writes on a disabled book
- **Area / Dimension.** critic:cli-entry-points
- **Affected files.** `src/trading_research/paper_books/cli_support.py`
- **Line ranges.** cli_support.py:123-137
- **Observed behavior.** The reconcile CLI validates `cfg.enabled` and that `book_id` is one of the configured pair, but never calls `cfg.is_book_enabled(book_id)`. Compare to `paper_book_snapshot_cli` (98-103) and other mutating commands which do gate on per-book enablement.
- **Failure scenario.** Operator disables the ENHANCED book (`paper_books.enhanced.enabled: false` while `paper_books.enabled: true`) after retiring it. Running `paper-book-reconcile --book-id ENHANCED --as-of ...` still executes `reconciliation.reconcile_book`, which reads/writes reconciliation rows against a book that is explicitly turned off.
- **Impact.** Weak fail-closed posture - reconciliation activity persists against a book the config has retired. Not directly a submission-safety issue, but inconsistent with the surrounding pattern and confuses ops-history readers.
- **Recommended correction.** Add `if not cfg.is_book_enabled(book_id): return {'error': ...}` to `paper_book_reconcile_cli`, matching the snapshot/soak commands.
- **Verification.** 1 vote - CONFIRMED: paper_book_reconcile_cli (123-137) checks cfg.enabled and configured-pair membership but does not call is_book_enabled; paper_book_snapshot_cli (98-103) and paper_book_run_cycle_cli (155-159) do.

### F-101 [Medium / CONFIRMED] apply_pending_schema_migrations reads the applied set outside the migration lock - concurrent connect() aborts on PK conflict
- **Area / Dimension.** critic:schemas-init
- **Affected files.** `src/trading_research/storage/schema_version.py`
- **Line ranges.** 311-343
- **Observed behavior.** Line 323 computes `applied = {row[0] for row in conn.execute("SELECT version FROM schema_version")...}` BEFORE the `with transaction(conn):` block at line 333 acquires BEGIN IMMEDIATE. Two connections opening the same DB near-simultaneously both observe an empty (or older) `applied` set, both plan the same `pending` list, and race for BEGIN IMMEDIATE. The loser, now inside its own BEGIN IMMEDIATE, has stale `pending`, re-runs the migration body (safe because idempotent), then executes `INSERT INTO schema_version (version, description, applied_at)` at line 336, which is a PRIMARY KEY collision on `version`.
- **Failure scenario.** On a fresh DB, `run_dashboard.sh` and the primary scoring process both call storage.database.connect() within the busy_timeout window. Both read `applied = {}`. Process A wins BEGIN IMMEDIATE, applies migrations 1..11, commits. Process B replays migrations idempotently, then crashes on the schema_version INSERT with `UNIQUE constraint failed: schema_version.version`, propagating out of connect() and killing the second process.
- **Impact.** Non-deterministic startup crash on any deployment that legitimately opens two connections against a not-yet-initialised DB (dashboard + scheduler, or campaign runner + operator CLI). Fails closed but as an unhandled IntegrityError.
- **Recommended correction.** Re-read `applied` INSIDE the transaction after BEGIN IMMEDIATE succeeds, and skip the version if already applied; alternatively `INSERT OR IGNORE` on schema_version. Add a multi-process regression test.
- **Verification.** 1 vote - CONFIRMED: `applied` snapshot is taken in autocommit at line 323 before any BEGIN IMMEDIATE; per-migration transaction unconditionally INSERTs into schema_version (line 335-338) where `version` is the primary key.

### F-102 [Medium / PLAUSIBLE] _migration_2 backfills paper_order_execution_claims with an unbounded Python-side row-by-row scan inside one transaction
- **Area / Dimension.** critic:schemas-init
- **Affected files.** `src/trading_research/storage/schema_version.py`
- **Line ranges.** 61-93, 311-343
- **Observed behavior.** _migration_2_backfill_execution_namespace_claims (line 71) fetches every row of paper_book_orders unconditionally, then for each row does four separate `SELECT 1 FROM <external_table> ... LIMIT 1` probes (line 79) and an INSERT ... WHERE NOT EXISTS (line 87). The entire loop runs inside a single `with transaction(conn):` from apply_pending_schema_migrations (line 333). There is no batching, no server-side set query, and no way to make progress incrementally.
- **Failure scenario.** On a legacy paper-book DB with 200 000 pre-existing paper_book_orders rows, migration 2 issues ~1 000 000 sqlite queries in a single transaction on the connect() hot path. The migration holds the write lock the entire time, blocking every other connection under busy_timeout=5s. If any single row's INSERT hits an unexpected constraint, the whole transaction rolls back and the migration marker is never written, so the next connect() replays the same slow scan.
- **Impact.** Multi-minute startup stall and 'database is locked' cascades across peer processes on any non-trivial legacy DB; every restart repeats the work if the once-only transaction ever fails.
- **Recommended correction.** Rewrite as a set-based `INSERT INTO paper_order_execution_claims SELECT ... FROM paper_book_orders LEFT JOIN external tables WHERE claim row does not already exist`, or page the loop with intermediate commits. Add a size probe up front and log the row count.
- **Verification.** 1 vote - PLAUSIBLE: Line 71 does an unbounded SELECT with fetchall(); for every row, lines 75-93 issue up to four probes plus an INSERT. apply_pending_schema_migrations wraps each migration in a single BEGIN IMMEDIATE.

### F-103 [Low / PLAUSIBLE] _migration_10 misuses SchemaVersionError to signal a corrupted-DB precondition, muddying the forward-version invariant
- **Area / Dimension.** critic:schemas-init
- **Affected files.** `src/trading_research/storage/schema_version.py`
- **Line ranges.** 225-242, 37-40, 296-308
- **Observed behavior.** SchemaVersionError is documented (line 37) as raised only when the persisted schema_version is newer than CURRENT_SCHEMA_VERSION. _migration_10_advanced_risk_controls (line 242) reuses that same exception for the unrelated 'expected Milestone 13 tables are missing' precondition.
- **Failure scenario.** Operator writes a diagnostic wrapper: `try: connect(path) except SchemaVersionError: prompt 'this DB was written by newer code, refuse to open'`. A different regression (a hand-edited paper_books_schema.py that accidentally removed backtest_runs) trips migration 10's `raise SchemaVersionError(...)` at line 242, and the operator gets the misleading 'newer code' message.
- **Impact.** Diagnostic clarity, not correctness: forward-version refusal and missing-precondition errors are indistinguishable at the exception type level.
- **Recommended correction.** Introduce a distinct exception (e.g. SchemaPreconditionError) for the 'expected tables missing' case and keep SchemaVersionError reserved for the downgrade-refusal case its docstring describes.
- **Verification.** 1 vote - PLAUSIBLE: Semantic overloading confirmed by docstring vs. usage. Impact limited to diagnostic clarity - no data corruption, no silent failure.

### F-104 [Medium / CONFIRMED] activation_stage advances via YAML edit alone; no persisted evidence gates STAGE_3..STAGE_7
- **Area / Dimension.** critic:scheduler-activation
- **Affected files.** `src/trading_research/strategies/config.py`, `config/strategies.yaml`
- **Line ranges.** strategies/config.py:24-32, 176-191, 227-231; strategies.yaml:18-20
- **Observed behavior.** `ACTIVATION_STAGES` is a 7-stage progression, but `load_strategy_config` only refuses the (enabled=True, activation_stage in {STAGE_1, STAGE_2}) combination. Every advancement from STAGE_3 -> STAGE_7 is validated by a pure membership check with no consultation of `shadow_scheduler_runs`, `shadow_run_summaries`, `research_attempts`, paper-book soak state, or any persisted-evidence source. The value is not read anywhere else in `src/`.
- **Failure scenario.** An operator wanting to advance from STAGE_3_DAILY_READ_ONLY_CANDIDATE_LIST to STAGE_7_MULTI_DAY_PAPER_SOAK edits one line in config/strategies.yaml. `load_strategy_config` accepts the value; no code path verifies that any shadow tracking (Stage 4), local paper book (Stage 5), supervised Alpaca paper (Stage 6), or multi-day soak (Stage 7) has actually occurred. The activation ratchet is documentation-only.
- **Impact.** The staged rollout advertised in docs/strategy-candidate-selection.md is enforced only against Stages 1-2; the remaining five stages provide no runtime safety and cannot detect skipped-stage advancement.
- **Recommended correction.** Bind each stage-advancement transition to a persisted attestation (e.g., a stage-completion row keyed by config_hash + evidence counts) and refuse to load a strategies.yaml whose `activation_stage` exceeds the highest attested stage in storage.
- **Verification.** 1 vote - CONFIRMED: Grep across src/ confirms activation_stage is only referenced in strategies/config.py.

### F-105 [Medium / CONFIRMED] run_due_shadow_cycle enables recurring operation from YAML alone; evaluate_activation_readiness is advisory-only
- **Area / Dimension.** critic:scheduler-activation
- **Affected files.** `src/trading_research/shadow/scheduler.py`, `src/trading_research/shadow/readiness.py`, `src/trading_research/cli.py`
- **Line ranges.** scheduler.py:643-733; readiness.py:429-524; cli.py:1692-1861
- **Observed behavior.** `run_due_shadow_cycle` gates only on `shadow_config.shadow_operations.enabled` and `schedule.enabled`. `evaluate_activation_readiness` (readiness.py:429) exists and produces readiness statuses, but no scheduler code path imports it. `run_due_shadow_cycle_cli` (cli.py:1641-1861) invokes the scheduler without ever consulting activation readiness; only the separate `shadow-readiness` command surfaces it as a report.
- **Failure scenario.** An operator flips `shadow_operations.enabled: true` and `schedule.enabled: true` and points launchd at the shell script; each due window, `run_due_shadow_cycle` proceeds with lease + budget + real-provider calls even if `evaluate_activation_readiness` would have returned `NOT_READY_INSUFFICIENT_HISTORY` or `NOT_READY_HEALTH_UNEXPLAINED`.
- **Impact.** The intended manual-vs-recurring gate is documentation, not enforcement; a config edit alone can drive real-provider scheduled runs without ever satisfying the advertised minimum completed-cycle / real-provider-cycle floors.
- **Recommended correction.** Call `evaluate_activation_readiness` inside `run_due_shadow_cycle` (or in `run_due_shadow_cycle_cli` before the scheduler is entered) and short-circuit to a no-op status when the result is not `ACTIVATION_READY_FOR_LIMITED_RECURRING_SHADOW`.
- **Verification.** 1 vote - CONFIRMED: Grep confirms evaluate_activation_readiness is only referenced from the reporting `shadow-readiness` CLI and paper_books/controlled_soak_readiness.py - never from the scheduling path.

### F-106 [Medium / CONFIRMED] shadow_operations.allow_baseline_paper_submission is loaded but never enforced
- **Area / Dimension.** critic:scheduler-activation
- **Affected files.** `src/trading_research/shadow/config.py`, `src/trading_research/shadow/scheduler.py`, `src/trading_research/research/scheduled_cycle.py`
- **Line ranges.** shadow/config.py:60, 546-548; scheduler.py:551-1298; scheduled_cycle.py:571-578
- **Observed behavior.** `ShadowOperationsSection.allow_baseline_paper_submission` is parsed and validated, but grep returns matches only in `shadow/config.py` itself. `run_due_shadow_cycle` never inspects it; `run_scheduled_research_cycle` gates baseline submission on `configuration.submit_paper_orders and paper_submitter is not None`, which is sourced from `scheduled_research.yaml`, not `shadow_operations.yaml`.
- **Failure scenario.** An operator sets `shadow_operations.allow_baseline_paper_submission: true` expecting it to gate paper submission on the shadow orchestration side; nothing changes. Conversely, an operator leaving it `false` believing it prevents submission is protected only by `scheduled_research.submit_paper_orders`.
- **Impact.** A field advertised as one of the fail-closed capability decisions is inert; two operators reading shadow_operations.yaml get contradictory mental models of how baseline submission is gated.
- **Recommended correction.** Either (a) actually consult `shadow_config.shadow_operations.allow_baseline_paper_submission` when constructing the paper_submitter and refuse submission when false, or (b) delete the field and its docstring.
- **Verification.** 1 vote - CONFIRMED: No consumer exists in src/. In cli.py:1848, `_cycle_kwargs_builder` sets `paper_submitter=None` unconditionally.

### F-107 [High / CONFIRMED] Rate-limit/timeout on wrapped provider call strands reservation as AMBIGUOUS and defeats orchestrator retry
- **Area / Dimension.** critic:provider-boundary
- **Affected files.** `src/trading_research/research/token_budget.py`, `src/trading_research/strategies/research_budget.py`, `src/trading_research/research/orchestration.py`
- **Line ranges.** token_budget.py:65-77; research_budget.py:292-309; orchestration.py:444-522
- **Observed behavior.** `PersistentResearchTokenBudgetController.invoke` uses `except BaseException` to mark the reservation AMBIGUOUS on anything other than `ProviderUnavailableError` - including `ProviderRateLimitError` and `ProviderTimeoutError`, both of which the orchestrator's `_RETRYABLE_ERRORS` tuple explicitly intends to retry. Because `reserve_research_tokens` refuses to hand out a new reservation while any AMBIGUOUS row exists with the same prefix on the same UTC date, attempt N+1 comes back as `ProviderUnavailableError(retryable=False)`.
- **Failure scenario.** Anthropic returns HTTP 429 on the first analyst attempt. anthropic_provider raises `ProviderRateLimitError` (retryable=True). Controller catches it via `except BaseException`, marks the reservation AMBIGUOUS, re-raises. Orchestrator enters the retry branch; on attempt 2 it calls invoke again, which calls reserve_research_tokens - the AMBIGUOUS row triggers `TOKEN_RESERVATION_RECONCILIATION_REQUIRED`. Controller converts to `ProviderUnavailableError(retryable=False)`, orchestrator breaks the retry loop and terminates with `NON_RETRYABLE_FAILURE`. A single 429 kills the run.
- **Impact.** Every rate limit or connection error escalates to an operator ticket, and outer retry logic for rate limits is unreachable behind the budget cap.
- **Recommended correction.** Narrow the ambiguous-branch: catch `(ProviderRateLimitError, ProviderTransientError with stage=PROVIDER_REQUEST)` and release; keep `BaseException` -> AMBIGUOUS only for timeouts after transmission and unclassified failures.
- **Verification.** 1 vote - CONFIRMED: ProviderRateLimitError/ProviderTransientError/ProviderTimeoutError are RuntimeError subclasses caught by bare `except BaseException`. On retry, reserve_research_tokens rejects the new reservation.

### F-108 [Low / PLAUSIBLE] Anthropic SDK client left at default max_retries=2 - internal wire retries consume real tokens outside any reservation accounting
- **Area / Dimension.** critic:provider-boundary
- **Affected files.** `src/trading_research/research/anthropic_provider.py`
- **Line ranges.** 96-101, 125-166
- **Observed behavior.** `anthropic.Anthropic(api_key=config.api_key, timeout=config.request_timeout_seconds)` is constructed without `max_retries=0`. The Anthropic SDK's default is to retry on 429/5xx/connection errors up to 2 additional times. When those internal retries eventually succeed, `response.usage` reports only the final call's tokens; failed intermediate calls can be billed for input tokens.
- **Failure scenario.** Provider hits a transient 5xx twice, third attempt succeeds. The controller reserved worst-case ceiling once; usage reported once; actual wire tokens billed = up to 3x input. The classifier logic in this file will never see the retryable errors - the SDK swallows them.
- **Impact.** Token reservations under-count actual wire usage under transient conditions; orchestrator retry semantics are duplicated (SDK retries plus outer retries) and could exceed the daily cap despite reservation math.
- **Recommended correction.** Pass `max_retries=0` when constructing the SDK client so all retry policy lives in the orchestrator + reservation layer.
- **Verification.** 1 vote - PLAUSIBLE: Confirmed at anthropic_provider.py:100 - constructor omits max_retries override; SDK default retries hide retryable exceptions from the outer layer.

### F-109 [Low / CONFIRMED] openai_compatible_provider records retry_count off-by-one relative to every other provider
- **Area / Dimension.** critic:provider-boundary
- **Affected files.** `src/trading_research/research/openai_compatible_provider.py`, `src/trading_research/research/anthropic_provider.py`, `src/trading_research/research/claude_code_provider.py`, `src/trading_research/research/codex_provider.py`, `src/trading_research/research/deterministic_provider.py`
- **Line ranges.** openai_compatible_provider.py:205; anthropic_provider.py:228; claude_code_provider.py:493; codex_provider.py:577; deterministic_provider.py:107,182
- **Observed behavior.** `build_usage_record(..., retry_count=request.attempt_number)` in openai_compatible_provider.py L205; every other provider passes `retry_count=request.attempt_number - 1` (attempt_number is 1-based per orchestration.py L376). So the OpenAI-compatible provider reports retry_count one greater than every other provider for the same attempt.
- **Failure scenario.** First-attempt success on Anthropic records retry_count=0; first-attempt success on OpenAI-compatible (DeepSeek/Together/vLLM) records retry_count=1. Any downstream dashboard, retry-rate SLO, or cost attribution rule that filters `retry_count == 0` for first attempts silently misclassifies every OpenAI-compatible successful call as a retry.
- **Impact.** Distorted retry metrics and cost attribution for any deployment that uses an OpenAI-compatible provider; failure-taxonomy analytics are provider-inconsistent.
- **Recommended correction.** Change `retry_count=request.attempt_number` to `retry_count=request.attempt_number - 1` to match the other five providers.
- **Verification.** 1 vote - CONFIRMED: All other providers and `_unavailable_usage` in orchestration.py:686 use `attempt_number - 1`.

### F-110 [Medium / CONFIRMED] Legit LIST_RECENT_ORDERS response can exceed 64 KiB client cap and desync transport
- **Area / Dimension.** critic:runtime-ipc
- **Affected files.** `paper_runtime/src/trading_paper_runtime/dispatcher.py`, `src/trading_research/runtime/client/protocol.py`
- **Line ranges.** dispatcher.py:209-221; protocol.py:21,48-49
- **Observed behavior.** Dispatcher accepts limit up to 200 for LIST_RECENT_ORDERS and emits an unchecked response (`ResponseEnvelope.to_json_line` has no size gate). The client enforces `MAX_RESPONSE_BYTES = 65_536` and raises `ProtocolViolationError` on oversize responses; `process_client.py:310-316` catches that and calls `_mark_unhealthy_after_timeout`, tearing down the child. Each order JSON is ~350-500 bytes, so ~150-200 recent orders will overflow the 65 KiB cap.
- **Failure scenario.** Caller invokes `list_recent_external_orders(book_id, limit=200)` (a supported ceiling). Runtime dispatches, produces a valid but ~80-100 KiB JSON Lines response. Client's `parse_response_line` rejects it as oversize, marks the transport unhealthy, kills the child, and restarts. The exact same request will fail again indefinitely.
- **Impact.** Silent, permanent breakage of the read-only duplicate-detection path (Milestone 11 Part 9). Also a general size-cap asymmetry: emitter has no cap, receiver does.
- **Recommended correction.** Enforce the same size cap on the emitter (chunk or refuse), or lower dispatcher's limit ceiling to match `MAX_RESPONSE_BYTES` with margin. Add a regression test at limit=200.
- **Verification.** 1 vote - CONFIRMED: Dispatcher permits limit up to 200; ResponseEnvelope.to_json_line has no size gate; client's parse_response_line enforces 65,536-byte cap.

### F-111 [Medium / CONFIRMED] Third-party stderr not redaction-filtered; alpaca-py/LumiBot/urllib3 debug logs can leak API keys
- **Area / Dimension.** critic:runtime-ipc
- **Affected files.** `paper_runtime/src/trading_paper_runtime/logging_config.py`, `paper_runtime/src/trading_paper_runtime/main.py`
- **Line ranges.** logging_config.py:18-38; main.py:39
- **Observed behavior.** `_RedactingFilter` is attached only to the `trading_paper_runtime` named logger and `logger.propagate = False`. Third-party loggers (`alpaca`, `urllib3`, `requests`, `lumibot`) do NOT go through this filter. `main.py:39` reassigns `sys.stdout = sys.stderr`, routing third-party `print()` to the parent's captured stderr pipe untouched. Filter also only redacts three specific env-var values.
- **Failure scenario.** A user enables debug logging via `LOG_LEVEL=DEBUG` or urllib3 debug or a broker error path that logs the outbound request with headers. The alpaca-py client logs an `Authorization: Bearer <key>` line to its own logger; it is captured by the subprocess's stderr pipe, stored unbounded in `SubprocessTransport._stderr_lines`, and can be flushed to disk by any operator diagnostic tool.
- **Impact.** Credential exfiltration through operational diagnostics / log capture. Violates the module's explicit "Credential values are redacted from every log message" contract.
- **Recommended correction.** Install the redacting filter on the root logger (or configure logging via `dictConfig` with the filter at the handler level so all libraries flow through it). Load secrets once at startup into a set. Consider a header-pattern regex catch-all.
- **Verification.** 1 vote - CONFIRMED: Filter installed only on named logger; propagate=False; third-party records reach the root logger's handlers (or default lastResort stderr writer) without redaction.

### F-112 [Low / PLAUSIBLE] SubprocessTransport buffers stderr lines without bound; chatty runtime can OOM the parent
- **Area / Dimension.** critic:runtime-ipc
- **Affected files.** `src/trading_research/runtime/client/process_client.py`
- **Line ranges.** process_client.py:76-77, 90-93, 243-252
- **Observed behavior.** `_pump_stderr` appends every stderr line to `self._stderr_lines` under a lock, unbounded. `diagnostics()` drains and discards, but it's only called on error paths. During normal steady-state operation, stderr accumulates forever.
- **Failure scenario.** A LumiBot debug configuration (or a broker retry loop) writes multiple stderr lines per request. Over an intraday window with thousands of `list_open_orders` polls, `_stderr_lines` grows to hundreds of MB inside the long-lived parent process.
- **Impact.** Slow-motion memory pressure in the parent (which also runs the scheduler and DB session). Could OOM before EOD reconciliation.
- **Recommended correction.** Cap `_stderr_lines` at N with a bounded `collections.deque(maxlen=N)`, or periodically drain from a background thread with a counter of dropped-lines.
- **Verification.** 1 vote - PLAUSIBLE: Unbounded list append confirmed; drain only via `diagnostics()` on non-happy paths.

### F-113 [Medium / CONFIRMED] `write_line` doesn't catch BrokenPipeError; mid-flight child death raises through the caller
- **Area / Dimension.** critic:runtime-ipc
- **Affected files.** `src/trading_research/runtime/client/process_client.py`
- **Line ranges.** process_client.py:95-97, 276-297
- **Observed behavior.** `_request` checks `is_alive()` then calls `self._transport.write_line(line)`. Between those two lines the child can die; `SubprocessTransport.write_line` performs raw `stdin.write`+`flush` with no exception handling. On a broken pipe, `BrokenPipeError` propagates unchanged - not the documented `RuntimeUnavailableError` contract.
- **Failure scenario.** Runtime process crashes/is killed by OOM killer immediately after `is_alive()` returned True. Caller performs `submit_order`; `write_line` raises `BrokenPipeError`. Upstream code (paper_books submit path) expects `RuntimeUnavailableError` or `RuntimeRequestTimeoutError`; the credentialed submission path at services/submit_credentialed_paper_order.py:132-133 only catches those two, so BrokenPipeError escapes past the ambiguous-outcome recovery lookup and SUBMISSION_UNKNOWN bookkeeping.
- **Impact.** Ambiguous-outcome handling contract is violated; the exception type is not one the caller is prepared for.
- **Recommended correction.** Wrap `write_line` inside `_request` in `try/except (BrokenPipeError, OSError)` and translate to `RuntimeUnavailableError` with diagnostics, then invoke `_mark_unhealthy_after_timeout`.
- **Verification.** 1 vote - CONFIRMED: write_line has no exception handling; BrokenPipeError escapes callers that only handle RuntimeUnavailableError/RuntimeRequestTimeoutError/RuntimeOperationError.
